We shall (in a series of steps, some of which are performed in
this preprocessing step, and some of which are performed on later parts of
the proof) construct subsets
X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
,
X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
,
X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
and

X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
of
X_{i}
.
We define

X^{\mathrm{main}}=\bigcup_{i\in[r]}X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\,,\quad X^{\mathrm{q}}=\bigcup_{i\in[r]}X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}\,,\quad X^{\mathrm{c}}=\bigcup_{i\in[r]}X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}\,,\quad X^{\mathrm{buf}}=\bigcup_{i\in[r]}X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\,.

Here,
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\subseteq\tilde{X}_{i}
contains the
buffer vertices
, which will
be chosen from the set of potential buffer vertices (see Section
2.3.4
).
The set
X^{\mathrm{c}}
is only used in the
proof of the random graphs blow-up lemma, Lemma
1.21
; in the
other proofs we set
X^{\mathrm{c}}=\emptyset
. The vertices in
X^{\mathrm{c}}
shall form the

reserved cliques
which we will use to fix so-called
buffer
defects
(see Section
3.4
for more details). We choose this set of vertices at the beginning of the proof of Lemma
1.21
. Once we
chose
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
and
X^{\mathrm{c}}
, we set

X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}:=X_{i}\setminus(X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\cup X^{\mathrm{c}})
. These sets will have sizes

|X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}|=4\mu|X_{i}|
and
|X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}|\leq\mu|X_{i}|
and hence

(1-5\mu)|X_{i}|\leq|X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|\leq(1-4\mu)|X_{i}|
. The set
X^{\mathrm{q}}\subseteq X^{\mathrm{main}}

will form the
queue
and only gets chosen during the embedding, in
the random greedy algorithm; it will also be of size at most
\mu|X_{i}|
.
We remark that in the following definition we shall only refer to the
sets
X_{i}
and
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. We only chose to mention the sets
X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
and
X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}

here as well because we need them to motivate the refinement of the
partition of
G
in the next subsection.

The properties that we require of our refined partition and the buffer
vertices are collected in the following
definition. We remark that the reduced graphs
R
and
R^{\prime}
used for this
refined partition are blow-ups of the reduced graphs
R_{\mathrm{BL}}
and
R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{BL}}
provided to the
respective blow-up lemma. These properties bound the number of buffer vertices per part, and state that the first and second
neighbourhoods of buffer vertices lie along edges of
R^{\prime}
.
They also assert that any pair of vertices
within one part
X_{i}
, but also any pair of buffer vertices, and any pair
consisting of a buffer vertex and an image restricted vertex, are far apart. Note that this implies that
H[X_{i},X_{j}]
forms a matching for any
pair of parts
X_{i}
and
X_{j}
, and it also implies that buffer vertices may
not be image restricted. We further require that all vertices in
any one
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
have the same degree, and distinguish between clique buffers
and non-clique buffers. The latter will be important for the selection of
reserved cliques in the proof of Lemma
1.21
(Section
3.4
).

Definition 2.20

(Good
H
-partition)
.

We say that a partition

{\mathcal{X}}=\{X_{i}\}_{i\in[r]}
of
V(H)
is a
good
H
-partition
for
reduced graphs
R^{\prime}\subseteq R
on vertex sets
[r]
,
with buffer
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}=\{\tilde{X}_{i}\}_{i\in[r]}
, buffer vertices

X^{\mathrm{buf}}\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in[r]}\tilde{X}_{i}
and image restricting vertices

\mathcal{J}=\{J_{x}\}_{x\in V(H)}
,
if
the following
conditions are satisfied for each
i,j,k\in[r]
.

(
H
0)

(H,{\mathcal{X}})
is an
R
-partition and
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}
is an

(\alpha,R^{\prime})
-buffer for
(H,{\mathcal{X}})
.

(
H
0)

\mathrm{dist}_{H}(x,y)\geq 10
and
J_{x}\cap J_{y}=\emptyset
for each
x,y\in X_{i}
with
x\neq y
.

(
H
0)

|X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}|=4\mu|X_{i}|
,
and
|\{x\in X_{i}\colon x\in N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})\}|\leq 4\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}\mu|X_{i}|
.

(
H
0)

\mathrm{dist}_{H}(x,y)\geq 5
for each
x,y\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}
with

x\neq y
.

(
H
0)

\mathrm{dist}_{H}(x,y)\geq 3
for each
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}
and each image restricted
y
.

(
H
0)

All vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
have degree
b
for some
0\leq b\leq\Delta
.
We then call
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
a
degree-
b
buffer
.

(
H
0)

Either all or none of the vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
are in copies of

K_{\Delta+1}
. We then call
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
a
clique buffer
or
non-clique
buffer
, respectively.

(
H
0)

If
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a clique buffer, then at
least
\tfrac{1}{2\Delta+4}\alpha|X_{i}|
vertices of
\tilde{X}_{i}
are in copies
of
K_{\Delta+1}
which do not contain either vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
or
image restricted vertices.

Note that in this definition we do need to know about image restricting vertices (since they play a rôle in
(H 0)
), which are in
\Gamma
, and we do need to know which vertices of
H
are image restricted (since they play a rôle in
(H 0)
), but we do not need to know anything about
G
.

2.3.3.
The partition of
G

We next define the properties we require from our refined partition
of
G
. Firstly, we shall subdivide each cluster of the partition
\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{BL}}
of
G
, which is given as input to one of our blow-up lemmas, into several new
clusters to obtain a new partition
{\mathcal{V}}
matching the refined partition of
H
. Further, as also mentioned
in the proof overview, we shall partition each new cluster
V_{i}
into parts

V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
,
V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
,
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
and
V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
.
Again, we set

V^{\mathrm{main}}=\bigcup_{i\in[r]}V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\,,\quad V^{\mathrm{q}}=\bigcup_{i\in[r]}V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}\,,\quad V^{\mathrm{c}}=\bigcup_{i\in[r]}V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}\,,\quad V^{\mathrm{buf}}=\bigcup_{i\in[r]}V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\,.

As previously indicated, each vertex
x\in V(H)
will be embedded into the cluster
V_{i}

such that
x\in X_{i}
. In the rest of the paper, we often refer to
V_{i}
simply by

V(x)
, to
V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
by
V^{\mathrm{main}}(x)
, and so on, to avoid the use of
indices whenever these are not important.

As explained before, most of the vertices from
V^{\mathrm{main}}
will be used to
embed most vertices of
X^{\mathrm{main}}
, while some vertices from
V^{\mathrm{q}}
will be
used to embed the remainder, which constitutes the queue
X^{\mathrm{q}}
. We also have the set
V^{\mathrm{c}}
, the
clique
reservoir
, that we will use in the proof of the random graphs blow-up
lemma to embed some of the vertices in
X^{\mathrm{c}}
. Some vertices of each
X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
may need
to be used to fix so-called buffer defects, and these could be embedded
anywhere in
V_{i}
. All other vertices of each
X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
get embedded to
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
.
Finally, the vertices from

V^{\mathrm{buf}}
together with all remaining vertices of
V^{\mathrm{main}}\cup V^{\mathrm{q}}\cup V^{\mathrm{c}}
,
that is, vertices that were not
used when embedding
X^{\mathrm{main}}\cup X^{\mathrm{q}}\cup X^{\mathrm{c}}
,
will be used for embedding the buffer vertices
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
.

The properties we require of a good
G
-partition are as follows. It must be a regular
R
-partition, and furthermore the one-sided and two-sided inheritance properties of
\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{BL}}
on
R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{BL}}
must be transferred to
{\mathcal{V}}
on
R^{\prime}
. The super-regularity properties of
\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{BL}}
on
R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{BL}}
must be transferred to give minimum degree conditions for each of the parts
V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
,
V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
and
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
. Finally, we need to create new image restriction sets
\mathcal{I}
from the supplied
\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{BL}}
which with the (unchanged)
\mathcal{J}
form a restriction pair.

Definition 2.21

(Good
G
-partition)
.

Given a good
H
-partition of
V(H)
for reduced graphs
R^{\prime}\subseteq R
on
vertex sets
[r]
, with buffer
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}=\{\tilde{X}_{i}\}_{i\in[r]}
and buffer vertices

X^{\mathrm{buf}}\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in[r]}\tilde{X}_{i}
, with
image restrictions
\mathcal{I}=\{I_{x}\}_{x\in V(H)}
, and restricting vertices

\mathcal{J}=\{J_{x}\}_{x\in V(H)}
, we say that a partition

{\mathcal{V}}=\{V_{i}\}_{i\in[r]}
of
V(G)
with a partition

V_{i}=V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
of each cluster
is a
good
G
-partition
for

R^{\prime}\subseteq R
, if
the following conditions are satisfied.

(
G
0)

For each
i
we have
|V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|=(1-3\mu)|V_{i}|
and
|V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}|=|V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}|=|V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}|=\mu|V_{i}|
.

(
G
0)

(G,{\mathcal{V}})
is an
(\varepsilon,d,p)
-regular
R
-partition,
which
has one-sided inheritance on
R^{\prime}
,
and two-sided inheritance on
R^{\prime}
for
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}
.

(
G
0)

For each
ij\in R^{\prime}
and
v\in V_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle\deg_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}_{j})

\displaystyle\geq(1-3\mu)(d-\varepsilon)\max\big(p|V_{j}|,\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j})/2\big)\,,

\displaystyle\deg_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{q}}_{j})

\displaystyle\geq\mu(d-\varepsilon)\max\big(p|V_{j}|,\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j})/2\big)\,,

\displaystyle\deg_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j})

\displaystyle\geq\mu(d-\varepsilon)\max\big(p|V_{j}|,\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j})/2\big)\,.

(
G
0)

For each
x\in X_{i}
, we have

\displaystyle|I_{x}\cap V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|

\displaystyle\geq(1-\varepsilon)(1-3\mu)|I_{x}|\,,

\displaystyle|I_{x}\cap V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}|\,,\,|I_{x}\cap V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}|\,,\,|I_{x}\cap V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}|

\displaystyle\geq(1-\varepsilon)\mu|I_{x}|\,,

\displaystyle\big|N^{*}(J_{x};V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i})\big|

\displaystyle=\big(1\pm\varepsilon\big)(1-3\mu)p^{|J_{x}|}|V_{i}|\,,\text{ and}

\displaystyle\big|N^{*}(J_{x};V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i})\big|\,,\,\big|N^{*}(J_{x};V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i})\big|\,,\,\big|N^{*}(J_{x};V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i})\big|

\displaystyle=\big(1\pm\varepsilon\big)\mu p^{|J_{x}|}|V_{i}|\,.

(
G
0)

\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{J}
form a
(\varrho p^{\vartheta},\zeta,\Delta,\Delta_{J})
-restriction pair
for the partitions
{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{V}}
.

(
G
0)

For each
i
, the number of image restricted vertices in
X_{i}
is
at most
\varrho p^{\vartheta}|X_{i}|
.

Note that the sizes of the sets
V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
,
V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
,
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
,
V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}

are
not
the same as the sizes of the sets
X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
,
X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
,

X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
and
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. For example we will have

|X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|=(1-4\mu)|X_{i}|<(1-3\mu)|V_{i}|=|V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|
, and

|X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}|,|X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}|\leq 2\varrho|X_{i}|
, which is much smaller than
\mu|V_{i}|
. Thus, the subsets
X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
,
X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
,
X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
of
X_{i}
will be
significantly smaller than the corresponding sets in
V_{i}
, while the set

X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is larger than
V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
.
We also note that we do require two-sided inheritance for
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}
in
(G 0)
, not just for
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
. In the proof of Lemma
1.21
, when dealing with buffer defects, we will make use of this stronger statement.

2.3.4.
Obtaining a good
H
-partition and a good
G
-partition

The following lemma proves that we can obtain good partitions of
H

and
G
from the partitions provided to our blow-up lemmas. The proof of
this lemma is straightforward, though not short since several conditions
must be checked. Briefly, the idea is that we will draw an auxiliary graph

F_{i}
on each part
X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}
of
\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{BL}}
with edges joining pairs of vertices
which are at distance less than
10
or share an image restricting
vertex. We will apply our modification of the Hajnal-Szemerédi theorem
(Lemma
2.4
) to each of these graphs together with the
set
\tilde{X}_{i}
to obtain a partition
{\mathcal{X}}
and reduced graphs
R
and
R^{\prime}

satisfying the first three conditions of a good
H
-partition, and then
construct the sets
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
greedily to obtain the remaining
properties. We will then randomly refine the partition
\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{BL}}
to obtain a
matching partition
{\mathcal{V}}
, and further randomly split each part
V_{i}
of

{\mathcal{V}}
into
V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
,
V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
,
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
and
V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. We will use the
concentration inequalities in Theorem
2.1
to obtain
concentration for various set sizes in the randomly chosen parts, which in
particular show that with high probability we obtain the desired good

G
-partition. We stress that although
p
with a lower bound reminiscent
of our random graph blow-up lemmas makes an appearance in the lemma
statement, this does not mean we are going to assume
\Gamma
is a random
graph. We will simply need to know that the quantity
p^{b}n
is large
compared to
\log n
.

Lemma 2.22

(Good partitions lemma)
.

For all positive integers
\Delta
,
\Delta_{R^{\prime}}
,
\Delta_{J}
,
r_{1}
,
\vartheta
and

b
, all
\kappa>2
, all
\alpha,\zeta,d,\varepsilon>0
, and all sufficiently small
\mu,\varrho>0
, there exists

C
such that the following holds whenever
p\geq C\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/b}
and
n>C
. Let

\delta=\tfrac{1}{8}(\Delta+\Delta_{J})^{-10}
and
r\leq r_{1}
.

Let
R_{\mathrm{BL}}
be a graph on
r_{\mathrm{BL}}=\delta r
vertices and let
R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{BL}}\subseteq R_{\mathrm{BL}}
be a spanning
subgraph with
\Delta(R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{BL}})\leq\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{BL}}=\delta\Delta_{R^{\prime}}
.
Let
H
and
G\subseteq\Gamma
be graphs with
\tfrac{1}{2}\kappa
-balanced
size-compatible vertex partitions

\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{BL}}=\{X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}\}_{i\in[r_{\mathrm{BL}}]}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{BL}}=\{V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}\}_{i\in[r_{\mathrm{BL}}]}
,
respectively, which have parts of size at least
2n/(\kappa\delta r_{1})
.
Let
\mathcal{\tilde{X}}^{\mathrm{BL}}=\{\tilde{X}^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}\}_{i\in[r_{\mathrm{BL}}]}
be a family of subsets of
V(H)
,

\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{BL}}=\{I^{\mathrm{BL}}_{x}\}_{x\in V(H)}
be a family of image restrictions, and

\mathcal{J}=\{J_{x}\}_{x\in V(H)}
be a family of restricting vertices.
Suppose that

(

)

\Delta(H)\leq\Delta
,
(H,\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{BL}})
is an
R_{\mathrm{BL}}
-partition,
and
\mathcal{\tilde{X}}^{\mathrm{BL}}=\{\tilde{X}^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}\}_{i\in[r_{\mathrm{BL}}]}
is a

(2\alpha,R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{BL}})
-buffer for
(H,{\mathcal{X}})
,

(

)

(G,\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{BL}})
is a
\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\delta\varepsilon,d,p\big)
-regular
R_{\mathrm{BL}}
-partition, which is

\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\delta\varepsilon,d,p\big)
-super-regular on
R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{BL}}
,
has one-sided inheritance on
R^{\prime}
, and two-sided inheritance on
R^{\prime}
for
\mathcal{\tilde{X}}^{\mathrm{BL}}
,

(

)

\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{BL}}
and
\mathcal{J}
form
a
\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\delta\varrho p^{\vartheta},2\zeta,\Delta,\Delta_{J}\big)
-restriction pair ,
and
|J_{x}|\leq b
for each
x\in V(H)
.

Then there is a graph
R
on
r
vertices and a spanning subgraph

R^{\prime}\subseteq R
with
\Delta(R^{\prime})\leq\Delta_{R^{\prime}}
, together with

\kappa
-balanced size-compatible partitions
{\mathcal{X}}=\{X_{i}\}_{i\in[r]}

of
H
and
{\mathcal{V}}=\{V_{i}\}_{i\in[r]}
of
G
, which have parts of size at
least
n/(\kappa r_{1})
, a family
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}=\{\tilde{X}_{i}\}_{i\in[r]}
of potential
buffer vertices, a family
\mathcal{I}=\{I_{x}\}_{x\in V(H)}
of image restrictions,
subsets
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\subseteq\tilde{X}_{i}
for each
i\in[r]
, and a partition

V_{i}=V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
for each
i\in[r]
,
which give a good
H
-partition and a corresponding good
G
-partition.

Proof.

Let
\delta=\tfrac{1}{8}(\Delta+\Delta_{J})^{-10}
.
We set
C=10^{6}\delta^{-1}\mu^{-1}\varepsilon^{-2}d^{-\Delta}\zeta^{-1}\kappa r_{1}
.
We require

\mu<\frac{\alpha}{10000\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{4}\Delta^{10}(\Delta+2)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\varrho\leq\mu\,.

First we refine the vertex partition
\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{BL}}
of
H
to obtain properties
(H 0)
and
(H 0)
.
For each
i\in[r_{\mathrm{BL}}]
we define a graph
F_{i}
on the vertex set
X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}
by putting an edge between
x
and
x^{\prime}
whenever either
J_{x}\cap J_{x^{\prime}}\neq\emptyset
or
x
and
x^{\prime}
are at distance less than
10
in
H
. Observe that
\Delta(F_{i})\leq(\Delta+\Delta_{J})^{10}
. We now apply Lemma
2.4
to
F_{i}
with the set
\tilde{X}^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}
. This returns an equitable partition of the vertices
X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}
into independent sets in
F_{i}
which also partitions
\tilde{X}^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}
equitably. We do this for each
i\in[r_{\mathrm{BL}}]
to obtain a partition
{\mathcal{X}}
of
V(H)
into
8(\Delta+\Delta_{J})^{10}r_{\mathrm{BL}}=\delta^{-1}r_{\mathrm{BL}}=r
parts and a family
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}=\{\tilde{X}_{i}\}_{i\in[r]}
. We let
R
be the graph obtained from
R_{\mathrm{BL}}
by replacing each vertex with an independent set on
8(\Delta+\Delta_{J})^{10}=\delta^{-1}
vertices, and each edge with a complete bipartite graph between the corresponding sets. We obtain
R^{\prime}
similarly from
R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{BL}}
. Thus
(H,{\mathcal{X}})
is an
R
-partition, satisfying
(H 0)
by construction. Furthermore, we have
\Delta(R^{\prime})=\delta^{-1}\Delta(R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{BL}})\leq\Delta_{R^{\prime}}
.

Since each
X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}
with
i\in[r_{\mathrm{BL}}]
has size at least
2n/(\kappa\delta r_{1})
and is equipartitioned into
\delta^{-1}
parts, we see that, because
n
is chosen sufficiently large, we have
|X_{i}|\geq n/(\kappa r_{1})
for each
i\in[r]
. Given
i,j\in[r]
let
i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\in[r_{\mathrm{BL}}]
be such that
X_{i}\subseteq X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}
and
X_{j}\subseteq X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{j^{\prime}}
. Then we have

|X_{i}|\leq\delta|X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}|+1\leq\tfrac{\kappa}{2}\delta|X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{j^{\prime}}|+1\leq\tfrac{\kappa}{2}(|X_{j}|+1)+1\leq\kappa|X_{j}|

where the final inequality is since
|X_{j}|\geq n/(\kappa r_{1})
and
n
is sufficiently large. Thus
{\mathcal{X}}
is
\kappa
-balanced.

Given
i\in[r]
let
i^{\prime}\in[r_{\mathrm{BL}}]
be such that
X_{i}\subseteq X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}
. Then we have

|\tilde{X}_{i}|\geq\delta|\tilde{X}^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}|-1\geq 2\alpha\delta|X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}|-1\geq 2\alpha(|X_{i}|-1)-1\geq\alpha|X_{i}|

where again the final inequality is since
n
is sufficiently large. Thus
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}
forms an
(\alpha,R^{\prime})
-buffer for
(H,{\mathcal{X}})
.

Before we verify the remaining good
H
-partition properties, it is convenient to check
(G 0)
. By assumption
( )
‣
2.22
of the lemma, at most
\tfrac{1}{2}\delta\varrho p^{\vartheta}|X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}|
vertices of any given
X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}
are image restricted. Thus for any
i
such that
X_{i}\subseteq X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}
, at most

\tfrac{1}{2}\delta\varrho p^{\vartheta}|X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\varrho p^{\vartheta}\big(|X_{i}|+1\big)\leq\varrho p^{\vartheta}|X_{i}|

vertices of
X_{i}
are image restricted, as required for
(G 0)
.

We proceed with properties
(H 0)
–
(H 0)
by choosing buffer vertices
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
from the potential buffer vertices
\tilde{X}_{i}
for each
i\in[r]
sequentially. For a given
i\in[r]
, we first decide on the degree of the buffer and whether or not it is a clique buffer. We split the vertices of
\tilde{X}_{i}
into
\Delta+2
subsets, one for each of the possible degrees in
\{0,\dots,\Delta-1\}
, one for vertices of degree
\Delta
not in copies of
K_{\Delta+1}
, and one for vertices in copies of
K_{\Delta+1}
. We take a largest one
S_{i}
of these sets, and will choose
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
within it. We do this greedily, one vertex at a time, always picking a vertex at distance at least five from previously chosen buffer vertices, and at distance at least three from any image restricted vertices. We now justify that it is possible to do this, that is, that we can pick the desired
4\mu|X_{i}|
vertices in
S_{i}
without running out of vertices in
S_{i}
.

Since all neighbours (respectively, second neighbours) in
H
of vertices in
\tilde{X}_{i}
are in clusters
V_{j}
with
ij\in R^{\prime}
(respectively,
V_{k}
with
ij,jk\in R^{\prime}
), by
(G 0)
, verified above, it follows that the number of image restricted vertices at distance
2
or less to a vertex of
\tilde{X}_{i}
is at most
(\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{2}+\Delta_{R^{\prime}}+1)\varrho\kappa|X_{i}|
. Each of these vertices has at most one neighbour or second neighbour in
\tilde{X}^{\prime}_{i}
, since the distance between any two vertices of
X_{i}
is at least ten. Similarly, if
x
is in some
\tilde{X}_{k}
and is at distance four or less from some vertex of
\tilde{X}_{i}
, then the path between
x
and
\tilde{X}_{i}
follows edges of
R^{\prime}
, and there is only one vertex of
\tilde{X}_{i}
which is at distance four or less from
x
. Thus the total number of vertices of
\tilde{X}_{i}
which are at distance four or less from some vertex of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
is at most

(1+\Delta_{R^{\prime}}+\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{2}+\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{3}+\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{4})\cdot 4\mu\kappa|X_{i}|\,.

Since
|S_{i}|\geq\tfrac{1}{\Delta+2}|\tilde{X}_{i}|
, it follows that at each step the number of vertices in
S_{i}\subseteq\tilde{X}_{i}
we have to choose from is at least

\tfrac{1}{\Delta+2}\alpha|X_{i}|-3\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{2}\varrho\kappa|X_{i}|-20\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{4}\mu\kappa|X_{i}|\,,

which is greater than
\tfrac{1}{2\Delta+4}\alpha|X_{i}|
by choice of
\mu
and
\varrho
. Thus we can always choose the desired vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, giving
(H 0)
–
(H 0)
. In the event that
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a clique buffer, we have at the end at least
\tfrac{1}{2\Delta+4}\alpha|X_{i}|
vertices of
\tilde{X}_{i}
remaining which are in copies of
K_{\Delta+1}
(by definition of
S_{i}
) that do not contain either vertices in
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
or image restricted vertices, giving
(H 0)
.

Finally, neighbours of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
in
X_{i}
must be in parts
X_{j}
such that
ij\in R^{\prime}
. Since any two vertices in
X_{i}
are at distance at least ten, each such vertex has at most one neighbour in
X_{i}
and so at most
\Delta_{R^{\prime}}4\mu\kappa|X_{i}|
vertices of
X_{i}
are in
N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
, completing
(H 0)
. This establishes that we have a good
H
-partition.

We now construct a corresponding good
G
-partition. The construction is simple: for each
V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}\in\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{BL}}
we choose, uniformly at random, an equitable partition into
\delta^{-1}
parts, which parts we associate (arbitrarily) to subparts of
X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}
of corresponding size, obtaining
{\mathcal{V}}
. Note that this is possible since
|X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}|=|V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}|
. We then, for each
i\in[r]
, choose uniformly at random a partition of
V_{i}
into one set of size
(1-3\mu)|V_{i}|
and three of size
\mu|V_{i}|
, which form
V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
,
V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
,
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
and
V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
respectively. For each
x\in V_{i}
, we let
I_{x}=I^{\mathrm{BL}}_{x}\cap V_{i}
.

By construction
{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{V}}
are size-compatible, so what remains is to show that
{\mathcal{V}}
is with positive probability a good
G
-partition. In fact, we will show this holds asymptotically almost surely. Recall that we have already established
(G 0)
, and by construction
(G 0)
holds, so that the remaining conditions are
(G 0)
–
(G 0)
.

Observe that the sets
V_{i}
,
V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
,
V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
,
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
and
V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
are all distributed as the uniform random set of that size within
V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}
, where
V_{i}\subseteq V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}
, though they are of course not independent. It follows that for any set
U\subseteq V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}
, the sizes of each of the sets
V_{i}\cap U
,
V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap U
,
V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}\cap U
,
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}\cap U
and
V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\cap U
are hypergeometrically distributed, each with (since
n
is sufficiently large) expectation at least
\tfrac{1}{2}\mu\delta|U|
. In particular, if
|U|\geq 10^{3}\varepsilon^{-2}\log n
, then the probability that any given one of these sizes is not within a
\big(1\pm\tfrac{\varepsilon}{10}\big)
-factor of its expectation is by Theorem
2.1
at most

2e^{-\varepsilon^{2}|U|/300}\leq n^{-2}\,.

We now give various sets
U
to which we will apply this observation, and verify that each is sufficiently large.
For each
ij\in R^{\prime}
and each
v\in V_{j}
, by construction of
R^{\prime}
and since
(G,\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{BL}})
is super-regular on
R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{BL}}
, we have

\displaystyle\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\geq\deg_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\geq(d-\varepsilon)\max\big\{p|V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}|,\tfrac{1}{2}\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}})\big\}\geq 10^{3}\varepsilon^{-2}\log n\,,

where the final inequality holds by choice of
p
and
C
. We can thus take
U
to be
N_{\Gamma}(v;V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}})
or
N_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}})
.

For each
x\in X_{i}
, where
X_{i}\subseteq X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}
, since
\big(\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{BL}},\mathcal{J}\big)
form a
\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\delta\varrho,2\zeta,\Delta,\Delta_{J}\big)
-restriction pair, we have

\big|N^{*}_{\Gamma}(J_{x};V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}})\big|\geq|I^{\mathrm{BL}}_{x}|\geq\zeta(dp)^{|J_{x}|}|V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i}|\geq 10^{3}\varepsilon^{-2}\log n\,,

where the final inequality comes from the assumption
|J_{x}|\leq b
and our choice of
p
and
C
. We can thus also take
U
to be
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(J_{x};V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}})
or
I_{x}
.

In total, we have at most
5r
randomly chosen sets in which we are interested, and at most
4n
choices of
U
with which we intersect the random sets. Taking the union bound, we see that a.a.s. each of these intersections has size within a
\big(1\pm\tfrac{\varepsilon}{10}\big)
-factor of its expectation. In particular, there exists a partition
{\mathcal{V}}
in which each of these intersections is within a
\big(1\pm\tfrac{\varepsilon}{10}\big)
-factor of its expectation. We fix such a partition, and prove that it is the desired good
G
-partition.

We begin with
(G 0)
. Suppose
ij\in R
. Then
V_{i}
and
V_{j}
come from two parts
V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}
and
V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{j^{\prime}}
of
\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{BL}}
which form a
\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\delta\varepsilon,d,p\big)
-regular pair, and we have
|V_{i}|>\tfrac{1}{2}\delta|V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}|
and
|V_{j}|>\tfrac{1}{2}\delta|V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{j^{\prime}}|
. It follows from the definition that
(V_{i},V_{j})
is
(\varepsilon,d,p)
-regular, so
(G,{\mathcal{V}})
forms an
(\varepsilon,d,p)
-regular
R
-partition. Given now
ij,jk\in R^{\prime}
, with
V_{j}\subseteq V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{j}
and
V_{k}\subseteq V^{\mathrm{BL}}_{k}
, and any
v\in V_{i}
, we have by construction of
{\mathcal{V}}
that
|\big|N_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j})\big|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\delta\big|N_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j^{\prime}})\big|
and that
|V_{k}|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\delta|V_{k^{\prime}}|
. Since the pair
\big(N_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j^{\prime}}),V_{k^{\prime}}\big)
is
\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\delta\varepsilon,d,p\big)
-regular in
G
, it follows from the definition that the pair
\big(N_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j}),V_{k}\big)
is
(\varepsilon,d,p)
-regular in
G
, so that we have one-sided inheritance on
R^{\prime}
. A similar argument shows that if also
jk\in R^{\prime}
and there is a triangle of
H
with one vertex in each of
\tilde{X}_{i}
,
X_{j}
and
X_{k}
, then also
\big(N_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j}),N_{\Gamma}(v;V_{k})\big)
is
(\varepsilon,d,p)
-regular in
G
, so that we have two-sided inheritance on
R^{\prime}
for
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}
.

Both of
(G 0)
and
(G 0)
follow directly from the construction of
{\mathcal{V}}
together with, respectively, the
\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\delta\varepsilon,d,p\big)
-super-regularity of
(G,\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{BL}})
on
R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{BL}}
and that
(\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{BL}},\mathcal{J})
forms a
\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\delta\varrho p^{\vartheta},2\zeta,\Delta,\Delta_{J}\big)
-restriction pair. The same holds for everything except
( )
‣
1.20
in the definition of a restriction pair for
(G 0)
, since
(G 0)
was already established. Given an image restricted
x\in X_{i}\subseteq X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{i^{\prime}}
and an edge
xy\in E(H)
with
Y\in X_{j}\subseteq X^{\mathrm{BL}}_{j^{\prime}}
, the pair
\big(N^{*}_{\Gamma}(J_{x};V_{i^{\prime}}),N^{*}_{\Gamma}(J_{y};V_{j^{\prime}})\big)
is
\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\delta\varepsilon,d,p\big)
-regular by assumption. Since
\big|N^{*}_{\Gamma}(J_{x};V_{i})\big|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\delta\big|N^{*}_{\Gamma}(J_{x};V_{i^{\prime}})\big|
, and similarly for
y
, it follows from the definition that
\big(N^{*}_{\Gamma}(J_{x};V_{i}),N^{*}_{\Gamma}(J_{y};V_{j})\big)
is
(\varepsilon,d,p)
-regular, completing the verification of
(G 0)
.
∎

With this we are ready to define the general setup which is common to
our proofs and encapsulates the definitions of good embeddings and the
constant choices above. This encapsulation is convenient because we will
use this general setup in many of the lemmas to come.

Definition 2.23

(General Setup)
.

When we say that
we assume the General Setup
we assert that
constant choices satisfying the conditions of
Section
2.3.1
have been made, that there exist

\kappa
-balanced size-compatible partitions
{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{V}}
of
V(H)

and
V(G)
respectively whose parts are of size at least

\tfrac{n}{\kappa r_{1}}
, together with reduced graphs
R
and
R^{\prime}
with

\Delta(R^{\prime})\leq\Delta_{R^{\prime}}
, an image restriction pair
(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J})
, an

(\alpha,R^{\prime})
-buffer
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}
, buffer vertices
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
, and sets
V^{\mathrm{main}}
,

V^{\mathrm{q}}
,
V^{\mathrm{c}}
and
V^{\mathrm{buf}}
, that the partition of
V(H)
is a good

H
-partition, and that the partition of
G
is a good
G
-partition.

2.3.5.

In this section we provide definitions of a number of additional sets that
will play important roles in our embedding algorithms. We also state which
invariants are maintained throughout the embedding, and encapsulate this in
the definition of good partial embeddings.

Suppose
\psi
is a
partial embedding
of
H
into
G
, that is, an injective
graph homomorphism with domain
\mathrm{Dom}(\psi)\subseteq V(H)
.
We call a vertex
x\in H

embedded
if
x\in\mathrm{Dom}(\psi)
, and otherwise

unembedded
. Of course this is with respect to
\psi
, but this will
always be clear from the context and we will not in future mention it. The set
of embedded neighbours of
x
is

\Pi(x):=\big\{\psi(y):xy\in E(H),y\in\mathrm{Dom}(\psi)\big\}

and we define

\pi(x):=\big|\Pi(x)\big|\quad\text{and}\quad\pi^{*}(x)=\big|\Pi(x)\big|+\big|J_{x}\big|.

Thus,
\pi^{*}(x)
counts the number of already embedded neighbours of
x
by
the partial embedding
\psi
plus the ‘neighbours’ from
J_{x}
(if any).

Given an unembedded vertex
x\in X_{i}
, we define the
underlying
restriction set

U(x)
, the
candidate set

C(x)
, and the

available candidate set

A(x)
of
x
by

\displaystyle U(x)

\displaystyle:=V(x)\cap N^{*}_{\Gamma}\big(\Pi(x)\cup J_{x}\big)\,,

\displaystyle C(x)

\displaystyle:=I_{x}\cap N^{*}_{G}\big(\Pi(x)\big)\,,\text{ and}

\displaystyle A(x)

\displaystyle:=C(x)\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi)\,,\text{ respectively.}

For an illustration see Figure
2.1
.

Recall that
G
is a subgraph of
\Gamma
(being either a random or a
bijumbled graph), which is typically far from being complete. The
underlying set signifies thus the vertices that could in principle be used
for embedding (if
G=\Gamma
), whereas the candidate sets denote the
vertices that are possible
in

G
, and the available candidate sets
only consider those vertices from the candidate sets which have not been
previously taken as images by the vertices embedded earlier (in

\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
). We also refer to vertices from
C(x)
as candidate vertices
for
x
. Further, we define

\displaystyle U^{\mathrm{main}}(x)

\displaystyle:=U(x)\cap V^{\mathrm{main}}(x)\,,

\displaystyle U^{\mathrm{q}}(x)

\displaystyle:=U(x)\cap V^{\mathrm{q}}(x)\,,

\displaystyle U^{\mathrm{c}}(x)

\displaystyle:=U(x)\cap V^{\mathrm{c}}(x)\,,

\displaystyle U^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)

\displaystyle:=U(x)\cap V^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)\,,

and similarly for the respective subsets of the
candidate set
C(x)
and the available candidate set
A(x)
of
x
. For an illustration see
Figure
2.2
.

Further we define the
underlying
restriction graph
between
Y\subseteq X_{i}
and

Z\subseteq V_{i}
to be the bipartite graph with parts
Y
and
Z
and edges
yz

whenever
z\in U(y)
. We define similarly the
candidate
graph
between
Y\subseteq X_{i}
and

Z\subseteq V_{i}
to be the bipartite graph with parts
Y
and
Z
and edges
yz

whenever
z\in C(y)
. If
x\in V(H)
and
v\in C(x)
we say
v
is a

candidate
for
x
.

Now we can introduce the concept of good partial embeddings.
The intention of this definition is to fix properties which easily allow to
prove that a good partial embedding is ‘locally’
good: If
x
is unembedded, then almost all of
C(x)
consists of vertices
v

such that if
v
is not in
\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
, then
\psi\cup\{x\to v\}
is a good
partial embedding (this assertion is made formal in Lemma
2.27
).
For an illustration see Figure
2.3
.

Definition 2.24

(Good partial embedding)
.

We call
\psi
a
good partial embedding
if the following conditions hold.

(
GPE
0)

For each
x\in\mathrm{Dom}(\psi)
we have
\psi(x)\in I_{x}
.

(
GPE
0)

For each unembedded
x\in X_{i}
we have

\displaystyle\big|U(x)\big|=(p\pm\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}(x)}\big|V(x)\big|\,,\quad\big|U^{\mathrm{main}}(x)\big|

\displaystyle=(1-3\mu)(p\pm\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}(x)}\big|V(x)\big|\,,\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\quad\big|U^{\mathrm{q}}(x)\big|\,,\,\big|U^{\mathrm{c}}(x)\big|\,,\,\big|U^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)\big|

\displaystyle=\mu(p\pm\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}(x)}\big|V(x)\big|\,.

(
GPE
0)

For each unembedded
x
we have

\displaystyle\big|C(x)\big|

\displaystyle\geq(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi(x)}\big|I_{x}\big|\,,

\displaystyle\big|C^{\mathrm{main}}(x)\big|

\displaystyle\geq(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})(1-3\mu)(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi(x)}\big|I_{x}\big|\,,\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\big|C^{\mathrm{q}}(x)\big|\,,\,\big|C^{\mathrm{c}}(x)\big|\,,\,\big|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)\big|

\displaystyle\geq(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})\mu(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi(x)}\big|I_{x}\big|\,.

(
GPE
0)

For each
xy\in E(H)
with
x
and
y
unembedded,
the pair
\big(U(x),U(y)\big)
is
(\varepsilon_{\pi^{*}(x),\pi^{*}(y)},d,p)
-regular in

G
.

x,x^{\prime}

, the wavy lines of

, and the hatched areas represent regular pairs in
