.

It is important to observe that the trivial partial embedding, in which no
vertices are embedded, is not automatically a good partial embedding: image
restricted vertices might destroy any
of
(GPE 0)
–
(GPE 0)
. However, it follows from
straightforward checking of the definitions that if we are provided with a
good
H
-partition and a corresponding good
G
-partition, then the trivial
partial embedding is a good partial embedding.

Fact 2.25
.

If we assume the General Setup for
H
and
G
then the trivial embedding
is a good partial embedding of
H
into
G
.

Finally, in much of the rest of this paper we will want to consider not just one partial embedding, but a sequence
\psi_{0},\dots
of them. We will want to refer to sets
A^{\mathrm{main}}(x)
,
C^{\mathrm{main}}(x)
and so on, and quantities
\pi(x)
and so on, with reference to each of these. We will do this by following the convention that a subscript
t
attached to any of these means it is with reference to
\psi_{t}
. Thus we write
A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)

for the set
A^{\mathrm{main}}(x)
with respect to
\psi_{t}
, and similarly

C^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)
,
\Pi_{t}(x)
,
\pi_{t}(x)
,
\pi^{*}_{t}(x)
,
U_{t}(x)
,
C_{t}(x)
,

A_{t}(x)
and so on.

2.3.6.
Bad vertices

As mentioned in the proof overview, we will embed vertices of
H
into
G

one at a time, choosing each vertex in a way that allows for the future
continuation of the embedding. This ‘allowing for future continuation’
comes in two parts, ‘local’ conditions which broadly say that we embed a
vertex of
H
in a way which allows for the embedding of other vertices
nearby in
H
, and ‘global’ conditions which say that the embedding of many
(distant) vertices of
H
do not tend to cause problems for any given
vertex. We handle the latter probabilistically, and this constitutes the
main work of this paper. The purpose of this subsection is to define
precisely what we require locally, by specifying which vertices of
G
are
bad for
x\in V(H)
, that is, not suitable for embedding
x
to, and to
show that this is always a small set of vertices (in comparison to the
set
A(x)
). In fact, the definition of badness also depends on a set

Q\subseteq V(H)
. In the random greedy algorithm (outlined in the
proof overview, Section
1.4
),
Q
will be the current
queue, while at other times
Q
will simply be
V(H)
.

Definition 2.26

(Bad vertices, badness condition)
.

Let
\psi
be a good partial embedding and
Q\subseteq V(H)
be a set of
unembedded vertices. The vertex
v
is called
bad for
x
with
respect to
\psi
and
Q
if the extension
\psi\cup\{x\to v\}
is
not a good partial embedding or there is an unembedded neighbour
y
of

x
with
y\not\in Q
such that

\deg_{G}\big(v;A^{\mathrm{main}}(y)\big)<(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p|A^{\mathrm{main}}(y)|\,.

(2.5)

We stress that in this equation we take
A^{\mathrm{main}}(y)
with respect to the
embedding
\psi
(and not the extended embedding).
When
\psi
and
Q
are clear from the context (as they always will be) we let
B(x)
be the set of vertices in
C(x)
which are bad for
x
with respect to
\psi
and
Q
.
When we have a sequence of embeddings
\psi_{0},\dots
and a sequence of sets

Q_{0},\dots
then we write, again,
B_{t}(x)
for the set of bad
vertices for
x
with respect to
\psi_{t}
and
Q_{t}
.
We will also refer to (
2.5
) as the
badness condition
.

The following lemma provides control over the bad vertices with respect
to a good partial embedding. We remark that the definition of the
value
D
in this lemma is somewhat complex, because we want to apply
this lemma in the proofs of all three of our blow-up lemmas, in
particular the blow-up lemma for degenerate graphs.

Lemma 2.27

(Few bad vertices)
.

We assume the General Setup. Let
\psi
be a good partial embedding and let
Q\subseteq V(H)
be a set of vertices such that for each unembedded
x\in V(H)\setminus Q
we
have

|A^{\mathrm{main}}(x)|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{*}(x)}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(x)|\,.

Given an unembedded
x\in V(H)
, let
D
be such that for all unembedded

y,z\in V(H)
we have

(

)

D\geq\pi^{*}(x)+1
,

(

)

If
xy\in E(H)
then
D\geq\pi^{*}(y)+1
,

(

)

If
xy,yz\in E(H)
then
D\geq\pi^{*}(y)+2,\pi^{*}(z)+1
,

(

)

If
xy,yz,xz\in E(H)
then
D\geq\pi^{*}(x)+2,\pi^{*}(y)+2,\pi^{*}(z)+2
.

Then the following holds.

(

)

If all neighbours of
x
are embedded then no
vertex in
A(x)
is bad for
x
with respect to

\psi
and
Q
.

(

)

Suppose that
\Gamma
satisfies
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)

and
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},D)
. Then at most

20\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V(x)|
vertices of
C(x)
are bad for
x
with respect to
\psi
and
Q
.

Proof.

We require

\mu\leq\tfrac{1}{6}\,,\qquad\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\frac{\mu d^{\Delta}\zeta}{1000\kappa 4^{\Delta}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\varepsilon\leq\frac{d\varepsilon^{\prime}}{\kappa}\,.

For
( )
‣
2.27
, observe that all the conditions for a vertex
v
to be in
B(x)
are trivially false.

For part
( )
‣
2.27
, we need to consider all of the possible reasons

v
could be bad for
x
with respect to
\psi
and
Q
.
It could be that
there is some unembedded neighbour
y\in V(H)\setminus Q
of
x
such
that the badness condition (
2.5
) holds.
The pair
\big(U(x),U(y)\big)
is
(\varepsilon^{\prime},d,p)
-regular in
G

by
(GPE 0)
.
By assumption and because
(GPE 0)
holds for
y
with respect to
\psi

we have

\begin{split}|A^{\mathrm{main}}(y)|&\geq\frac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{*}(y)}p^{\pi^{*}(y)}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(y)|=\frac{1}{2}\mu(1-3\mu)(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{*}(y)}p^{\pi^{*}(y)}|V(y)|\\
&\geq\frac{1}{2}\mu(1-3\mu)(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{*}(y)}p^{\pi^{*}(y)}\frac{|U(y)|}{(1+\varepsilon)^{\pi^{*}(y)}p^{\pi^{*}(y)}}\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}|U(y)|\,.\end{split}

So the badness condition (
2.5
)
holds for at most
\varepsilon^{\prime}|U(x)|\leq 2\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V(x)|

vertices
v
of
C(x)
, where we use
(GPE 0)
and
(1+\varepsilon)^{\Delta}\leq 2
.
Since
x
has at most

\Delta
unembedded neighbours, in total there are at most

2\Delta\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V(x)|
vertices
v
of
C(x)
such
that the badness condition (
2.5
) holds for some unembedded neighbour of
x
.

It could be that
\psi\cup\{x\to v\}

is not a good partial embedding. Since
v\in C(x)\subseteq I_{x}
,
(GPE 0)
cannot fail.
We next consider
(GPE 0)
. Since
\psi
is a good partial
embedding,
(GPE 0)
cannot fail for any vertex
y
which is not a
neighbour of
x
(because the candidate sets of these vertices do not change).
So let
y
be any neighbour of
x
in
H
.
Recall that

\big(U(x),U(y)\big)
is
(\varepsilon^{\prime},d,p)
-regular in
G
by
(GPE 0)
.
We have

\begin{split}|C(y)|&\overset{\mbox{\tiny{\ref{GPE:sizeC}}}}{\geq}(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi(y)}|I_{y}|\overset{\mbox{\tiny{\ref{G:restr}}}}{\geq}(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}(y)}\zeta|V(y)|\\
&\overset{\mbox{\tiny{\ref{GPE:sizeU}}}}{\geq}(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\Delta}(1+\varepsilon^{\prime})^{-\Delta}\zeta|U(y)|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}|U(y)|\,.\end{split}

Since
C(x)\subseteq U(x)
, there are at most
\varepsilon^{\prime}|U(x)|\leq 2\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V_{i}|
vertices
v\in C(x)
such that
|C(y)\cap N_{G}(v)|<(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p|C(y)|
. Since

(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p|C(y)|\geq(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi(y)+1}|I(y)|
, there are
at most
2\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V(x)|
vertices
v\in C(x)
such that the first
condition in
(GPE 0)
fails for
y
with respect to

\psi\cup\{x\to v\}
. We can analogously argue for
each of the other four conditions. Since
x
has at most
\Delta

unembedded neighbours, we conclude that for all but at most
10\Delta\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V_{i}|

vertices
v\in C(x)
we have that
(GPE 0)
is satisfied with
respect to
\psi\cup\{x\to v\}
.

Now we turn to
(GPE 0)
. Again, it suffices to consider
neighbours
y
of
x
. As
(GPE 0)
holds with respect
to
\psi
, we see that
|U(y)|\geq(p-\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}(y)}|V(y)|\geq\varepsilon p^{D-1}n/r_{1}
since
|V(y)|\geq n/(\kappa r_{1})
and by
assumption
( )
‣
2.27
. It follows
that we can use
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)
and assumption
( )
‣
2.27
to
conclude that all but at most
\varepsilon p^{D-1}n/r_{1}^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V(x)|
vertices
v\in C(x)
are such that
|U(y)\cap N_{\Gamma}(v)|=(1\pm\varepsilon)p|U(y)|=(p\pm\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}(y)+1}|V(y)|
, again using
(GPE 0)
for
y
with respect to
\psi
. Again, we
can argue analogously for the other four conditions, and so in total for
all but at most
5\Delta\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V(x)|
vertices
v\in C(x)
we
have that
(GPE 0)
is satisfied with respect to
\psi\cup\{x\to v\}
.

It remains to consider
(GPE 0)
. Again, we only need to consider
edges
yz\in E(H)
between unembedded vertices such that
y\in N_{H}(x)
or

z\in N_{H}(x)
, or both. Consider first the case that only
y\in N_{H}(x)
. The case that only
z\in N_{H}(x)
follows
analogously. Since
(GPE 0)
is satisfied with respect to
\psi
we
have
|U(y)|\geq(p-\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}(y)}|V(y)|
and
|U(z)|\geq(p-\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}(z)}|V(z)|
, which together with assumption
( )
‣
2.27

implies
|U(y)|\geq(p-\varepsilon p)^{D-2}n/(\kappa r_{1})\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{D-2}n/r_{1}^{2}
and

|U(z)|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{D-1}n/r_{1}^{2}
. Hence, since
\Gamma
satisfies

\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},D)
and the pair
\big(U(y),U(z)\big)

is
(\varepsilon_{\pi^{*}(y),\pi^{*}(z)},d,p)
-regular in
G
because
(GPE 0)

is satisfied with respect to
\psi
, we get that there are at most

\varepsilon p^{D-1}n/r_{1}^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V(x)|
vertices
v\in C(x)
such that
\big(N_{\Gamma}(v)\cap U(y),U(z)\big)
is not

(\varepsilon_{\pi^{*}(y)+1,\pi^{*}(z)},d,p)
-regular in
G
. Analogously, in the second
case that
y,z\in N_{H}(x)
we can use assumption
( )
‣
2.27
to
conclude that there are at most
\varepsilon p^{D-2}n/r_{1}^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V_{i}|
vertices
v\in C(x)

such that
\big(N_{\Gamma}(v)\cap U(y),N_{\Gamma}(v)\cap U(z)\big)
is not

(\varepsilon_{\pi^{*}(y)+1,\pi^{*}(z)+1},d,p)
-regular in
G
. So, in total there are at
most
\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V(x)|
vertices
v\in C(x)
such
that
(GPE 0)
is not satisfied with respect to
\psi\cup\{x\to v\}
.

Summing up, there are at most

2\Delta\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V(x)|+10\Delta\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V(x)|+5\Delta\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V(x)|+\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V(x)|\\
\leq 20\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}(x)}|V(x)|

vertices
v\in C(x)
such
that
v
is bad for
x
with respect to
\psi
and
Q
.
∎

2.4.
The behaviour of random greedy algorithms

The proofs of our three blow-up lemmas each use, as crucial ingredient, a
slightly different random greedy algorithm (RGA). In this section we
collect four different lemmas which will be useful in conjunction with
these RGAs.

Roughly speaking, each of our RGAs has the property that we embed
vertices sequentially, at each time choosing an image of the next vertex
uniformly at random in a not-too-small subset of its candidate vertices,
and this, together with the General Setup (see Definition
2.23
),
is all we need to know about
our RGAs for the purposes of this section. Nevertheless, the reader who
wishes to see a concrete example of an RGA before reading the following
lemmas should look at Algorithm
1
in Section
3.2
,
which is the simplest of our RGAs. The other two RGAs are
Algorithm
3
in Section
4.2
and
Algorithm
4
in Section
5.2
.

Our first lemma (Lemma
2.28
) constructs the vertex ordering
which we shall use in the RGAs for the random graphs blow-up lemma
(Lemma
1.21
) and the blow-up lemma for bijumbled graphs
(Lemma
1.25
). The point of the ordering we use is that we
need to analyse the way neighbours of (non-clique) buffer vertices are
embedded during the RGA. Putting them first in the order, with each
neighbourhood coming as an interval in the order, makes this
possible. Even then, for Lemma
1.21
we need to insist that
in degree-
\Delta
buffers the final two neighbours of a buffer vertex
are not adjacent, thus their embeddings do not affect each other
much.

In the proof of our blow-up lemma for degenerate graphs
(Lemma
1.23
) we cannot apply this lemma, because we have to
work with the order we are supplied with. Ultimately, the fact that we
cannot expect these nice conditions from this supplied order is the
reason why we cannot obtain stronger bounds on
p
in that lemma.

Lemma 2.28

(A good vertex order for an RGA)
.

Let
H
be a graph with
\Delta(H)\leq\Delta
and
V(H)=X^{\mathrm{buf}}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}X^{\mathrm{main}}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}X^{\mathrm{c}}

such that
N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})\subseteq X^{\mathrm{main}}
and each pair of vertices in
X^{\mathrm{buf}}

has distance at least ten.
Then there exists an ordering
\tau
of
X^{\mathrm{main}}
with the
following properties.

(

)

For all
x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
and
y\in N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
we
have
\tau(x)>\tau(y)
.

(

)

For all
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}
we can enumerate
N(x)
as
y_{1},\dots,y_{b}

such that
\tau(y_{j+1})=\tau(y_{j})+1
for all

j\in[b-1]
. Moreover, if
\deg_{H}(x)=\Delta
and
x
is not in a copy of
K_{\Delta+1}
then

y_{\Delta-1}y_{\Delta}\not\in E(H)
.

(

)

The neighbours of non-clique buffer vertices come before the neighbours of the
clique-buffer vertices.

Note that in this
lemma we do not need to assume the General Setup, but we
nevertheless use names for the parts of
H
corresponding to those from
the General Setup.

Proof.

We separate the vertices in
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
into two classes, those not in
copies of
K_{\Delta+1}
and those in copies of
K_{\Delta+1}
. We take
an enumeration
X^{\mathrm{buf}}=\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{|X^{\mathrm{buf}}|}\}
with the vertices not
in copies of
K_{\Delta+1}
coming first. We now create the ordering

\tau
as follows. We start with the empty ordering. For each
i
in
succession such that
x_{i}\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}
is not in a copy of
K_{\Delta+1}
,
we append to
\tau
the vertices
N_{H}(x_{i})
in some order such that if

\deg_{H}(x_{i})=\Delta
then the last two vertices in
\tau
of
N_{H}(x_{i})

are not adjacent. Note that if
\deg_{H}(x)=\Delta
then there is a pair of non-adjacent vertices in

N_{H}(x_{i})
because
x_{i}
is not in a copy of
K_{\Delta+1}
. Next, for
each
i
in succession such that
x_{i}\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}
is in a copy of

K_{\Delta+1}
we append to
\tau
the vertices
N_{H}(x_{i})
in an
arbitrary order. Finally, we append to
\tau
any remaining vertices of

X^{\mathrm{main}}
in an arbitrary order.
∎

The next lemma states that provided we embed vertices uniformly at random
into not-too-small sets, the candidate sets of unembedded vertices are
likely to be distributed uniformly. In this lemma, as will be the case for
much of the rest of the paper, we have a sequence
\psi_{0},\dots
of good
partial embeddings, and we want to talk about the various sets and
quantities defined in Section
2.3.5
, such as candidate sets, with
respect to each of these good partial embeddings. As mentioned in
Section
2.3.5
, we will follow the convention that (for example)

C_{t}(x)
is the candidate set of
x
with respect to
\psi_{t}
. Recall also, that
the set
X_{i}^{*}\subseteq X_{i}
denotes image restricted
vertices in
X_{i}
(see Definition
1.20
).

Lemma 2.29

(Uniform distribution of candidate sets)
.

We assume the General Setup. Suppose that for some integer
T
we have a
sequence
\psi_{0},\psi_{1},\dots,\psi_{T}
of good partial embeddings,
where
\psi_{0}
is the trivial partial embedding
and each
\psi_{t}
is obtained from
\psi_{t-1}
by embedding some
vertex
x
in
V(H)\setminus\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{t-1})
to a uniform random
vertex from a subset
S
of
C_{t-1}(x)
with

|S|\geq\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi_{t-1}^{*}(x)}|V(x)|
.

Then the following holds with probability at least

1-2^{-n/(\kappa r_{1})}r_{1}
. For every
i\in[r]
and every set

W\subseteq V_{i}
of size at least
\varrho|V_{i}|
, the number of
vertices
x\in X_{i}\setminus X^{*}_{i}
(i.e. vertices which are not
image restricted) such that there exists
t=t(x)
when
x
is
unembedded and we have

|C_{t}(x)\cap W|<\big(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p\big)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|W|

(2.6)

is at most
\varrho|X_{i}|
.

This lemma corresponds to the Main Lemma of
[
54
]
, and its proof is
similar in spirit. The proof of this lemma exploits the fact that when a
vertex
y
is embedded, condition (
2.6
) might become
true for at most one vertex from
X_{i}\setminus X^{*}_{i}
(since
by
(H 0)
the vertices in
X_{i}
have distance at least

10
). Moreover, by the condition of always embedding into not too small
subsets and by the regularity property
(GPE 0)
, the vertex
y
makes
an unlucky choice with a very small probability, so that many such choices
are extremely unlikely.

Proof of Lemma
2.29
.

We require

\varepsilon^{\prime}<\min\big(4^{-\Delta}d^{\Delta}\varrho,\tfrac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}}{20\Delta}2^{-4/\varrho}\big)\quad\text{and}\quad 2^{-n/\kappa r_{1}}r_{1}<1\,.

Fix a set
W\subseteq V_{i}
of size at least
\varrho|V_{i}|
, and a set
X^{\prime}\subseteq X_{i}\setminus X^{*}_{i}
of size
\varrho|X_{i}|
. We aim to show that the probability of the following event is at most
2^{-4|X_{i}|}
. For each
x\in X^{\prime}
there is a
t=t(x)
such that
x
satisfies (
2.6
). The desired result then follows by taking the union bound over all
i
, subsets
W
of
V_{i}
and
X^{\prime}
of
X_{i}
.

If
x
is any vertex of
X^{\prime}
, then we have
C_{0}(x)=V_{i}
since
x
is not image restricted, thus

|C_{0}(x)\cap W|=|W|
and hence (
2.6
) is false for
x
.
If there is a
t(x)
such that
x
satisfies (
2.6
), then we can fix
t
to be the smallest integer such that (
2.6
) is true for
t+1
and
x
.
Since the candidate set of
x
changes
only when a neighbour of
x
is embedded, it follows that
the vertex
y
that is embedded to create
\psi_{t+1}
from
\psi_{t}
is a neighbour of
x
in
H
and thus
\pi^{*}_{t+1}(x)=\pi^{*}_{t}(x)+1
.
Moreover, since equation (
2.6
) becomes true for
x
,
the vertex
y
is embedded to a vertex
w
such that

\deg_{G}\big(w;C_{t}(x)\cap W\big)<(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p\big|C_{t}(x)\cap W\big|\,,

(2.7)

as otherwise we would still have

|C_{t+1}(x)\cap W|\geq((d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p\big)|C_{t}(x)\cap W|\geq((d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p)^{\pi^{*}_{t+1}(x)}|W|\,.

Since
\psi_{t}
is a good partial embedding, by
(GPE 0)
the pair

\big(U_{t}(x),U_{t}(y)\big)
is an
(\varepsilon^{\prime},d,p)
-regular pair in
G
, and by
(GPE 0)
we have

\big|U_{t}(x)\big|=(p\pm\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|V_{i}|
, and
\big|U_{t}(y)\big|=(p\pm\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}|V(y)|
. Since (
2.6
) is false for
x
at time
t
,
we have
\big|C_{t}(x)\cap W\big|\geq\big((d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p\big)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|W|
, and so

\big|C_{t}(x)\cap W\big|\geq\left(\tfrac{d-\varepsilon^{\prime}}{1+\varepsilon^{\prime}}\right)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}\varrho|U_{t}(x)|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}|U_{t}(x)|\,,

by the requirements on the constants at the beginning of the proof.
We conclude by
(\varepsilon^{\prime},d,p)
-regularity of
\big(U_{t}(x),U_{t}(y)\big)
that at most
\varepsilon^{\prime}\big|U_{t}(y)\big|\leq 2\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}|V(y)|
vertices
w
of
U_{t}(y)
satisfy (
2.7
).

Since
\psi_{t+1}
is created by embedding
y
uniformly at random into a subset of
C_{t}(y)\subseteq U_{t}(y)
of size at least
\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}|V(y)|
, the probability of embedding
y
to a vertex
w
satisfying (
2.7
), conditioning on any history, is at most

\frac{2\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}|V(y)|}{\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}|V(y)|}\leq 20\varepsilon^{\prime}\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}\,.

(2.8)

Next we argue that the probability that for each
x\in X^{\prime}
there is a first time
t=t(x)
such that
x
satisfies (
2.6
) is at most

\big(20\Delta\varepsilon^{\prime}\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}\big)^{\varrho|X_{i}|}\leq 2^{-4|X_{i}|}\,.

Let us denote this event by
\mathcal{E}_{X^{\prime}}
. Observe that
\mathcal{E}_{X^{\prime}}
is split into
\Delta^{|X^{\prime}|}
events (since
\Delta(H)\leq\Delta
) by specifying for each
x\in X^{\prime}
a neighbour
y_{x}
of
x
whose embedding occurs at time
t(x)
. Let
(y_{x})_{x\in X^{\prime}}
be any such assignment, and
\mathcal{E}_{X^{\prime},(y_{x})}
be the corresponding event. We aim to bound
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{X^{\prime},(y_{x})})
.

By
(H 0)
the vertices of
X_{i}
are at distance at least ten in
H
, so the vertices
(y_{x})_{x\in X^{\prime}}
are distinct. The corresponding conditional probabilities thus multiply, and we have

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{X^{\prime},(y_{x})})\leq\big(20\varepsilon^{\prime}\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}\big)^{|X^{\prime}|}

by (
2.8
). Applying the union bound over the events
\mathcal{E}_{X^{\prime},(y_{x})}
we conclude

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{X^{\prime}})\leq\big(20\Delta\varepsilon^{\prime}\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}\big)^{\varrho|X_{i}|}\leq 2^{-4|X_{i}|}\,.

where the final inequality is by choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
.

Taking the union bound over the at most
2^{|V_{i}|}=2^{|X_{i}|}
choices of
W
in
V_{i}
and
2^{|X_{i}|}
choices of
X^{\prime}
in
X_{i}
, we see that the probability that, for any fixed
i
, there exist subsets
W
of
V_{i}
and
X^{\prime}
of
X_{i}
, of sizes at least
\varrho|V_{i}|
and
\varrho|X_{i}|
respectively, such that each vertex
x
of
X^{\prime}
satisfies (
2.6
) at some time
t
, is at most
2^{2|X_{i}|}2^{-4|X_{i}|}=2^{-2|X_{i}|}
. Now we have
|X_{i}|\geq n/(\kappa r_{1})
, and
n\geq n_{0}
where
n_{0}
is chosen large enough such that
2^{-n_{0}/(\kappa r_{1})}r_{1}<1
. Thus taking the union bound over the at most
r_{1}
choices of
i
, we conclude that the probability that there exists
i
, and a subset
W
of
V_{i}
such that there are
\varrho|X_{i}|
vertices
x
of
X_{i}\setminus X^{*}_{i}
each of which satisfies (
2.6
) at some time
t
, is at most
2^{-n/(\kappa r_{1})}r_{1}
as desired.
∎

The next lemma, Lemma
2.30
, shows that, again provided we embed vertices uniformly
into not-too-small sets, we do not tend to cover vertex neighbourhoods
in
G
disproportionately fast. Specifically, if
ij\in E(R^{\prime})
then,
by
(G 0)
, each
v\in V_{i}
has a large
G
-neighbourhood in

V_{j}
. At some time
T
when only a small but linear fraction of each
part of
H
has been embedded, it is very likely that less than half of
this
G
-neighbourhood is in the image
\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{T})
of the current
partial embedding. One should think of this as: early on in the
embedding process, the minimum degree conditions
(G 0)
provided by
super-regularity are preserved. This fact is trivially satisfied in the dense blow-up
lemma
[
54
]
, but it is far from trivial in our case.

We will apply this lemma in the proofs of the random graphs blow-up
lemma (Lemma
1.21
) as well as the jumbled graphs blow-up
lemma (Lemma
1.25
). For the proof of the degenerate graphs
blow-up lemma, on the other hand, we cannot use it; but we will replace
it by a suitable modification of the ideas in its proof.

The idea behind Lemma
2.30
is as follows. As discussed in
the proof overview (Section
1.4
), we will need our
RGAs to guarantee that each
v\in V_{i}
is a candidate for many vertices

x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
in order to complete the embedding. This means we need it
to be not too unlikely that
N_{H}(x)
is embedded to
N_{G}(v)
for any
given
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, and as a first step to showing this, it is
necessary to show that we have not covered
N_{G}(v)
with embedded
vertices before we get around to embedding
N_{H}(x)
. In the proofs of
the random graphs blow-up lemma and the bijumbled blow-up lemma, we show
this by embedding
N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
first and applying
Lemma
2.30
.

Lemma 2.30

(Preservation of super-regularity)
.

We assume the General Setup. Suppose that
\Gamma
has

\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},B)
. Suppose that for some
T
we have a sequence

\psi_{0},\psi_{1},\dots,\psi_{T}
of good partial embeddings, where

\psi_{0}
is the trivial partial embedding and each
\psi_{t}
is
obtained from
\psi_{t-1}
by embedding one vertex
x_{t}\in V(H)
to a
uniform random vertex from a subset
S
of
C_{t-1}(x_{t})
with

|S|\geq\tfrac{1}{10}(dp)^{\pi_{t-1}^{*}(x_{t})}|V(x_{t})|
. Suppose furthermore
that for each
t
the vertex
x_{t}
has at most
B-2
neighbours in

\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{t-1})
, and that for each
i\in[r]
we have

\big|\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{T})\cap X_{i}\big|\leq 8\mu\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}|X_{i}|
.

Then with probability at least
1-\exp(-\varepsilon pn/r_{1})
, for each

i\in[r]
, each vertex
v\in V_{i}
, and each
j
such that

ij\in E(R^{\prime})

we have
\big|N_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}_{j})\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{T})\big|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\deg_{G}(v;V_{j})
.

In the proof of the bijumbled blow-up lemma we set
B=\Delta+1
and will show
that this is enough for Lemma
2.30
to handle all of

N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
. In the proof of the random graphs blow-up lemma, by contrast, we set

B=\Delta
, which turns out to be good enough to handle buffers of degree
up to and including
\Delta
which are not clique buffers. This is one of
the reasons why we have to handle clique buffers differently.

The proof of Lemma
2.30
involves estimating the probability
of embedding
x_{t}
to
N_{G}(v)
, conditioned on
\psi_{t-1}
. We show that
either this probability is small, or that a previously embedded neighbour
of
x_{t}
was embedded oddly, which is guaranteed to be a low-probability
event. In either case, for
x_{t}
to be embedded to
N_{G}(v)
an unlikely
event must occur. For
N_{G}(v)
to be substantially filled up, many of these
events, which are sequentially dependent, have to occur. The sequential
dependence lemma, Lemma
2.2
, then shows this is unlikely
enough to take a union bound over all choices of
v
.

Proof.

We require

\mu<\tfrac{d^{\Delta}}{1320\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}(\Delta+1)}\,,\quad\varepsilon<\tfrac{\mu d}{10\kappa}\quad\text{and}\quad r_{1}n\exp(-\varepsilon pn/r_{1})<1\,.

Fix
ij\in E(R^{\prime})
and a vertex
v\in V_{i}
. We first estimate the conditional probability that
x_{t}\in X_{j}\cap\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{T})
is embedded to
N_{G}(v)\cap V^{\mathrm{main}}(x_{t})
, given the history. Because
x_{t}
is embedded uniformly at random to a subset of
C_{t-1}(x_{t})
of size at least
\tfrac{1}{10}(dp)^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(x_{t})}|V_{j}|
, this probability is at most

\frac{\big|U^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(x_{t})\cap N_{G}(v)\big|}{\tfrac{1}{10}(dp)^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(x_{t})}|V_{j}|}\,,

(2.9)

which we will show to be at most
20d^{-\Delta}\tfrac{\deg_{G}(v;V_{j})}{|V_{j}|}
.

This clearly holds when
\big|U^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(x_{t})\cap N_{G}(v)\big|\leq(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(x_{t})}\deg_{G}(v;V_{j})

and we therefore assume the opposite.
In this case the estimate on the conditional
probability (
2.9
) that
x_{t}
occupies a vertex from

N_{G}(v)
could be as great as
1
, but we will show that this is an unlikely event. Specifically, for this to occur
there must be a first neighbour
y_{t}
of
x_{t}
in
H
which is

embedded oddly
at time
t^{\prime}<t
, that is, is such that
\big|U^{\mathrm{main}}_{t^{\prime}-1}(x_{t})\cap N_{G}(v)\big|\leq(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}_{t^{\prime}-1}(x_{t})}\deg_{G}(v;V_{j})
but (with
\pi^{*}_{t^{\prime}}(x_{t})=\pi^{*}_{t^{\prime}-1}(x_{t})+1
)

\deg_{\Gamma}\big(\psi_{t^{\prime}}(y_{t});U^{\mathrm{main}}_{t^{\prime}-1}(x_{t})\cap N_{G}(v)\big)>(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}_{t^{\prime}-1}(x_{t})+1}\deg_{G}(v;V_{j})\,.

(2.10)

Let
W
be a superset of
U^{\mathrm{main}}_{t^{\prime}-1}(x_{t})\cap N_{G}(v)
of size
(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}_{t^{\prime}-1}(x_{t})}\deg_{G}(v;V_{j})>\mu(d-\varepsilon)p^{B-1}|V_{j}|
, where the inequality uses the fact that
\pi^{*}_{t^{\prime}-1}(x_{t})\leq B-2
and
(G 0)
.
By property
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},B)
, which
\Gamma
satisfies, we see that there are at most
\varepsilon p^{B-1}n/r_{1}^{2}
vertices
Z
of
\Gamma
which have this many neighbours in
W
and thus satisfy (
2.10
).
Now
\pi^{*}_{t^{\prime}-1}(y)\leq B-2
by the conditions of the lemma. We conclude that the probability of embedding
y
to a vertex of
Z
, conditioning on the history, is at most

\tfrac{\varepsilon p^{B-1}n/r_{1}^{2}}{\tfrac{1}{10}(dp)^{B-2}|V(y)|}\leq 10\varepsilon d^{-\Delta}\kappa p<10d^{-\Delta+1}p\leq 20d^{-\Delta}\tfrac{\deg_{G}(v;V_{j})}{|V_{j}|}\,,

(2.11)

where we use
|V(y)|\geq n/(\kappa r_{1})
in the first and the choice of
\varepsilon
in the second and
(G 0)
in the third inequality.

We define a sequence of Bernoulli random variables
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{T}
as follows. Given the embedding
\psi_{t-1}
, if
x_{t}
is a neighbour of a vertex in
X_{j}\cap\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{T})
none of whose previous neighbours were oddly embedded, and
x_{t}
is oddly embedded, we set
Y_{t}=1
. If
x_{t}
is in
X_{j}
, none of its previous neighbours were oddly embedded, and
x_{t}
is embedded to
N_{G}(v;V_{j})
, we set
Y_{t}=1
. Otherwise we set
Y_{t}=0
. By assumption, the total number of
Y_{t}
which are not deterministically zero (that is, are in
X_{j}\cap\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{T})
or are neighbours of such a vertex) is at most
8\mu\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}(\Delta+1)|X_{i}|
. Observe furthermore that for all of the
Y_{t}
which are not deterministically zero, we have just shown in (
2.11
) that
Y_{t}
is one with probability at most
20d^{-\Delta}\tfrac{\deg_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}_{j})}{|V_{j}|}
conditioning on the history up to, but not including, embedding
x_{t}
. This history determines
Y_{t-1}
, so that the
Y_{1},\dots,Y_{T}
are sequentially dependent. It follows that we can apply Lemma
2.2
, with

x=8\mu\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}(\Delta+1)|X_{j}|\cdot 20d^{-\Delta}\tfrac{\deg_{G}(v;V_{j})}{|V_{j}|}=160\mu\kappa d^{-\Delta}\Delta_{R^{\prime}}(\Delta+1)\deg_{G}(v;V_{j})

and
\delta=1
, to show that

\mathbb{P}\big(Y_{1}+\dots+Y_{T}\geq 2x\big)\leq 2\exp\big(-\tfrac{x}{3}\big)<\exp\big(-\tfrac{2\varepsilon pn}{r_{1}}\big)\,,

where the final inequality is by choice of
\varepsilon
and since
\deg_{G}(v;V_{j})\geq(1-3\mu)(d-\varepsilon)p|V_{j}|
by
(G 0)
. By choice of
\mu
, we conclude that with probability at most
\exp(-2\varepsilon pn/r_{1})
we have
Y_{1}+\dots+Y_{T}\geq\tfrac{1}{4}\deg_{G}(v;V_{j})
. Now observe that for any vertex to be embedded to
N_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}_{j})
one of these variables
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{T}
must be one, and since vertices of
X_{j}
are at distance at least ten in
H
(by
(H 0)
), no
Y_{t}
can be responsible for two different vertices of
X_{j}
being embedded to
N_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}_{j})
. Thus
Y_{1}+\dots+Y_{T}
is an upper bound for
\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{T})\cap N_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}_{j})
. Taking a union bound over the choices of
j
and of
v
, and using
(G 0)
, we see that with probability at least
1-r_{1}n\exp(-2\varepsilon pn/r_{1})>1-\exp(-\varepsilon pn/r_{1})
the statement of the lemma holds.
∎

Our final lemma in this section complements the above lemma, showing that
provided
v\in V_{i}
does not have
N_{G}(v)
more than half covered by

\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
early in the embedding, it is reasonably likely that
N_{H}(x)

is embedded to
N_{G}(v)
for any given
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. As with the previous
lemma, it contains a parameter
B
which will be either set to
\Delta

(in the proof of the random graphs blow-up lemma) or to
\Delta+1
(in the proof
of the bijumbled graphs blow-up lemma), and again in the former case the consequence
is that we cannot use it to deal with clique buffers (that is, when

N_{H}(x)
is a copy of
K_{\Delta}
). For the degenerate graphs blow-up lemma
we again need to replace this lemma by suitable similar ideas.

Lemma 2.31

(Buffer neighbour embedding)
.

We assume the General Setup. Let

B\in\{\Delta,\Delta+1\}
and assume that
\Gamma
has
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},B)

and
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},B)
. Given
v\in V_{i}
and

x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
,
let
y_{1},\ldots,y_{b}
be the neighbours
of
x
, and suppose that if
b=B
then
y_{b-1}y_{b}
is not an edge of

H
.
Let
\psi_{0}
be a good partial embedding in which
no vertex at distance two or less from
x
is embedded, and let

Q_{0}\subseteq X^{\mathrm{main}}
. Suppose further that

\big|N_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}_{j})\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{0})\big|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\deg_{G}(v;V_{j})

for each
j
such that
ij\in R^{\prime}
.

Let
\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{b}
be good partial embeddings and

Q_{1},\ldots,Q_{b}
subsets of
X^{\mathrm{main}}
, such
that for each
t=1,\dots,b
the embedding
\psi_{t}
is obtained
from
\psi_{t-1}
by embedding
y_{t}
uniformly at random into its
available candidates minus the vertices which are bad for
y_{t}
, that is, into

A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})\setminus B_{t-1}(y_{t})
, and such that
we have

Q_{t}=Q_{t-1}\cup\big\{z\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{t})\colon\big|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(z)\big|<\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{*}_{t}(z)}p^{\pi^{*}_{t}(z)}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(z)|\big\}\,.

Then with probability at least
\big(d^{\Delta}p/10\big)^{b}
, we have
\psi_{b}\big(N_{H}(x)\big)\subseteq N_{G}(v)
.

The proof of this lemma is quite long, but much of it is ‘bookkeeping’.
Briefly, the idea is as
follows. In
\psi_{0}
, no neighbour of any
y_{j}
is embedded and hence
each
y_{j}
has candidate set
C_{0}(y_{j})=V(y_{j})
. We know that

N_{G}\big(v;V(y_{1})\big)
is not covered by
\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{0})
, so if we
choose a uniform random vertex of
A_{0}(y_{1})
the probability of
choosing a member of
N_{G}(v)
is at least
dp/4
. However, the random
greedy algorithm does not choose a random vertex of
A_{0}(y_{1})
, but
rather of
A_{0}(y_{1})\setminus B_{0}(y_{1})
. Thus we have to show that

B_{0}(y_{1})
does not cover too much of
N_{G}(v)
, which we can do using
properties
(G 0)
and
(G 0)
of the partition of
G
,
and
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},B)
and
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},B)
which
we assume of
\Gamma
, in much the same way as the proof of
Lemma
2.27
. In addition we have to show that some extra
properties are preserved which allow us to analyse the embedding of

y_{2},\dots,y_{b}
, which we can show is likely in a similar way. We will
refer to a good partial embedding with these extra properties as a
buffer-friendly partial embedding. We conclude that the probability of
embedding
y_{j}
to
N_{G}(v)
and maintaining a buffer-friendly partial embedding
is at least
d^{\Delta}p/10
, conditioning on the history, for each

j
. The conditional probabilities multiply, giving the desired
result.

Proof.

We require

\mu\leq\tfrac{1}{6}\,,\qquad\varepsilon<(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2},\qquad\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\frac{\mu d^{\Delta}\zeta}{1000\kappa\Delta^{2}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{10\Delta}>\tfrac{1}{2}d^{10\Delta}\,.

For each
0\leq t\leq b
and each
y_{j}
,
j\in[b]
, we define

\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(y_{j}):=A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(y_{j})\cap N_{G}(v)
and
for any vertex
x^{\prime}\in X
we set
\hat{U}_{t}(x^{\prime}):=U_{t}(x^{\prime})\cap N_{\Gamma}(v)
.

Observe that since
y_{t}
is embedded to a vertex of
C_{t-1}(y_{t})\setminus B_{t-1}(y_{t})
for each
t=1,\dots,b
we automatically maintain the property
that
\psi_{t}
is a good partial embedding for each
t
(see
Definition
2.26
of bad vertices). We now formulate five
additional conditions on
\psi_{t}
for
t=0,\dots,b
, which allow us to show
a lower bound on the desired probability inductively. If
\psi_{t}
satisfies
these conditions we say that it is a
buffer-friendly partial embedding
.

(
BPE
0)

We have that
\{\psi_{t}(y_{1}),\dots,\psi_{t}(y_{t})\}\subseteq N_{G}(v)
,

(
BPE
0)

|\hat{U}_{t}(y_{k})|=(p\pm\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi_{t}^{*}(y_{k})}\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V(y_{k}))
for
k=t+1,\ldots,b
,

(
BPE
0)

|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(y_{k})|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi_{t}^{*}(y_{k})}\deg_{G}(v;V(y_{k}))
for
k=t+1,\ldots,b
,

(
BPE
0)

for unembedded
y_{k}
and
y_{\ell}
with

y_{k}y_{\ell}\in E(H)
we have
(\hat{U}_{t}(y_{k}),\hat{U}_{t}(y_{\ell}))
is

(\varepsilon_{\pi_{t}^{*}(y_{k}),\pi_{t}^{*}(y_{\ell})},d,p)
-regular in
G
,

(
BPE
0)

for unembedded
y_{k}
and
z
with

y_{k}z\in E(H)
the pair
(\hat{U}_{t}(y_{k}),U_{t}(z))
is

(\varepsilon_{\pi_{t}^{*}(y_{k}),\pi_{t}^{*}(z)},d,p)
-regular in
G
.

Observe that these conditions are all satisfied for
\psi_{0}
, i.e.
\psi_{0}
is a buffer-friendly partial embedding. Indeed,
(BPE 0)
is vacuously satisfied.
(BPE 0)
holds by
definition of
\hat{U}
and since no neighbours of any
y_{k}
are embedded.
(BPE 0)
holds by the definition of
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}
, the assumption on

\deg_{G}\big(v;V(x_{k})\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{0})\big)
of the lemma, and since no neighbours of any
y_{k}
are embedded. If
y_{k}
and
y_{\ell}
are adjacent in
H
then
xy_{k}y_{\ell}
is a triangle in
H
with
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\subseteq\tilde{X}_{i}
, so by
(G 0)
we have
(BPE 0)
. Finally, again since
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\subseteq\tilde{X}_{i}
, by
(G 0)
we have
(BPE 0)
.

For each
t=0,\dots,b-1
we let
P_{t}
be the set of
unfriendly
vertices

u
in
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(y_{t+1})
, that is, the vertices that are such that if
\psi_{t+1}(y_{t+1})=u
then
\psi_{t+1}
is not a buffer-friendly partial embedding.

We now show that, for any
t=1,\dots,b
, given a buffer-friendly partial embedding
\psi_{t-1}
, the probability that
\psi_{t}
is a buffer-friendly partial embedding is at least

d^{\Delta}p/10
. This clearly yields, by multiplying the conditional probabilities,
the lower bound of
(d^{\Delta}p/10)^{b}
claimed
in the statement of the lemma.
For the analysis it will suffice to show that only a tiny proportion of vertices
from
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
are in
B_{t-1}(y_{t})
or
P_{t-1}
.

So assume that
\psi_{t-1}
is a buffer-friendly partial embedding.
We first give some lower bounds on set sizes. Since
\psi_{t-1}
is a good partial embedding
we have for an unembedded vertex
z
that

|U^{\mathrm{q}}_{t-1}(z)|\geq\mu(p-\varepsilon p)^{\pi_{t-1}^{*}(z)}\big|V(z)\big|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(z)}n/r_{1}

(2.12)

where the first inequality is by
(GPE 0)

and the second because
|V(z)|\geq n/(\kappa r_{1})
and by choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
. The same lower bound also holds for
U_{t-1}(z),C_{t-1}(z)
et cetera since these sets are all by
(GPE 0)
and
(GPE 0)
at least as large.
Since in addition
\psi_{t-1}
is a buffer-friendly partial embedding, we
have for any
y_{k}
with
k\geq t
that

|\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{k})|\geq|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{k})|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{k})}\deg_{G}\big(v;V(y_{k})\big)\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{k})+1}n/r_{1}

(2.13)

where the second inequality is by
(BPE 0)
and the third is by
(G 0)
and choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
.
We also have for
k\geq t
, using
(BPE 0)
,
(BPE 0)
,
(GPE 0)
,
(G 0)
and the choice of
\varepsilon
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}
that

|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{k})|\geq\max\big(\tfrac{1}{4}d^{\Delta}p|U_{t-1}(y_{k})|\,,\,\tfrac{1}{4}d^{\Delta}|\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{k})|\big)\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{k})|\,.

(2.14)

We are next going to estimate the number of bad vertices in
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
.

Claim 2.32
.

|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})\cap B_{t-1}(y_{t})|\leq 30\Delta^{2}d^{-\Delta}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|
.

Proof.

We need to estimate the number of vertices
w
from
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})

which are bad with respect to
\psi_{t-1}
and
Q_{t-1}
. For that we will consider
all of the possible reasons.

First assume that there is some unembedded
neighbour
z\in V(H)\setminus Q_{t-1}
of
y_{t}
such
that the badness condition

\deg_{G}\big(w;A^{\mathrm{main}}(z)\big)<(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p|A^{\mathrm{main}}(z)|

(2.15)

holds for
w
and
z
. The pair

\big(\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{t}),U_{t-1}(z)\big)
is
\big(\varepsilon_{\pi_{t-1}^{*}(y_{t}),\pi_{t-1}^{*}(z)},d,p\big)
-regular
in
G
by
(BPE 0)
.
Because
z\not\in Q_{t-1}
we have that

\displaystyle|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(z)|

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(z)}p^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(z)}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(z)|

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(1-3\mu)(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(z)}p^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(z)}|V(z)|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}|U_{t-1}(z)|\,,

where the first inequality is by definition of
Q_{t-1}
in the statement of the lemma,
the second inequality is by
(GPE 0)
and the last by choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
and by
(GPE 0)
.
Therefore the badness condition (
2.15
) holds
for at most

\varepsilon_{\pi_{t-1}^{*}(y_{t}),\pi_{t-1}^{*}(z)}|\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{t})|\overset{\mbox{\tiny{\eqref{eq:buffer:relhat}}}}{\leq}4d^{-\Delta}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|

vertices

w
of
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
.
Since
y_{t}
may have at most
\Delta
unembedded neighbours,
in total there are at most
4\Delta d^{-\Delta}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|
vertices
w
of
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
such
that the badness condition (
2.15
) holds for some unembedded neighbour of
y_{t}
.

Next we need to estimate the number of vertices
w
from
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
such that

\psi_{t-1}\cup\{y_{t}\to w\}
is not a good partial embedding (i.e. does not satisfy properties
(GPE 0)
–
(GPE 0)
). First observe that since
w\in\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})\subseteq C_{t-1}(y_{t})\subseteq I_{y_{t}}
,

(GPE 0)
cannot fail.

Next we turn to
(GPE 0)
. It is sufficient
to consider unembedded neighbours
z
of
y_{t}
, so
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(z)\leq\Delta-1
. As the bound in (
2.12
) also holds for
U_{t-1}(z)
we have
|U_{t-1}(z)|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\Delta-1}n/r_{1}
.
Therefore, by the neighbourhood size property
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},B)
, all but at most
\varepsilon p^{B-1}n/r_{1}^{2}
vertices
w
from
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})

are such that
|U_{t-1}(z)\cap N_{\Gamma}(w)|=(1\pm\varepsilon)p|U_{t-1}(z)|=(p\pm\varepsilon p)^{\tau^{*}_{t-1}(z)+1}|V(z)|
, referring to
(GPE 0)
for
z
with respect to
\psi_{t-1}
.
Further we have
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t})\leq B-2
since
y_{t}
has at most
y_{1},\dots,y_{B-2}
as embedded neighbours (if
b=B
then by assumption
y_{b}y_{b-1}
is not an edge of
H
), so since
\varepsilon<(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}
we have

\varepsilon p^{B-1}n/r_{1}^{2}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{\eqref{eq:buffer:largehat}}}}{\leq}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|\,.

We can argue analogously for the other four conditions of
(GPE 0)
obtaining
in total that for
all but at most
5\Delta\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|
vertices
w
from
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
,

(GPE 0)
is satisfied with respect to
\psi_{t-1}\cup\{y_{t}\to w\}
.

Now we consider
(GPE 0)
. Let
z
be any unembedded neighbour of
y_{t}
. Because
z
is at distance at most
2
from
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}
, by
(H 0)

z
is not image restricted, so
I_{z}=V(z)
. Since

(GPE 0)
and
(GPE 0)
hold for
\psi_{t-1}
we have:

|C_{t-1}(z)|\geq(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(z)}|V(z)|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}|U_{t-1}(z)|.

By
(BPE 0)
,
(\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{t}),U_{t-1}(z))
is

(\varepsilon_{\pi_{t-1}^{*}(y_{t}),\pi_{t-1}^{*}(z)},d,p)
-regular in
G
.
So, there are at most

\varepsilon_{\pi_{t-1}^{*}(y_{t}),\pi_{t-1}^{*}(z)}|\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{t})|\overset{\mbox{\tiny{\eqref{eq:buffer:relhat}}}}{\leq}4d^{-\Delta}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|

vertices
w
in

\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{t})
such that
|C_{t-1}(z)\cap N_{G}(w)|<(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p|C_{t-1}(z)|
.
Similarly we argue for each of the other four conditions.
Thus, since
y_{t}
has at most
\Delta
unembedded neighbours, in total for all but at most

20\Delta d^{-\Delta}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|
vertices
w\in\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
we have that
(GPE 0)

is satisfied with respect to
\psi_{t-1}\cup\{y_{t}\to w\}
.

It remains to consider
(GPE 0)
. Again, we only need to consider
edges
zz^{\prime}\in E(H)
between unembedded vertices such that
z\in N_{H}(y_{t})
. Consider first the case that
z^{\prime}\not\in N_{H}(y_{t})
. In this case we have
\pi^{*}(z)\leq\Delta-2
,
\pi^{*}(z^{\prime})\leq\Delta-1
and
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t})\leq\Delta-2
(since both
x
and
z
are unembedded neighbours of
y_{t}
). Since
\Gamma
satisfies
property
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},B)
, using (
2.12
) and the fact that by
(GPE 0)
the pair
\big(U_{t-1}(z),U_{t-1}(z^{\prime})\big)

is
(\varepsilon_{\pi_{t-1}^{*}(z),\pi_{t-1}^{*}(z^{\prime})},d,p)
-regular in
G
, we see that there are at most

\varepsilon p^{\Delta-1}n/r_{1}^{2}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{\eqref{eq:buffer:largehat}}}}{\leq}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|

vertices
w\in\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
such that the pair

\big(N_{\Gamma}(w;U_{t-1}(z)),U_{t-1}(z^{\prime})\big)
is not

(\varepsilon_{\pi_{t-1}^{*}(z)+1,\pi_{t-1}^{*}(z^{\prime})},d,p)
-regular in
G
.

In the case that

z,z^{\prime}\in N_{H}(y_{t})
we have
\pi_{t-1}^{*}(z^{\prime})\leq\Delta-2
. We also have
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t})\leq B-3
. This requires a little explanation. Observe that
y_{t}
has unembedded neighbours
z
and
z^{\prime}
, so
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t})\leq\Delta-2
, and if
B=\Delta+1
then we are done. If
B=\Delta
, then observe that the embedded neighbours of
y_{t}
are contained in
\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{t-1}\}
. It follows that if
t\leq\Delta-2
, we have
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t})\leq\Delta-3
as desired. If
t=\Delta
, then
x
,
z
and
z^{\prime}
are distinct (since the only unembedded neighbour of
x
is
y_{t}
) and we again have
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t})\leq\Delta-3
. It remains to consider the case
t=\Delta-1
. Again we are done if
x
,
z
and
z^{\prime}
are distinct. If, however, (without loss of generality) we have
x=z
, then
z^{\prime}
is an unembedded neighbour of
x
which is not
y_{t}
; in other words, we have
z^{\prime}=y_{\Delta}
. But we assumed that
y_{\Delta-1}y_{\Delta}
is not an edge of
H
, contradicting the assumption
y_{t}z^{\prime}\in H
. We have thus justified
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t})\leq B-3
, so by
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},B)
we conclude that there are at most

\varepsilon p^{B-2}n/r_{1}^{2}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{\eqref{eq:buffer:largehat}}}}{\leq}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|

vertices
w\in\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})

such that the pair
\big(N_{\Gamma}(w;U_{t-1}(z)),N_{\Gamma}(w;U_{t-1}(z^{\prime}))\big)
is not

(\varepsilon_{\pi_{t-1}^{*}(z)+1,\pi_{t-1}^{*}(z^{\prime})+1},d,p)
-regular. So, in total there are at
most
\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|
vertices
w\in\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
such
that
(GPE 0)
is not satisfied with respect to

\psi_{t-1}\cup\{y_{t}\to w\}
.

Summing up, we conclude that
|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})\cap B_{t-1}(y_{t})|\leq 4\Delta d^{-\Delta}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|+5\Delta\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|+20\Delta d^{-\Delta}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|+\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|
as required.

∎

Next we will estimate the number of unfriendly vertices in
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
.

Claim 2.33
.

|P_{t-1}|\leq 10\Delta^{2}d^{-\Delta}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|
.

Proof.

Observe that by definition, since
\psi_{t-1}
is a buffer-friendly partial embedding, for each
w\in\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
the embedding
\psi_{t-1}\cup\{y_{t}\to w\}
has
(BPE 0)
. For
t=b
the remaining properties
(BPE 0)
–
(BPE 0)
are trivial, so we from now on assume
t\leq b-1
.

For
(BPE 0)
, let
k\in\{t+1,\ldots,b\}
be such that
y_{t}y_{k}\in E(H)
. Observe that
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t})\leq t-1\leq b-2\leq B-2
.
Therefore, by
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},B)
, by (
2.13
) and (
2.14
), for all but at most
\varepsilon p^{B-1}n/r_{1}^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|
vertices
w
we have

|N_{\Gamma}(w;\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{k}))|=(1\pm\varepsilon^{\prime})p|\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{k})|
.

To estimate the number of vertices
w
that do not preserve
(BPE 0)
, we only need to consider those

y_{k}
with
k\geq t+1
such that
y_{t}y_{k}\in E(H)
. We
use that
\big(\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{t}),\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{k})\big)
is
(\varepsilon^{\prime},d,p)
-regular in
G
by
(BPE 0)
.
Further, by (
2.14
) we have
|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{t})|
, so there are at most

\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{t})|\overset{\mbox{\tiny{\eqref{eq:buffer:relhat}}}}{\leq}4d^{-\Delta}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|

vertices
w
in

\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
such that
\deg_{G}(w;\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{k}))<(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{k})|
.

Next we consider
(BPE 0)
. Let
k\in\{t+1,\dots,b\}
,
and
z\in V(H)
be such that
y_{k}z\in E(H)
. By
(BPE 0)

the pair
\big(\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{k}),U_{t-1}(z)\big)
is
(\varepsilon_{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{k}),\pi^{*}_{t-1}(z)},d,p)
-regular in
G
.
There are three cases to consider:
y_{t}y_{k}\in E(H)
,
y_{t}z\in E(H)
, and both.

In the first case (i.e.
y_{t}y_{k}\in E(H)
,
y_{t}z\not\in E(H)
), we have
t<B-1
because
y_{B-1}y_{B}
is not an edge of
H
,
so we conclude
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t}),\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{k})\leq t-1\leq B-3
, and
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(z)\leq\Delta-1
.
By (
2.12
) and (
2.13
),
and
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},B)
, we see that for all vertices
w
of
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
but at most

\varepsilon p^{B-1}n/r_{1}^{2}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{\eqref{eq:buffer:largehat}}}}{\leq}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|\,,

the pair
\big(N_{\Gamma}(w;\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{k})),U_{t-1}(z)\big)
is
(\varepsilon_{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{k})+1,\pi^{*}_{t-1}(z)},d,p)
-regular in
G
.

In the second case (i.e.
y_{t}y_{k}\not\in E(H)
,
y_{t}z\in E(H)
), we have

\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t}),\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{k})\leq t-1\leq B-2
, and
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(z)\leq t-1\leq\Delta-2
and similarly, by
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},B)
, there are not more than
\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|
vertices
w
of
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
for which the pair

\big(\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{k}),N_{\Gamma}(w;U_{t-1}(z))\big)
is not
(\varepsilon_{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{k}),\pi^{*}_{t-1}(z)+1},d,p)
-regular in
G
.

In the final case (i.e.
y_{t}y_{k}\in E(H)
,
y_{t}z\in E(H)
), again we have
t<B-1
, hence
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t}),\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{k})\leq B-3
, and
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(z)\leq\Delta-2
, and again all but at most
\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|
vertices
w
of
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
are such that
\big(N_{\Gamma}(w;\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{k})),N_{\Gamma}(w;U_{t-1}(z))\big)
is
(\varepsilon_{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{k})+1,\pi^{*}_{t-1}(z)+1},d,p)
-regular in
G
.

In total, we see that for all but at most
\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|
vertices
w
of
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
, the partial embedding
\psi_{t-1}\cup\{y_{t}\to w\}
has
(BPE 0)
.

Finally, we handle
(BPE 0)
. Let
k,\ell\in\{t+1,\dots,b\}
be such that

y_{k}y_{\ell}\in E(H)
. We have
\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t}),\pi_{t-1}^{*}(y_{k}),\pi_{t-1}^{*}(y_{\ell})\leq t-1\leq B-3
. By
(BPE 0)
, the pair
\big(\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{k}),\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{\ell})\big)
is

(\varepsilon_{\pi_{t-1}^{*}(y_{k}),\pi_{t-1}^{*}(y_{\ell})},d,p)
-regular in
G
. Without loss of generality we may assume
y_{t}y_{k}\in E(H)
, and again there are two cases to consider depending on whether
y_{t}y_{\ell}\in E(H)
or not. As before, using (
2.13
), by
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},B)
, at most

\varepsilon p^{B-2}n/r_{1}^{2}\overset{\mbox{\tiny{\eqref{eq:buffer:largehat}}}}{\leq}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|

vertices
w\in\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
are such that

\big(N_{\Gamma}(w;\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{k})),N_{\Gamma}(w;\hat{U}_{t-1}(y_{\ell}))\big)
is not

(\varepsilon_{\pi_{t-1}^{*}(y_{k})+1,\pi_{t-1}^{*}(y_{\ell})+1},d,p)
-regular in
G
. The other case follows similarly, and we conclude that for all but at most
\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|
vertices
w
of
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
, the partial embedding
\psi_{t-1}\cup\{y_{t}\to w\}
has
(BPE 0)
.

In total we get
|P_{t-1}|\leq\Delta\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|+4\Delta d^{-\Delta}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|+\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|+\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|

as claimed.

∎

We conclude from Claim
2.32
and Claim
2.33
that
the number of vertices
w\in\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})
such
that
w
is bad for
y_{t}
with respect to
\psi_{t-1}
and
Q_{t-1}
or

w\in P_{t-1}
is at most
40\Delta^{2}d^{-\Delta}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|
.

Now we can estimate the probability that
\psi_{t}
is a buffer-friendly partial embedding, conditioning on the history and on
\psi_{t-1}
being a buffer-friendly partial embedding. This event occurs if
y_{t}
is embedded to a member of
\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})\setminus\big(B_{t-1}(y_{t})\cup P_{t-1}\big)
. The number of such vertices is at least

\big|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})\big|-40\Delta^{2}d^{-\Delta}\varepsilon^{\prime}|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\big|\hat{A}^{\mathrm{main}}_{t-1}(y_{t})\big|\overset{\mbox{\tiny{\eqref{eq:buffer:largehat}}}}{\geq}\tfrac{1}{4}(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t})+1}|V(y_{t})|\,,

while
y_{t}
is embedded into a set of size at most
U_{t-1}(y_{t})
, which by
(GPE 0)
has size at most
(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t})}|V(y_{t})|
. We see that the desired conditional probability is at least

\frac{\tfrac{1}{4}(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t})+1}|V(y_{t})|}{(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}_{t-1}(y_{t})}|V(y_{t})|}\geq\frac{d^{\Delta}p}{10}\,.

The statement of the lemma follows since the conditional probabilities multiply.∎

Chapter 3
Proof of the blow-up lemma for random graphs

In this chapter we provide the proof of Lemma
1.21
. This
proof relies on four main lemmas: The RGA Lemma (Lemma
3.1
), the
queue embedding lemma (Lemma
3.2
), the buffer defect lemma
(Lemma
3.3
, and the buffer embedding lemma
(Lemma
3.4
). These lemmas and the actual proof of
Lemma
1.21
are provided in Section
3.1
. The RGA
Lemma is then proved in Section
3.2
, the queue embedding lemma in
Section
3.3
, and the buffer defect lemma in
Section
3.4
. The proof of Lemma
3.4
is
short and thus given already in Section
3.1
.

3.1.
Main lemmas and the proof of the blow-up lemma

We divide the proof of Lemma
1.21
into four lemmas, which
correspond to our four different embedding stages described in the proof
overview in Section
1.4
. At the end of this section we
show how these four lemmas together with
Lemma
2.22
(good partitions lemma) imply
Lemma
1.21
.

The first of our lemmas encapsulates the outcome after applying the
random greedy algorithm (RGA) which tries to embed
X^{\mathrm{main}}

into
V^{\mathrm{main}}
. How this RGA operates is explained in the proof of
this lemma in Section
3.2
. The lemma claims the existence of a
good partial embedding with certain deterministic properties which we
require and which the RGA with high probability produces. Recall that the
RGA constructs the queues
X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
.

Lemma 3.1

(RGA lemma)
.

We assume the General Setup. Suppose that
\Gamma
has

\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
and

\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},\Delta)
. Then there is a good
partial embedding
\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
of
H
into
G
with the following
properties. For each
i
, let
X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}:=X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\setminus\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}})
.
Then the
following hold for each
i
. Let
b
be such that
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a degree-
b

buffer.

(
RGA
0)

All neighbours of all buffer vertices
are embedded by
\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
.

(
RGA
0)

Every vertex in
X_{i}\cap\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}})
is
embedded to
V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
by
\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
.

(
RGA
0)

We have
|X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}|\leq 2\varrho|X_{i}|
.

(
RGA
0)

For every set
W\subseteq V_{i}
of size at least

\varrho|V_{i}|
, there are at most
\varrho|X_{i}|

vertices in
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
with fewer than
(dp)^{b}|W|/2
candidates in
W
.

(
RGA
0)

If
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is not a clique buffer, then every vertex in
V_{i}
is a
candidate for at least
\mu\big(d^{\Delta}p/100\big)^{b}|X_{i}|
vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
.

We shall next extend the good partial embedding provided by the RGA lemma
and embed the queue vertices into the sets
V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
. That this is possible
is stated by the following lemma. As mentioned in the proof overview, the
proof of this lemma relies on
\Gamma
not having a dense spot, which the
congestion condition
\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
guarantees.

Lemma 3.2

(Queue embedding lemma)
.

We assume the General Setup. Suppose that
\Gamma
has
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
,
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},\Delta)
and
\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
.
Let
\psi
be a good partial embedding whose image is disjoint from the sets

V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
, and suppose that for each
i
we have a set
X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}\subseteq X_{i}
of
size at most
2\varrho|X_{i}|
. Then there is a good partial embedding
\psi_{\mathrm{q}}

extending
\psi
such that

\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{\mathrm{q}})\setminus\mathrm{Dom}(\psi)=X^{\mathrm{q}}\text{ and }\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{\mathrm{q}})\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi)\subseteq V^{\mathrm{q}}\,.

Note that in the RGA lemma, Lemma
3.1
, we do not have that all vertices
v
in
V_{i}
are candidates for many
vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
if
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a clique buffer (and in fact our RGA
may fail to produce this property). In this case we say that
v
has
a
buffer defect
for
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
.
It follows from
(RGA 0)
, however, that only at most
\varrho|V_{i}|

vertices of
V_{i}
can have a buffer defect for
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
.
Our next lemma, the buffer defect lemma, deals with fixing these buffer defects.

In order to apply this lemma we need to alter the embedding strategy
described so far slightly.
Indeed, before we begin the embedding, we will reserve some copies of

K_{\Delta+1}
in
H
, each of which has a vertex in
\tilde{X}_{i}
. We call these the

reserved cliques
and denote the family of reserved cliques for
\tilde{X}_{i}

by
\mathcal{K}_{i}
. We put the vertices
of all reserved cliques into the set
X^{\mathrm{c}}
, which is disjoint from
X^{\mathrm{main}}
and

X^{\mathrm{buf}}
. Then we apply the RGA lemma and the queue embedding lemma to
obtain a good partial embedding of all of
H
but the reserved
cliques
X^{\mathrm{c}}
and the buffer vertices
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
.
Next, we use the buffer defect lemma to embed the reserved
cliques (and, for technical reasons, also
some vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
) on vertices with buffer defects, to obtain a good partial embedding
\psi_{\mathrm{good}}
in which only
the vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
remain unembedded and without any buffer defects
left.
Note that in the following lemma
A^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)=A(x)\cap V^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)=C(x)\cap V^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
.

Lemma 3.3

(Buffer defect lemma)
.

We assume the General Setup. Suppose that
\Gamma
has

\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
,
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},\Delta)
and

\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
. Let
\psi
be a good partial embedding whose
image is disjoint from the
sets
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
and whose domain contains
N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
. Suppose that for each
i
we are given a family
\mathcal{K}_{i}
of
reserved cliques
K_{\Delta+1}
, not embedded by
\psi
, such that for each
K\in\mathcal{K}_{i}
and
x,y\in K
we have
x\in X_{j}
and

y\in X_{k}
with
jk\in R^{\prime}
, and that either

(

)

X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a degree-
b
non-clique buffer with
\mathcal{K}_{i}=\emptyset
,
and all vertices in
V_{i}
are candidate for at least
\mu\big(d^{\Delta}p/100\big)^{b}|X_{i}|

vertices in
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, or

(

)

X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a clique buffer with
|\mathcal{K}_{i}|=2\varrho|X_{i}|
, and
all but at most
\varrho|V_{i}|
vertices of

V_{i}
are candidate for at least
\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|X_{i}|/2
vertices in

X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
.

Let
X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
be the set of vertices
of
X_{i}
contained in some reserved clique from
\mathcal{K}_{i}
and assume that

|X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}|\leq 2\kappa(\Delta_{R^{\prime}}+1)\varrho|X_{i}|
.

Then there is a good partial
embedding
\psi_{\mathrm{good}}
extending
\psi
such that for each
i
the
following hold. Let
b
be such that
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a degree-
b

buffer.

(
BD
0)

X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}\subseteq\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{\mathrm{good}})
.

(
BD
0)

For each
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{\mathrm{good}})
we have

|A^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)|\geq\mu(dp)^{b}|V_{i}|/4
.

(
BD
0)

Each
v\in V_{i}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{\mathrm{good}})
is candidate
for at least
\mu\big(d^{\Delta}p/100\big)^{b}|X_{i}|
of the vertices
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{\mathrm{good}})
.

It remains to embed the buffer vertices, which is performed with the help
of the following lemma.

Lemma 3.4

(Buffer embedding lemma)
.

We assume the General Setup. Suppose that
\Gamma
has

\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
. Suppose that
\psi
is a good partial embedding,
in which all the vertices
N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
are embedded, and that for each
i

we have a subset
X^{\prime}_{i}
of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, and a subset
V^{\prime}_{i}
of

V_{i}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
with
|X^{\prime}_{i}|=|V^{\prime}_{i}|
. Suppose
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a
degree-
b
buffer, and for some
\delta>0
and
\varrho\leq\frac{\delta\mu d^{b}}{100\kappa}
we have

(
BUF
0)

for each
x\in X^{\prime}_{i}
we have

|C(x)\cap V^{\prime}_{i}|\geq\mu(dp)^{b}|V_{i}|/4
,

(
BUF
0)

for each
W\subseteq V^{\prime}_{i}
with
|W|\geq\varrho|V_{i}|
, there are at most
\varrho|X_{i}|
vertices in
X^{\prime}_{i}
which do not have a candidate in
W
, and

(
BUF
0)

each
v\in V^{\prime}_{i}
is a candidate
for at least
\delta p^{b}|X_{i}|
vertices in
X^{\prime}_{i}
.

Then there is a good partial embedding
\psi^{\prime}
extending
\psi
such that

\mathrm{Dom}(\psi^{\prime})=\mathrm{Dom}(\psi)\cup\bigcup_{i}X^{\prime}_{i}
and
\mathrm{Im}(\psi^{\prime})=\mathrm{Im}(\psi)\cup\bigcup_{i}V^{\prime}_{i}
.

The proof of this lemma mainly consists of a straightforward check of
Hall’s condition and we provide it right away.
We remark that we will use this lemma again in the proof of the degenerate
graphs blow-up lemma, which
is why we do not give an explicit constant in place of
\delta
in
(BUF 0)
.

Proof of Lemma
3.4
.

Since
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
is independent in H (and the sets
V^{\prime}_{i}
are disjoint in
G
), we can embed the vertices of
X^{\prime}_{i}

for each
i
in succession, without affecting the vertices in any
other
X^{\prime}_{i}
. So we fix
i
and will show that
X^{\prime}_{i}
can be embedded into
V^{\prime}_{i}
.

Let
Y\subseteq X^{\prime}_{i}
be non-empty, and let
W
be the set of vertices in

V^{\prime}_{i}
which are a candidate for some member of
Y
. We wish to verify
Hall’s condition, i.e. show that
|W|\geq|Y|
. We separate three cases.

First, suppose
0<|Y|\leq\varrho|X_{i}|
. If
|W|<|Y|
, then we can take a
subset
Y^{\prime}
of
Y
of size
|W|
. Recall that the candidate graph
between
Y^{\prime}
and
W
is the bipartite graph with edges
yw
for
y\in Y^{\prime}

and
w\in W
with
w\in C(y)
, and that this is a subgraph of the
underlying restriction graph between
Y^{\prime}
and
W
with edges
yw
with

w\in U(y)
. Observe, moreover, that this underlying restriction graph is
isomorphic to the congestion graph
\mathrm{CG}(\Gamma,W,\mathcal{F})
with

\mathcal{F}=\big\{\psi\big(N_{H}(y)\big)\colon y\in Y^{\prime}\big\}
, which has edges

wF
with
w\in N^{*}_{\Gamma}(F)
. Now, since by
(BUF 0)
each vertex
in
Y^{\prime}
has at least
\mu(dp)^{b}|V_{i}|/4
candidates in
V^{\prime}_{i}
, which must
lie in
W
, the number of edges in the candidate graph between
Y^{\prime}
and

W
is at least

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\mu(dp)^{b}|V_{i}||Y^{\prime}|

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{8}\mu(dp)^{b}|V_{i}||Y^{\prime}|+\tfrac{1}{8}\mu(dp)^{b}|V_{i}||Y^{\prime}|

\displaystyle>\frac{\mu d^{b}}{8\varrho}p^{b}|Y^{\prime}||W|+\frac{\mu d^{b}}{8\kappa r_{1}}p^{b}n|Y^{\prime}|

\displaystyle>7p^{b}|Y^{\prime}||W|+\varrho p^{b}n|Y^{\prime}|/r_{1}\,,

where the second line comes from substituting
|V_{i}|=|X_{i}|>|Y^{\prime}|/\varrho=|W|/\varrho

and using
|V_{i}|\geq n/(\kappa r_{1})
, and
the third follows from the bound on
\varrho
.
Hence
e(\mathrm{CG}(\Gamma,W,\mathcal{F}))>7p^{b}|Y^{\prime}||W|+\varrho p^{b}n|Y^{\prime}|/r_{1}
, in
contradiction to
\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
. We conclude that
|W|\geq|Y|
in this
case.

Second, if
\varrho|X_{i}|<|Y|\leq|X^{\prime}_{i}|-\varrho|X_{i}|=|V_{i}^{\prime}|-\varrho|V_{i}|
and
|W|<|Y|
, then

|V_{i}^{\prime}\setminus W|>\varrho|V_{i}|
, so by
(BUF 0)
there are at most

\varrho|X_{i}|
vertices of
X^{\prime}_{i}
which do not have candidates in
V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus W
. In particular there is a vertex of
Y
with
candidates in
V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus W
, in contradiction to the definition of
W
. We
conclude that
|W|\geq|Y|
in this case as well.

Finally, suppose
|Y|>|X^{\prime}_{i}|-\varrho|X_{i}|=|V_{i}^{\prime}|-\varrho|V_{i}|
. The vertices
V_{i}^{\prime}\setminus W

are candidates only for vertices in
X^{\prime}_{i}\setminus Y
, and each vertex in

V_{i}^{\prime}\setminus W
is a candidate for at least
\delta p^{b}|X_{i}|
vertices in

X_{i}^{\prime}\setminus Y
by
(BUF 0)
. If
|W|<|Y|
, then
|V_{i}^{\prime}\setminus W|>|X^{\prime}_{i}\setminus Y|
, and we can take a set
\tilde{W}\subseteq V_{i}^{\prime}\setminus W
of size
|X^{\prime}_{i}\setminus Y|
.
Now the number of edges in the candidate graph
between
\tilde{W}
and
X^{\prime}_{i}\setminus Y
is at least

\displaystyle\delta p^{b}|X_{i}||\tilde{W}|

\displaystyle>\tfrac{1}{2}\delta p^{b}\big(\tfrac{1}{\varrho}|X^{\prime}_{i}\setminus Y||\tilde{W}|+\tfrac{n}{\kappa r_{1}}|X^{\prime}_{i}\setminus Y|\big)

\displaystyle>7p^{b}|X^{\prime}_{i}\setminus Y||\tilde{W}|+\varrho p^{b}n|X^{\prime}_{i}\setminus Y|/r_{1}

by essentially the same calculation as in the first case, using

|X_{i}|=|V_{i}|\geq n/(\kappa r_{1})
.
Taking
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}=\big\{\psi\big(N_{H}(x)\big)\colon x\in X^{\prime}_{i}\setminus Y\big\}
,
the number of edges in the corresponding congestion graph

\mathrm{CG}(\Gamma,\tilde{W},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
is also at least this quantity, in contradiction to

\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
.

This completes the verification of Hall’s condition, so there is a partial
embedding
\psi^{\prime}
extending
\psi
with
\mathrm{Dom}(\psi^{\prime})=\mathrm{Dom}(\psi)\cup X^{\prime}_{i}

in which the vertices
X^{\prime}_{i}
are embedded to
V^{\prime}_{i}
. Since
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
is independent and all the vertices
N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
are
embedded in
\psi
, it is trivially the case that
\psi^{\prime}
is a good partial embedding.
∎

We are now ready to prove Lemma
1.21
. Summarising, the proof will
go as follows. We will apply Lemma
2.22
to find partitions
of
G
and
H
, and graphs
R
and
R^{\prime}
, satisfying the General Setup. We
choose, for each
i
such that
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a clique buffer a set of
reserved cliques with vertices
X^{\mathrm{c}}
. We can then apply the RGA lemma, the
queue embedding lemma, the buffer defect lemma, and the buffer embedding
lemma, in this order.

Proof of Lemma
1.21
.

First we choose constants as follows. Given
\Delta
,
\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{BL}}
,

\Delta_{J}
integers,
\alpha^{\mathrm{BL}}
,

and

, and

, we

,
\Delta_{R^{\prime}}=8(\Delta+\Delta_{J})^{10}\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{BL}}
,

\alpha=\tfrac{1}{2}\alpha^{\mathrm{BL}}
,

and

.
We now choose

,

and

,

,

,

,

. For convenience we provide here sufficient choices:

\mu<\frac{d^{\Delta}}{1320\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}},\text{ and }\varrho\leq\frac{\mu^{2}d^{\Delta^{2}+1}}{250^{\Delta+1}\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}},\text{ and }\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\frac{\mu\zeta\varrho d^{\Delta}}{32^{\Delta+2}2^{4/\varrho}\kappa\Delta^{2}\Delta_{R^{\prime}}}\,.

Now for input

,

and
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, Lemma

returns constants

and

. Here we additionally require that

.

.
Now Lemma
1.21
returns
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{BL}}
and

. Given

. We choose

with input

,

,

and

.

Given

Given a graph

) a graph

-balanced size-compatible partitions

and

of

and

, together with a family

Now for each

such that

and a set

\Delta_{R^{\prime}}2\varrho\kappa|X_{i}|<\tfrac{1}{4\Delta+8}\alpha|X_{i}|
by choice of

. We conclude the following properties for these reserved cliques.

(
RSC
0)

The sets
\{\mathcal{K}_{i}\}_{i\in[r]}
are pairwise disjoint, and if

is a clique buffer, then

contains

, otherwise it

(
RSC
0)

For each

,
K\in\mathcal{K}_{i}
and

x\in X_{j}
and

where

.

(
RSC
0)

For each

, the set

of vertices

contained in some reserved clique satisfies
|X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}|\leq 2\kappa(\Delta_{R^{\prime}}+1)\varrho|X_{i}|
.

Notice that
(RSC 0)
holds by
(H 0)
, since the

go along the edges of

R^{\prime}
by the definition of an

-buffer.

We let

. We now begin the embedding of

into
G
.
By Lemma
3.1
, there is a good partial embedding
\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
with

(RGA 0)
–
(RGA 0)
. Letting

|X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}|\leq 2\varrho|X_{i}|
for each

, and by

, so the conditions of

\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
into

3.2
we obtain a good partial embedding

extending

\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
whose domain is

and whose image is contained in

. By
(RGA 0)
all neighbours of all buffer
vertices are in

, and therefore the candidate sets of all

as with respect to

. In particular

satisfies
(RGA 0)

and
(RGA 0)
.

We now verify the conditions for Lemma
3.3
. Since
\psi_{\mathrm{q}}

extends
\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
and the vertices
\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{\mathrm{q}})\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}})
are

V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
it follows by
(RGA 0)
that

\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{\mathrm{q}})
is disjoint from the sets
\bigcup_{i}V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
and
\bigcup_{i}V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. By
(RGA 0)
, if
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a degree-
b
buffer for some

but
is not a clique buffer, then all vertices in
V_{i}
are candidate for at least

\mu(d^{\Delta}p/100)^{b}|X_{i}|
vertices in
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
and
\mathcal{K}_{i}
is empty by
(RSC 0)
.

If on the other hand

is a clique buffer, then
|\mathcal{K}_{i}|=2\varrho|X_{i}|
. Now let

W
be the set of vertices in
V_{i}
which are candidates for fewer than

\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|X_{i}|/2
vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. We will show that
|W|<\varrho|V_{i}|
.
If
|W|\geq\varrho|V_{i}|
, then by
(RGA 0)
there are at most

\varrho|X_{i}|
vertices in
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
with fewer than
(dp)^{\Delta}|W|/2
candidates in
W
. It follows that the average over
v\in W
of the number of
vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
for which

is a candidate, is at least

\frac{1}{|W|}\big(|X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}|-\varrho|X_{i}|\big)\frac{(dp)^{\Delta}|W|}{2}>\frac{1}{2}\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|X_{i}|,

where we used the facts
|X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}|=4\mu|X_{i}|
and
\varrho<\mu
in the inequality.
Since at least one

attains at least the average, we have a
contradiction to the definition of
W
.

The
conditions of Lemma
3.3
are thus satisfied, and by applying it
we obtain a good partial embedding
\psi_{\mathrm{good}}
. Since
\psi_{\mathrm{good}}
extends

\psi_{\mathrm{q}}
and all sets

are contained in

by

, we conclude that the only vertices remaining

.

Finally, for
i\in[r]
, let

and let

be a degree-
b
buffer for some

. Let

. Because

we have

|X^{\prime}_{i}|=|V^{\prime}_{i}|
. Because
\psi_{\mathrm{good}}
satisfies
(BD 0)
, we
have
(BUF 0)
. Because
\psi_{\mathrm{good}}
satisfies
(RGA 0)
, in
particular we have
(BUF 0)
. Finally, because
\psi_{\mathrm{good}}

satisfies
(BD 0)
we have
(BUF 0)
with

\delta=\mu(d^{\Delta}/100)^{b}
. Thus by Lemma
3.4
there
is an embedding
\psi^{\prime}
extending

which embeds

into

.
∎

3.2.
Proof of the random graphs RGA lemma

In this section we describe the random greedy algorithm (RGA) and prove
that it produces a partial embedding which satisfies the assertions of
the RGA lemma, Lemma
3.1
, with high probability. This is Algorithm
1
. It embeds vertices
x
of
H

sequentially, following an order
\tau
given by Lemma
2.28
(a good vertex order for an RGA). In
doing so, it builds up a sequence
\psi_{0},\dots
of good partial
embeddings, and a
queue
of vertices which it will not embed; we
let
Q_{t}
be the queue at time
t
(i.e. corresponding to
\psi_{t}
).
Recall that by
A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)
we mean the set
A^{\mathrm{main}}(x)
with reference
to the partial embedding
\psi_{t}
. We let
B_{t}(x)
denote the set of bad
vertices (defined in Section
2.3.6
) with respect
to
\psi_{t}
and
Q_{t}
. As mentioned in the proof overview
(Section
1.4
), to create
\psi_{t+1}
from
\psi_{t}

we embed some vertex
x
uniformly at random into the set

A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)\setminus B_{t}(x)
, the set of available candidate vertices in

V^{\mathrm{main}}
minus the bad vertices; and we add
y
to the queue if
the set
A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(y)\setminus B_{t}(y)
gets small.

Note that exactly
t
vertices are embedded in
\psi_{t}
, though these
vertices are not necessarily the first
t
vertices of
\tau
because
vertices in
Q_{t}
are skipped. The queue set
Q_{t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}}
at the time

t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}
when the RGA terminates will then form the queue
X^{\mathrm{q}}
.

Input :

G\subseteq\Gamma
and
H
with partitions satisfying the General Setup; an ordering
\tau
of
X^{\mathrm{main}}

t:=0
;

\psi_{0}:=\emptyset
;

Q_{0}:=\{x\in V(H):|I_{x}|<\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon)^{|J_{x}|}p^{|J_{x}|}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(x)|\}
;

repeat

let
x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus(\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{t})\cup Q_{t})
be the next vertex in
the order
\tau
;

choose
v\in A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)\setminus B_{t}(x)
uniformly at random ;

\psi_{t+1}:=\psi_{t}\cup\{x\to v\}
;

Q_{t+1}:=Q_{t}
;

forall

y\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{t+1})

do

if

(\big|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t+1}(y)\big|<\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{*}_{t+1}(y)}p^{\pi^{*}_{t+1}(y)}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(y)|)

then

Q_{t+1}:=Q_{t+1}\cup\{y\}
;

end if

end forall

t:=t+1
;

until

\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{t})\cup Q_{t}=X^{\mathrm{main}}

;

t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}:=t\;

Algorithm 1

Random greedy algorithm for random graphs

The proof that Algorithm
1
a.a.s. produces a good partial
embedding with the properties required in Lemma
3.1
is now
quite straightforward: most of the work is to check that the conditions
of the various lemmas on RGAs given in Section
2.4
are
met. The critical point is to show that certain invariants (see
Claim
3.5
below) are maintained.

Proof of Lemma
3.1
.

We require

\varrho<\mu\leq\frac{1}{100\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}}\,,\quad\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\frac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}\varrho 2^{-4/\varrho}}{1000\kappa\Delta^{2}4^{\Delta}\Delta_{R^{\prime}}}\,,\quad\varepsilon\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{\kappa d}

and

p\geq 10r_{1}\varepsilon^{-1}\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/\Delta}\,,\quad n>\kappa r_{1}^{2}\,.

First, we apply Lemma
2.28
to obtain an ordering
\tau
of the
vertices of
H
with properties
( )
‣
2.28
–
( )
‣
2.28
of this lemma.
With this ordering
\tau
we run the random greedy algorithm, Algorithm
1
, and in the following we show
that a.a.s. it produces a good partial embedding
\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}=\psi_{t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}}
satisfying
properties
(RGA 0)
–
(RGA 0)
of Lemma
3.1
.

We first show that some invariants are maintained (deterministically)
throughout the algorithm. Recall that
\pi^{*}_{t}(x):=\pi_{t}(x)+|J_{x}|
, where

\pi_{t}(x)
denotes the number of the embedded neighbours of
x
at time

t
.

Claim 3.5
.

The following hold at each time
t
in Algorithm
1
.

(
INV
0)

\psi_{t}
is a good partial embedding.

(
INV
0)

For each
x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{t})
, either
x\in Q_{t}
or we have

|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}p^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(x)|\,.

(
INV
0)

When we embed
x
to create
\psi_{t+1}
, we do so
uniformly at random into a set of size at least
\frac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|V(x)|
.

We remark that the bound given in
(INV 0)
is not sharp, but we
give this relaxed bound in order to re-use the same invariants in the
proofs of our other two blow-up lemmas.

Proof of Claim
3.5
.

The invariant
(INV 0)
is maintained by definition of
A_{t}(x)
and

B_{t}(x)
. Observe that at the end of each iteration of the repeat loop
of Algorithm
1
the
queue is updated by adding precisely any vertices which would
fail
(INV 0)
, so that this invariant also holds.
Finally, by choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
and by
(INV 0)
, if the vertex
x

is embedded to create
\psi_{t+1}
, then we have

|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)|\geq\tfrac{1}{4}\mu(dp)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|V(x)|
. Now, if all
neighbours of
x
are embedded under
\psi_{t}
then by Lemma
2.27
( )
‣
2.27

we have
(INV 0)
, while otherwise we apply
Lemma
2.27
( )
‣
2.27
with
D=\Delta
, for which the
required assumptions on
\Gamma
hold, to bound
|B_{t}(x)|
. We conclude that
(INV 0)
holds.

∎

We now begin to verify the conclusions of Lemma
3.1
. Property
(RGA 0)
, stating that all vertices in
X_{i}\cap\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}})
are embedded to
V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
, holds by our RGA.

Claim 3.6
.

Property
(RGA 0)
holds. Moreover,
let
x
be a buffer vertex and
y
its neighbour whose embedding creates
\psi_{t+1}
, then

\big|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(y)\big|\geq\tfrac{2}{3}(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t}}|V(y)|\,.

(3.1)

Proof.

We require
\mu\leq 1/(100\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}})
.
To show
(RGA 0)
, i.e., that all
neighbours of buffer vertices are in
\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}})
, it is sufficient to
prove that none of the neighbours of buffer vertices are in
Q_{t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}}
.
However, this is clear. Indeed, suppose that
y_{1},\dots,y_{b}
with
b\leq\Delta
are the neighbours of a buffer vertex
x
appearing in this order
in
\tau
. Suppose the embedding of
y_{1}
creates
\psi_{t_{1}+1}
. By
(H 0)
, buffer vertices are at distance at least five in
H
, hence,
the neighbours of the vertices have distance at least three, and by
(H 0)
they are not image restricted. Therefore,
at time
t_{1}
the available candidate set of each
y_{j}
is

A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t_{1}}(y_{j})=V^{\mathrm{main}}(y_{j})\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t_{1}})
. The size of

A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t_{1}}(y_{j})
is by
(H 0)
,
(G 0)
and choice of
\mu
at least

|V^{\mathrm{main}}(y_{j})|-4\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}\mu|V(y_{j})|=(1-3\mu-4\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}\mu)|V(y_{j})|\geq\tfrac{2}{3}|V(y_{j})|\,.

It follows that
y_{j}
is not added to
Q_{t}
for any
t\leq t_{1}
. Now the
vertices
y_{1},\ldots,y_{b}
are embedded consecutively by
Lemma
2.28
( )
‣
2.28
, and since they are neighbours of
x
,
by
(H 0)
, into distinct clusters of
G
.
By Definition
2.26
(of bad vertices with respect to
\psi
and
Q
),
for each time
t^{\prime}
with
t_{1}\leq t^{\prime}\leq t
, where
y_{j}
is embedded to create
\psi_{t+1}
, we have

|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t^{\prime}}(y_{j})|\geq\tfrac{2}{3}(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t^{\prime}}(y_{j})}|V(y_{j})|\,,

which gives (
3.1
) and that
y_{j}
never enters
Q_{t}
, as desired.

∎

We next verify
(RGA 0)
and
(RGA 0)
. To this end
observe that because Algorithm
1
preserves
(INV 0)
,
the conditions of Lemma
2.29
are met. Thus with
probability at least
1-2^{-n/(\kappa r_{1})}
