, the following event

\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:rga:welldistr}}}}}
holds. For every
i\in[r]
and
W\subseteq V_{i}
with
|W|\geq\varrho|V_{i}|
, the number of vertices
x\in X_{i}\setminus X^{*}_{i}
such that for some time
t
(at which
x
is unembedded) we have

\big|C_{t}(x)\cap W\big|<(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|W|
is at most

\varrho|X_{i}|
.

Suppose now that
\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:rga:welldistr}}}}}
holds. We first show
that then
(RGA 0)
holds. Set

W:=V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}})
. By
(H 0)

and
(G 0)
, we have
|X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|\leq(1-4\mu)|X_{i}|
and

|V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|=(1-3\mu)|V_{i}|
. We conclude
|W|\geq\mu|V_{i}|
. Suppose that

x\in Q_{t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}}
. Then there is a first time
t
at which
x\in Q_{t}
.
Since we have
A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)\supseteq C_{t}(x)\cap W
, by the construction of

Q_{t}
in Algorithm
1
, we have

|C_{t}(x)\cap W|<\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}p^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(x)|<\big((d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p\big)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|W|

so that
x
satisfies condition (
2.6
) of
Lemma
2.29
. Since
|W|>\varrho|V_{i}|
, and because

\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:rga:welldistr}}}}}
holds, the number of
x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}}
which are in
X_{i}\setminus X^{*}_{i}
is at most
\varrho|X_{i}|
.
By
(G 0)
, we have
|X^{*}_{i}|\leq\varrho|X_{i}|
, so
|X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}}|\leq\varrho|X_{i}|+|X^{*}_{i}|\leq 2\varrho|X_{i}|
. By definition of
Algorithm
1
, the vertices
X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}}
are
precisely the vertices of
X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
not in
\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}})
,
giving
(RGA 0)
as desired.

Property
(RGA 0)
follows from

\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:rga:welldistr}}}}}
by observing that
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\subseteq X_{i}

and that when
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a degree-
b
buffer we have

(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}}(x)}\geq(dp)^{b}/2
by choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
.

To complete the proof of Lemma
3.1
we now verify that a.a.s. after Algorithm
1
finishes, also
(RGA 0)
is
satisfied. By
( )
‣
2.28
and
( )
‣
2.28
of
Lemma
2.28
, the set of vertices in
N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
which are not
neighbours of clique buffer vertices is an initial segment of
T
vertices
of
\tau
. We first show that the embedding of these
T
vertices cannot
fill up vertex neighbourhoods in
G
. For this purpose we want to apply
Lemma
2.30
to the
sequence of good partial embeddings produced by Algorithm
1
on
this initial segment. Observe that by Claim
3.6
, all
vertices in this initial segment satisfy (
3.1
) and none
enters the queue. By (
3.1
), Lemma
2.27
, and the
choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, each such
y
, embedded to create
\psi_{t+1}
, is
embedded into a subset of
C_{t}(y)
of size at least

\tfrac{2}{3}(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t}}|V(y)|-20\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}|V(y)|>\tfrac{1}{10}(dp)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}|V(y)|\,.

Furthermore, for each such
y
we have
\pi^{*}_{t}(y)\leq\Delta-2
by
Lemma
2.28
( )
‣
2.28
. Finally, by
(H 0)
we have

\big|\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{T})\cap X_{i}\big|\leq 4\mu\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}|X_{i}|
for each

i\in[r]
. This justifies that we can apply Lemma
2.30
with

B=\Delta
, and the result is that with probability at least
1-\exp(-\varepsilon pn/r_{1})
, the following event
\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:large_nbs}}}}}
holds. For
each
v\in V_{i}
and
j
such that
ij\in E(R^{\prime})
, we have

\big|N_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}_{j})\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{T})\big|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\deg_{G}(v;V_{j})\,.

(3.2)

We assume from now on that
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a non-clique degree-
b
buffer, and
we fix a vertex
v\in V_{i}
. We would like to estimate the probability
that
(RGA 0)
fails for
v
. To that end, first fix

x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, and let
y_{1},\ldots,y_{b}
be an enumeration of
N_{H}(x)
in
the order
\tau
. We now justify that if
\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:large_nbs}}}}}

holds, then we are in a position to apply Lemma
2.31

with
B=\Delta
. Recall that no vertices of
N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
enter the queue, by
Claim
3.6
, so that
\psi_{\tau(y_{1})}
is the good partial
embedding created by the embedding of
y_{1}
(and so on). By
(H 0)
,
and since
\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{\tau(y_{1})-1})\subseteq N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
, no vertices at
distance two or less from
x
in
H
are embedded in
\psi_{\tau(y_{1})-1}
.
Note that Lemma
2.28
( )
‣
2.28
states that if
b=\Delta

then
y_{b-1}y_{b}
is not an edge of
H
. Algorithm
1
thus
creates a sequence
\psi_{\tau(y_{1})-1},\dots,\psi_{\tau(y_{b})}
of good
partial embeddings, and a sequence of queue sets

Q_{\tau(y_{1})-1},\dots,Q_{\tau(y_{b})}
, matching the requirements of
Lemma
2.31
. Thus, by
Lemma
2.31
, the probability that
N_{H}(x)
is embedded
to
N_{G}(v)
, conditioning on
\psi_{\tau(y_{1})-1}
and that

\big|N_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}_{j})\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{\tau(y_{1})-1})\big|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\deg_{G}(v;V_{j})

for each
j
such that
ij\in R^{\prime}
(which is justified by (
3.2
)), is at least
(d^{\Delta}p/10)^{b}
.

Now we use this to estimate the cumulative effect of all
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. Let

x_{1},\dots,x_{m}
be an enumeration of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
according to the order on

N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
given by
\tau
. Let
Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{m}
be Bernoulli random
variables with
Y_{j}=1
if either
N_{H}(x_{j})
is embedded by

\psi_{t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}}
to
N_{G}(v)
or we witness a failure of

\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:large_nbs}}}}}
before the first neighbour of
x_{j}
is
embedded. Then we have just shown that
Y_{j}
has probability at least

(d^{\Delta}p/10)^{b}
of being one, conditioned on the history up to, but not
including, the embedding of the first vertex of
N_{H}(x_{j})
. This history
determines
Y_{j-1}
, so we can apply Lemma
2.2
, with

x=4\mu|X_{i}|(d^{\Delta}p/10)^{b}
and
\delta=\tfrac{1}{2}
, to conclude that

Y_{1}+\dots+Y_{m}\geq 2\mu|X_{i}|(d^{\Delta}p/10)^{b}
with probability at least

1-\exp(-\mu|X_{i}|(d^{\Delta}p/10)^{b}/3)
. Taking the union bound over all

v\in V(G)
we see that with probability at least
1-n\exp(-\varepsilon p^{\Delta}n/r_{1})>1-1/n
(where the inequality is by choice of
p
and since

b\leq\Delta
), either we witness a failure of

\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:large_nbs}}}}}
, or
(RGA 0)
holds.

Putting together the three probability bounds, we conclude that a.a.s. the events
\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:rga:welldistr}}}}}
,
\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:large_nbs}}}}}
and
(RGA 0)
all hold, proving Lemma
3.1
.
∎

3.3.
Queue embedding

In this section we prove Lemma
3.2
. The idea is as follows. For each
i\in[r]
in succession, we embed
X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
into
V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
, maintaining a good partial embedding. The way we do this is as follows. We need to embed each
x\in X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
into
C^{\mathrm{q}}(x)\setminus B(x)
, where
B(x)
is the set of bad vertices for
x
with respect to the current good partial embedding and
Q=V(H)
. (This choice of
Q
is
made only so that we are able to apply Lemma
2.27
without having to verify the at this time pointless badness condition (
2.5
).)
We therefore try to find a system of distinct representatives
v_{x}\in C^{\mathrm{q}}(x)\setminus B(x)
for each
x\in X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
, which we do by verifying
Hall’s condition. In turn, we verify Hall’s condition by showing that its
failure implies the existence of a dense spot in
G
, violating

\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
, which
\Gamma
satisfies. At this point one might
be concerned that embedding
x^{\prime}\in X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
to
v_{x^{\prime}}
could change
B(x)
.
Observe, however, that since
x
and
x^{\prime}
are at distance at least ten in
H

by
(H 0)
this does not occur.

Proof of Lemma
3.2
.

We require

\mu\leq\frac{1}{6}\,,\qquad\varrho\leq\frac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}}{200\kappa\Delta}\,,\qquad\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\frac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}}{1000\kappa 4^{\Delta}\Delta^{2}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\varepsilon\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{\kappa d}\,.

Let
\psi_{0}
be a good partial embedding whose image is disjoint from
V^{\mathrm{q}}
, and suppose that for each
i\in[r]
the set
X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
has size at most
2\varrho|X_{i}|
. We now define a sequence of good partial embeddings
\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{r}
with
\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{r})=\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{0})\cup\bigcup_{t=1}^{r}X^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}
and
\psi_{t}(x)\in V^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}
for each
x\in X^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}
and
1\leq t\leq r
. We let
C_{t}(x)
be the candidate set of
x
with respect to
\psi_{t}
, and so on, and let
B_{t}(x)
be the set of bad vertices for
x
with respect to
\psi_{t}
and
Q=V(H)
.

Suppose that for some
1\leq t\leq r
we have constructed
\psi_{t-1}
as above. We let
(v_{x})_{x\in X^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}}
be a system of distinct representatives for the sets
\big(C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t-1}(x)\setminus B_{t-1}(x)\big)_{x\in X^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}}
, and we set
\psi_{t}=\psi_{t-1}\cup\{x\to v_{x}\colon x\in X^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}\}
. We need to prove that this system of distinct representatives exists, and that the resulting
\psi_{t}
is a good partial embedding.

To see that the system of distinct representatives exists, we verify Hall’s condition. Let
X\subseteq X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
be non-empty, and let
W=\bigcup_{x\in X}C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t-1}(x)\setminus B_{t-1}(x)
. Then we need to show
|W|\geq|X|
. Assume for a contradiction that
|W|<|X|
holds. By averaging, there is
b\in\{0,\dots,\Delta\}
such that we find a subset
X_{b}
of (not necessarily all) vertices
x
in
X
with
\pi_{t-1}^{*}(x)=b
of size exactly
\tfrac{1}{\Delta+1}|W|
. Now each
x\in X_{b}
has
C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t-1}(x)\geq(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})\mu(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{b-|J_{x}|}|I_{x}|
by
(GPE 0)
, and
|I_{x}|\geq\zeta(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{|J_{x}|}|V_{i}|
by
(G 0)
. We conclude, by Lemma
2.27
, that

\big|C_{t-1}(x)\setminus B_{t-1}(x)\big|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\mu\zeta(dp)^{b}|V_{i}|-20\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{b}|V_{i}|\geq\tfrac{1}{4}\mu\zeta(dp)^{b}|V_{i}|

by choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
. In particular, we see that for each
x\in X_{b}
we have
\big|U_{t-1}(x)\cap W\big|\geq\tfrac{1}{4}\mu\zeta(dp)^{b}|V_{i}|
. We therefore have

\sum_{x\in X_{b}}\big|U_{t-1}(x)\cap W\big|\geq|X_{b}|\cdot\tfrac{1}{4}\mu\zeta(dp)^{b}|V_{i}|\,.

Since we would like to resort to the congestion property
\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
we eventually need to pass to a subset
W^{\prime}
of
W
of size
|X_{b}|
. Picking uniformly at random a subset
W^{\prime}
of
W
of size
|X_{b}|=\tfrac{|W|}{\Delta+1}
, we
have

\mathbb{E}_{W^{\prime}}\sum_{x\in X_{b}}\big|U_{t-1}(x)\cap W^{\prime}\big|\geq\tfrac{|X_{b}|}{\Delta+1}\cdot\tfrac{1}{4}\mu\zeta(dp)^{b}|V_{i}|\,,

and so in particular there is a subset
W^{\prime}
of
W
of size
|X_{b}|
such that

\sum_{x\in X_{b}}\big|U_{t-1}(x)\cap W^{\prime}\big|\geq\tfrac{|X_{b}|}{\Delta+1}\cdot\tfrac{1}{4}\mu\zeta(dp)^{b}|V_{i}|\,.

(3.3)

Recall that
\Pi_{t-1}(x)
is the set of embedded neighbours of
x
in
\psi_{t-1}
.
We now apply
\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
with

\mathcal{F}=\big\{\psi_{t-1}\big(\Pi_{t-1}(x)\cup J_{x}\big)\colon x\in X_{b}\big\}

and the set
W^{\prime}
. Note that

|W^{\prime}|=|X_{b}|=|\mathcal{F}|\leq 2\varrho|X_{i}|/(\Delta+1)\leq\varrho|V(\Gamma)|

because the sets
\psi_{t-1}\big(\Pi_{t-1}(x)\cup J_{x}\big)
are disjoint, so that we
can do this. We conclude that the congestion graph

\mathrm{CG}(\Gamma,W^{\prime},\mathcal{F})
satisfies

e\big(\mathrm{CG}(\Gamma,W^{\prime},\mathcal{F})\big)\leq 7p^{b}|W^{\prime}||\mathcal{F}|+\varrho p^{b}n|\mathcal{F}|/r_{1}\,.

(3.4)

But the edges of this congestion graph are precisely the pairs
F_{x}u

such that
F_{x}=\psi_{t-1}\big(\Pi_{t-1}(x)\cup J_{x}\big)

for
x\in X_{b}
and
u\in U_{t-1}(x)\cap W^{\prime}
, so
combining (
3.3
) and (
3.4
) we have

\tfrac{1}{4(\Delta+1)}\mu\zeta(dp)^{b}|X_{b}||V_{i}|\leq 14\varrho p^{b}|X_{b}||V_{i}|+\varrho\kappa p^{b}|X_{b}||V_{i}|\,,

where we use
|X_{b}|=|W^{\prime}|=|\mathcal{F}|\leq 2\varrho|V_{i}|
and
|V_{i}|\geq n/(\kappa r_{1})
. This is a contradiction by choice of
\varrho
. We conclude that the desired system of distinct representatives exists.

Now we show that
\psi_{t}
is a good partial embedding. Since the
v_{x}
are
distinct,
\psi_{t}
is injective. Since
C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t-1}(x)\subseteq I_{x}
for each

x
, we have
(GPE 0)
for each
x\in X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
. If
(GPE 0)

or
(GPE 0)
were to fail for some
y
, then
y\in N_{H}(x)
for some

x\in X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
. Since by
(H 0)
, vertices of
X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
are at distance
at least
10
in
H
, this
x
is unique. But by definition of

B_{t-1}(x)
the vertex
v_{x}
is not bad for
x
with respect to

\psi_{t-1}
, i.e. this case does not occur. Finally, if
(GPE 0)

fails for some
yz\in E(H)
, then again at least one of
y
and
z

(possibly both) is a neighbour of some
x\in X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
. Again
by
(H 0)
this
x
is unique, and again by definition of

B_{t-1}(x)
the vertex
v_{x}
is not bad for
x
with respect to
\psi_{t-1}
, so this case too does not occur. Thus
\psi_{t}
is a good partial embedding as desired.

By induction on
t
, the final
\psi_{\mathrm{q}}:=\psi_{r}
is a good partial embedding satisfying the conclusion of Lemma
3.2
.
∎

3.4.
Fixing buffer defects

To prove Lemma
3.3
we need to embed the reserved
cliques. The basic idea is as follows. If
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a clique buffer,
and a vertex
v
of
V_{i}
is a candidate for too few vertices in

X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
and not in the image of
\psi
(we call such a vertex poor),
then we will embed a reserved clique
K\in\mathcal{K}_{i}
on
v
and some further vertices
of
V^{\mathrm{c}}
. Observe that, since
K
is a
(\Delta+1)
-clique, none of the
vertices of
K
have embedded neighbours, hence
v
is in the available
candidate set of the vertex of
K
in
X_{i}
.

The only difficulty now is that some of the poor vertices
v
may
lie in
V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, and we risk destroying the hard-earned property that
every vertex in
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
has many candidates. In order to deal with this,
we will need to embed some vertices from
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
as well, at which point
further vertices of
V_{i}
may become poor and require embedding, and so
on.

Before we prove Lemma
3.3
we state two auxiliary lemmas. The
first justifies that the process just described terminates without eating up too many
vertices, that is, that we can find for each
i
small subsets
P_{i}
of
V_{i}
and

D_{i}
of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
such that every vertex
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus D_{i}
has many candidates in
V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus P_{i}
, and every vertex of

V_{i}\setminus P_{i}
is a candidate for many vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus D_{i}
.

Lemma 3.7
.

We assume the General Setup. Suppose that
\Gamma
has

\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
and let
i
be fixed. Suppose
\psi
is a good
partial embedding whose domain contains
N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
, and that
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}

is a clique buffer. Suppose furthermore that all but at most
\varrho|V_{i}|
vertices of
V_{i}
are candidates for at least

\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|X_{i}|/2
vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. Then there are subsets

P_{i}
of
V_{i}
and
D_{i}
of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
with the following properties.

(
PD
0)

We have
|P_{i}|<2\varrho|V_{i}|
and

|D_{i}|<2\varrho|X_{i}|
.

(
PD
0)

Each vertex of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus D_{i}

has at least
\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|V_{i}|/4
candidates in
V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus P_{i}
.

(
PD
0)

Each vertex of
V_{i}\setminus P_{i}
is a
candidate for at least
\mu(dp/100)^{\Delta}|X_{i}|
of the vertices in

X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus D_{i}
.

(
PD
0)

Each vertex in
D_{i}
has at least

\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|V_{i}|/4
candidates in
P_{i}\cap V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
.

The conclusion
(PD 0)
will be used later to show that we can embed
D_{i}
into
P_{i}\cap V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
.

Proof of Lemma
3.7
.

We require

\varrho<\frac{\mu d^{\Delta}}{250\kappa}\,.

We start with
D_{i}=\emptyset
and
P_{i}
being the set of vertices which
fail
(PD 0)
. We sequentially add vertices to
D_{i}

and
P_{i}
which fail
(PD 0)

and
(PD 0)
respectively (note that the property of
failing either condition is monotone), until either there are no
failing vertices to add to either set or one of the two sets reaches

2\varrho|V_{i}|
vertices. Observe that any vertex
x
of
D_{i}
has
by
(GPE 0)
at least
(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\Delta}p^{\Delta}|V_{i}|
candidates in
V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. Since
x
must have
violated
(PD 0)
at some point, by choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}

it therefore has at least
\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|V_{i}|/4
candidates in

P_{i}\cap V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, establishing
(PD 0)
.

In the process of building
D_{i}
and
P_{i}
we call a vertex
v\in V_{i}\setminus P_{i}

poor
if
v
is a candidate for less than
\mu(dp/100)^{\Delta}|X_{i}|
vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus D_{i}
.
If
P_{i}
reaches
2\varrho|V_{i}|
vertices first, let
P=P_{i}

and let
D
a superset of
D_{i}
, both of size exactly
2\varrho|V_{i}|
. Since,
by the assumption of the lemma, at most

\varrho|V_{i}|
vertices in
V_{i}
are poor with respect to
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, at
least
\varrho|V_{i}|
of the vertices in
P
were added to
P_{i}
because
they became poor, that is, they are candidate for at least

\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|X_{i}|/2
vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, but for less than

\mu(dp/100)^{\Delta}|X_{i}|
of the vertices
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus D_{i}
. It
follows that each is candidate for at least
\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|X_{i}|/4

vertices of
D_{i}
, and so there are at least

\varrho|V_{i}|\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|X_{i}|/4
edges in the candidate graph between
P

and
D
. Now

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\varrho\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|X_{i}||V_{i}|

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{8}\varrho\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|X_{i}||V_{i}|+\tfrac{1}{8}\varrho\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|X_{i}||V_{i}|

\displaystyle>\frac{\mu d^{\Delta}}{32\varrho}p^{\Delta}|P||D|+\frac{\mu d^{\Delta}}{16\kappa r_{1}}p^{\Delta}n|D|

\displaystyle>7p^{\Delta}|P||D|+\varrho p^{\Delta}n|D|/r_{1}\,,

where the first inequality comes from the sizes of
P
and
D
, and the
second from the choice of
\varrho
sufficiently small. The last line is in
contradiction to the congestion condition
\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
, since the candidate graph
between
P
and
D
is a subgraph of
\mathrm{CG}(\Gamma,P,\mathcal{F}_{D})
with

\mathcal{F}_{D}:=\{\psi(N_{H}(x))\colon x\in D\}
.

If on the other hand
D_{i}
reaches

2\varrho|V_{i}|
vertices first, we define similarly
P
a superset of
P_{i}
and
D=D_{i}
, both of size
2\varrho|V_{i}|
. Each
vertex of
D
has at least
\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|V_{i}|/4
candidates in
P

by
(PD 0)
, and thus the candidate graph between
P
and

D
has at least
2\varrho|X_{i}|\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|V_{i}|/4
edges, which is larger
than in the previous case and so also gives a contradiction to

\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
.

We conclude that the process terminates for lack of failing vertices, which establishes
(PD 0)
.
∎

The second lemma simply gives a condition on a vertex
v\in V_{i}
under which
we can find a clique
K_{\Delta+1}
containing
v
in
G
whose remaining
vertices lie in
V^{\mathrm{c}}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
.

Lemma 3.8
.

We assume the General Setup. Suppose that
\Gamma
has
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
,

\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},\Delta)
and
\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
. Let

i,j_{1},\ldots,j_{\Delta}
form a copy of
K_{\Delta+1}
in
R^{\prime}
and
v
be a
vertex of
V_{i}
. Suppose that
\psi
is any good partial embedding
which embeds no vertex on
v
, and is such that
v
has at least

\tfrac{\mu d}{8}\max\big(p|V_{j_{k}}|,\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j_{k}})\big)

(3.5)

neighbours in

V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j_{k}}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
in
G
for each
1\leq k\leq\Delta
.
Suppose that there is a copy of
K_{\Delta+1}
in
H
using a vertex of

\tilde{X}_{i}
and a vertex of each of
X_{j_{1}},\ldots,X_{j_{\Delta}}
. Then there are vertices
v_{k}\in V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j_{k}}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
for
1\leq k\leq\Delta
,
such that
v,v_{1},\ldots,v_{\Delta}
form a copy of
K_{\Delta+1}
in
G
.

Proof.

We require

\varepsilon<\varepsilon^{\prime}<\frac{(d/32)^{\Delta}\mu}{2\kappa\Delta^{2}}\,.

We choose the vertices
v_{1},\ldots,v_{\Delta}
in succession. When we choose

v_{s}
, if
s<\Delta
we require that for each
s+1\leq k\leq\Delta
we have

|N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v_{1},\ldots,v_{s})\cap V_{j_{k}}|=(p\pm\varepsilon p)^{s}\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j_{k}})\text{ and }

(3.6)

|N^{*}_{G}(v,v_{1},\ldots,v_{s})\cap\left(V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j_{k}}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi)\right)|\geq\big(\tfrac{dp}{4}\big)^{s}\tfrac{\mu d}{8}\max\big(p|V_{j_{k}}|,\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j_{k}})\big)\,.

(3.7)

If
s<\Delta-1
we further require that for
each
s+1\leq k<k^{\prime}\leq\Delta
the pair

\big(N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v_{1},\ldots,v_{s})\cap V_{j_{k}},N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v_{1},\ldots,v_{s})\cap V_{j_{k^{\prime}}}\big)

(3.8)

is
(\varepsilon_{s,s},d,p)
-regular.

Note that these conditions are satisfied, with
s=0
, before we have chosen
any vertices. Indeed, the first is a tautology, the second is the assumption on
v

in the lemma statement, and the third is a statement that
V_{i}
has
two-sided inheritance with respect to
V_{j_{k}}
and
V_{j_{k^{\prime}}}
, which
holds since we assumed there is a triangle of
X
using one vertex of each
of
\tilde{X}_{i}
,
X_{j_{k}}
and
X_{j_{k^{\prime}}}
and
by
(H 0)
and
(G 0)
.

Suppose we have chosen vertices
v_{1}
,…,
v_{s-1}
so far
and that we have
s\leq\Delta-1
.
Because
\Gamma
has
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
, at
step
s
the number of vertices
w
failing condition (
3.6
) with
v_{s}=w
, is at most

\Delta\varepsilon p^{\Delta-1}|V(\Gamma)|/r_{1}^{2}<\big(\tfrac{dp}{4}\big)^{s-1}\tfrac{\mu d}{32}\max\big(p|V_{j_{k}}|,\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j_{k}})\big)\,.

By

(\varepsilon_{s-1,s-1},d,p)
-regularity of the pair

\big(N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v_{1},\ldots,v_{s-1})\cap V_{j_{k}},N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v_{1},\ldots,v_{s-1})\cap V_{j_{k^{\prime}}}\big)\,,

and using the upper bound from (
3.6
) on

|N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v_{1},\ldots,v_{s-1})\cap V_{j_{k}}|
, at most

\Delta\varepsilon^{\prime}(p+\varepsilon p)^{s-1}\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j_{k}})<\big(\tfrac{dp}{4}\big)^{s-1}\tfrac{\mu d}{32}\max\big(p|V_{j_{k}}|,\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j_{k}})\big)

vertices

w\in V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j_{s}}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi)
fail condition (
3.7
) with
v_{s}=w
.
Finally, if
s\leq\Delta-2
and since
\Gamma
has
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},\Delta)
, at most

\Delta^{2}\varepsilon p^{\Delta-2}|V(\Gamma)|/r_{1}^{2}<\big(\tfrac{dp}{4}\big)^{s-1}\tfrac{\mu d}{32}\max\big(p|V_{j_{k}}|,\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j_{k}})\big)

vertices fail condition (
3.8
). Thus there are at least

\big(\tfrac{dp}{4}\big)^{s-1}\tfrac{\mu d}{32}\max\big(p|V_{j_{k}}|,\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j_{k}})\big)>0

vertices
from
N^{*}_{G}(v,v_{1},\ldots,v_{s-1})\cap\left(V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j_{s}}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi)\right)
which
satisfy all three conditions, and we can choose
v_{s}
for each
1\leq s\leq\Delta
as desired.

Furthermore observe that for
s=\Delta
it suffices to choose
an arbitrary vertex from
N^{*}_{G}(v,v_{1},\ldots,v_{\Delta-1})\cap\left(V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j_{\Delta}}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi)\right)
.
The lemma follows.
∎

We can now prove Lemma
3.3
. We construct the desired
embedding in two steps. First, we embed each
D_{i}
into
P_{i}\cap V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
,
which by verifying Hall’s condition. Let
P^{\prime}_{i}
be those vertices
of
P_{i}
not covered in this process. It then remains to cover all
P^{\prime}_{i}

using the reserved cliques, and embed any left-over reserved cliques
at the end of this process. We do this sequentially by applying
Lemma
3.8
to find a destination for a reserved clique,
either letting
v
be the next poor vertex or, if all of these are used,
letting
v
be some vertex of
V^{\mathrm{c}}
. Note that in this step, we
need to maintain the degree condition (
3.5
) of
Lemma
3.8
. We have this condition initially
by
(G 0)
, and we will see that to maintain it, it suffices to
choose at each step a most dangerous vertex
v
, that is, one
minimising the parameter
\mindeg
we define in the proof.

Proof of Lemma
3.3
.

We require

\varrho<\min\Big(\frac{\mu d^{\Delta}}{250\kappa},\frac{\mu d}{64\kappa(\Delta_{R^{\prime}}+1)}\Big)\quad\text{ and }\varepsilon<\varepsilon^{\prime}<\frac{d^{\Delta}}{32^{\Delta}\kappa\Delta\Delta_{R^{\prime}}}\,,

and assume that
\Gamma
has
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
,
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},\Delta)
and

\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
.

For each
i
such that
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a clique buffer, we let
P_{i}
and
D_{i}

be as given by Lemma
3.7
.
If
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is not a clique buffer, then we let
P_{i}=D_{i}=\emptyset
.

Now for each
i
in succession, we find a matching in the candidate graph
between
D_{i}
and
P_{i}\cap V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. To do this we simply verify Hall’s
condition, using the congestion condition and
(PD 0)
.
Specifically, suppose we have a non-empty
W\subseteq D_{i}
, and
Z\subseteq P_{i}
is the set of vertices in
P_{i}
which are candidate for some
w\in W
.
We need to verify that
|W|\leq|Z|
. If this were false, then we could let

Z^{\prime}
be a superset of
Z
of size
|W|
, and the number of edges in the
candidate graph between
W
and
Z^{\prime}
is by
(PD 0)
at
least
\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|V_{i}||W|/4
. Notice that
|D_{i}|
,
|P_{i}|<2\varrho|V_{i}|

by
(PD 0)
. Hence we have

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{4}\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|V_{i}||W|

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{8}\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|V_{i}||W|+\tfrac{1}{8}\mu(dp)^{\Delta}|V_{i}||W|

\displaystyle>\frac{\mu d^{\Delta}}{16\varrho}p^{\Delta}|Z^{\prime}||W|+\frac{\mu d^{\Delta}}{8\kappa r_{1}}p^{\Delta}n|W|

\displaystyle>7p^{\Delta}|Z^{\prime}||W|+\varrho p^{\Delta}n|W|/r_{1}\,,

which is in contradiction to
\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
, applied with
Z^{\prime}

and the family
\mathcal{F}=\{\psi(N_{H}(w))\colon w\in W\}
to the congestion
graph
\mathrm{CG}(\Gamma,Z^{\prime},\mathcal{F})
. We conclude that Hall’s condition holds and
hence the desired matching exists. We let
\psi^{\prime}
be the embedding
obtained from
\psi
by embedding each vertex of each
D_{i}
to its
matching partner in
P_{i}\cap V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, and let
P^{\prime}_{i}\subseteq P_{i}
be the
set of those vertices in
P_{i}
not embedded by
\psi^{\prime}
. Observe that

\psi^{\prime}
is a good partial embedding since all neighbours of the buffer
vertices are already embedded under
\psi
, and
thus
(GPE 0)
–
(GPE 0)
trivially hold for
\psi^{\prime}
.

As second step, we provide an algorithm, Algorithm
2
, to embed the reserved cliques
in
\mathcal{K}_{i}
for each
i\in[r_{1}]
. Given a vertex
v\in V_{i}
and a
partial embedding
\psi
we define the parameter

\mindeg_{\psi}(v):=\min_{j:ij\in R^{\prime}}\frac{\deg_{G}\big(v;V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi)\big)}{\max\big(p|V_{j}|,\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j})\big)}\,,

which is used in Algorithm
2
.
Observe that
we have initially
\mindeg_{\psi}(v)\geq\mu(d-\varepsilon)/2
for
all
v\in V_{i}
with
\deg_{R^{\prime}}(i)>0
, which follows from
(G 0)
.
We claim that the algorithm runs correctly and the finally returned

\psi_{\mathrm{good}}
is the desired good partial embedding. It remains to justify this
claim.

t:=0
;

\psi_{0}:=\psi^{\prime}
;

while

\bigcup_{i}\mathcal{K}_{i}\neq\emptyset

do

I:=\{i\colon\text{there is an unembedded clique in }\mathcal{K}_{i}\}
;

if

\bigcup_{i}P^{\prime}_{i}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})\neq\emptyset

then

choose
v\in\bigcup_{i}P^{\prime}_{i}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})
minimising

\mindeg_{\psi_{t}}(v)
;

else

choose
v\in\bigcup_{i\in I}V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})
minimising
\mindeg_{\psi_{t}}(v)
;

end if

set
i
such that
v\in V_{i}
;

choose an unembedded clique
K_{H}
in
\mathcal{K}_{i}
;

choose a clique
K_{G}
containing
v
and one vertex
from each
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})
such that
X_{j}
intersects
K_{H}
and
j\neq i
;

set
\psi_{t+1}:=\psi_{t}\cup\{K_{H}\to K_{G}\}
;

t:=t+1
;

end while

return
\psi_{\mathrm{good}}:=\psi_{t}
;

Algorithm 2

Removing poor vertices

Roughly speaking
each of the vertices from
P^{\prime}_{i}
can serve us as an image for each of the

2\varrho|X_{i}|
cliques from
\mathcal{K}_{i}
.
During the first phase of the algorithm, that is, while the
if-condition is true, we embed reserved cliques in these vertices,
always choosing a vertex
v
minimising
\mindeg_{\psi}(v)
, which will guarantee
successful completion.
Note that
|P^{\prime}_{i}|<2\varrho|V_{i}|
holds by
(PD 0)
and
|\mathcal{K}_{i}|=2\varrho|X_{i}|
, so
if the algorithm doesn’t fail then all the vertices from

\bigcup_{i}P^{\prime}_{i}
will be used. In the second phase, when the
if-condition becomes false, we have to take
care of the remaining unembedded cliques in
\bigcup_{i}\mathcal{K}_{i}
.
Since the
algorithm embeds only onto vertices
\bigcup P^{\prime}_{i}\cup V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
, if it
completes successfully, then the desired
properties
(BD 0)
and
(BD 0)
are
guaranteed for
\psi_{\mathrm{good}}

by
(PD 0)
and
(PD 0)
.

Therefore, it remains to show that the algorithm succeeds, that is,
that there is no
failure while choosing a clique
K_{G}
in some iteration.
By Lemma
3.8
, this is
guaranteed if we can show that at each time
t
the vertex
v\in V_{i}
chosen
has at least

\tfrac{\mu d}{8}\max\big(p|V_{j}|,\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j})\big)

(3.9)

neighbours in each set
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})
such that
ij\in R^{\prime}
.

By the assumption of the lemma, for each
i
, the set
X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
of vertices

x\in X_{i}
contained in some reserved clique from
\bigcup_{j}\mathcal{K}_{j}
satisfies

|X^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}|\leq 2\kappa(\Delta_{R^{\prime}}+1)\varrho|X_{i}|
.
It follows that for
any
t
and
j
we have

|\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})\cap V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j}|\leq 2\kappa(\Delta_{R^{\prime}}+1)\varrho|X_{j}|\,.

(3.10)

Claim 3.9
.

For any
t
at most
\Delta_{R^{\prime}}\varepsilon p^{\Delta-1}|V(\Gamma)|/r_{1}^{2}

vertices
v\in V_{i}
satisfy

\mindeg_{\psi_{t}}(v)<3\mu d/16\,.

Proof.

Each vertex
v
of
V_{i}
has at least

\mu(d-\varepsilon)\max\big(p|V_{j}|,\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j})/2\big)>\tfrac{\mu d}{4}\max\big(p|V_{j}|,\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j})\big)

neighbours in

V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j}
by
(G 0)
.
Moreover, it is a direct consequence of the
neighbourhood size property
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
of
\Gamma
and (
3.10
)
that the number of vertices in

\Gamma
which have more than
4\kappa(\Delta_{R^{\prime}}+1)\varrho p|X_{j}|

neighbours in
\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})\cap V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j}
is at most
\varepsilon p^{\Delta-1}|V(\Gamma)|/r_{1}^{2}
.
Since
4\kappa(\Delta_{R^{\prime}}+1)\varrho p|X_{j}|<\mu dp|V_{j}|/16
, it follows that for any
t
there are at most

\Delta_{R^{\prime}}\varepsilon p^{\Delta-1}|V(\Gamma)|/r_{1}^{2}
vertices in
V_{i}
with
fewer than
\tfrac{3\mu d}{16}\max\big(p|V_{j}|,\deg_{\Gamma}(v;V_{j})\big)
neighbours in any
set
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})
such that
ij\in R^{\prime}
, that is, with

\mindeg_{\psi_{t}}(v)<3\mu d/16
.

∎

In the remainder we show that if Algorithm
2
chooses a vertex
v

such that (
3.9
) fails, then we obtain a contradiction to
Claim
3.9
.
Indeed, in each iteration at most one vertex is embedded in any given
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j}
. So if
there is a time
t
at which a vertex
v\in V_{i}
is chosen with fewer
neighbours
than the bound in (
3.9
)
in some set
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{j}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})
such that
ij\in R^{\prime}
, then at each
of the preceding
\mu dp|V_{j}|/16
times
t^{\prime}
the vertex
v
had

\mindeg_{\psi_{t^{\prime}}}(v)<3\mu d/16
. Hence each of the at least
\mu dpn/(16\kappa r_{1})
vertices
v^{\prime}
chosen at these times has

\mindeg_{\psi_{t}}(v^{\prime})\leq\mindeg_{\psi_{t^{\prime}}}(v^{\prime})<3\mu d/16\,.

Since
there are at most
r_{1}
clusters
V_{i}
in
G
, and since

\mu dpn/(16\kappa r_{1})>r_{1}\Delta_{R^{\prime}}\varepsilon p^{\Delta-1}|V(\Gamma)|/r_{1}^{2}\,,

this
contradicts Claim
3.9
.
∎

Chapter 4
Proof of the blow-up lemma for bijumbled graphs

4.1.
The RGA lemma and the proof of the blow-up lemma

In this section we prove Lemma
1.25
, conditional on the random greedy lemma (Lemma
4.1
) which we prove in Section
4.2
.

The proof is quite similar to that of Lemma
1.21
, and indeed we make use of the same General Setup. The difference is that we can no longer use property
\mathrm{CON}(\varrho,r_{1},\Delta)
as
a bijumbled graph
\Gamma
need not satisfy it (see the discussion in Section
2.2
). Instead, we ‘replace’ it with the lopsided neighbourhood size property
\mathrm{LNS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
.

In the next section we will use a somewhat different random greedy strategy than Algorithm
1
to prove the following lemma. However, we will re-use the auxiliary lemmas proved in Section
2.4
in its proof (recall that these lemmas are not dependent on Algorithm
1
).

Lemma 4.1

(bijumbled RGA lemma)
.

We assume the General Setup. Suppose further that
\Gamma
has properties
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta+1)
,
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},\Delta+1)
and
\mathrm{LNS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
, and that at most
\varrho p^{\Delta}|X_{i}|
vertices in each
X_{i}
are image restricted. Then there is a good partial embedding
\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
such that the
following hold for each
i
. Let
b
be such that
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a degree-
b

buffer.

(
PRGA
0)

Every vertex in

X^{\mathrm{main}}
is embedded to

V^{\mathrm{main}}\cup V^{\mathrm{q}}
by
\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
, and no vertex in
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
is embedded.

(
PRGA
0)

Every vertex in
V_{i}
is a
candidate for at least
\mu(d^{\Delta}p/100)^{b}|X_{i}|
vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
.

(
PRGA
0)

For every set
W\subseteq V_{i}
of size at least

\varrho|V_{i}|
, there are at most
\varrho|X_{i}|

vertices in
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
with fewer than
\tfrac{1}{2}(dp)^{b}|W|
candidates in
W
.

(
PRGA
0)

For all
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, letting

\qquad Y:=\big\{y\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}:\big|U^{\mathrm{buf}}(y)\cap C^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)\big|>(p+\varepsilon p)^{b}|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)|\big\}\,,

we have

\sum_{y\in Y}\frac{\big|U^{\mathrm{buf}}(y)\cap C^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)\big|}{\big|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y)\big|}\leq\frac{|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)|}{20}\,.

(
PRGA
0)

For each
i
, for all but at most
\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\Delta}|V_{i}|
vertices
v\in V_{i}
, there are at most
\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\Delta}|V_{i}|
vertices
v^{\prime}\in V_{i}
such that

\big|\{x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}:v,v^{\prime}\in C(x)\}\big|>24\mu\Delta^{2}\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}\big)^{b}p^{2b}|X_{i}|\,.

Comparing this lemma to Lemma
3.1
(RGA lemma), one notices that we demand more of the
\mathrm{NS}
and
\mathrm{RI}
pseudorandomness properties. The reason for doing this is that we can then make use of Lemmas
2.30
and
2.31
for all buffer vertices, whether or not they are in copies of
K_{\Delta+1}
, and thus there is no exception for clique buffers in
(PRGA 0)
. This simplifies our proof substantially, as we no longer need to fix buffer defects. We will explain later why this does not harm our eventual bijumbledness requirement.

Property
(PRGA 0)
corresponds to
(RGA 0)
. Further, properties
(PRGA 0)
and
(PRGA 0)

form the ‘no dense spots’ property we require in order to
complete the embedding. In Lemma
3.1
no such property appears, but in random graphs the
\mathrm{CON}
property guarantees that no dense spots exist. Somewhat
informally,
(PRGA 0)
asserts that typically candidate sets
intersect as if they are random sets, while
property
(PRGA 0)
states the same about intersections of the
neighbourhoods of vertices from
V(G)
in the candidate graph.

Observe that, in comparison to Lemma
3.1
, here we ask for an additional pseudorandomness property

\mathrm{LNS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
, which we require to establish
(PRGA 0)
and
(PRGA 0)
and in order to embed all
vertices.

4.1.1.
Outline of the proof of Lemma
1.25

The proof of Lemma
1.25
now looks quite similar to the proof of Lemma
1.21
(the blow-up lemma for random graphs). Again, we use Lemma
2.22
to obtain the General Setup. However, this time we do not choose any reserved cliques, whether or not we have buffer vertices in cliques. We note that although we could state a version of Lemma
4.1
which asked only for
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
and
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},\Delta)
rather than with
\Delta+1
, and which made exceptions for clique buffers, this would not improve our eventual bijumbledness requirement on
\Gamma
, except for
\Delta=2
, since for
\Delta\geq 3
the requirement is determined by
\mathrm{LNS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
. We apply Lemma
4.1
to obtain a good partial embedding whose domain is
X^{\mathrm{main}}
with the additional properties stated there. We then have only to embed
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
. Again, we do this one part at a time, by verifying Hall’s condition, and again we separate the verification into small, large and medium-sized subsets of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. Again, the ‘medium-sized’ case is dealt with quickly using
(PRGA 0)
.

However, now we approach the ‘small’ and ‘large’ cases differently, since we no longer have access to the
\mathrm{CON}
property. In the ‘small’ case (see Claim
4.2
), our approach is the following. Given
X\subseteq X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, we construct an auxiliary embedding of
X
into
V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
such that each
x
is embedded into
C(x)
. Although this auxiliary embedding is
not
part of the embedding we will finally give, its existence verifies Hall’s condition for
X
. The way we will construct the embedding is simply to embed
X
vertex by vertex, at each step choosing for
x
uniformly at random a so far unused vertex of
C^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)
. Since
\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
has
(GPE 0)
we know
C^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)
is always reasonably large, and we will show that with high probability it is never more than half covered. Let us briefly explain how this works. We assume that no candidate sets are more than half covered before reaching
x
. When we embedded some vertex
y
, the probability of embedding it to
C(x)
was at most

\frac{\big|U^{\mathrm{buf}}(y)\cap C^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)\big|}{\tfrac{1}{2}|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y)|}

(4.1)

since (by assumption) we embed
y
into a set of size at least
\tfrac{1}{2}|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y)|
, of which certainly at most
\big|U^{\mathrm{buf}}(y)\cap C^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)\big|
vertices are in
C^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)
. But
(PRGA 0)
gives us an upper bound for the sum of these probabilities over all
y\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
such that (
4.1
) is ‘large’, and it is easy to account for the
y\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
such that (
4.1
) is not large. We can thus apply Lemma
2.2
to conclude that it is unlikely that
C^{\mathrm{buf}}(x)
was more than half covered, and by the union bound over all
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
we conclude that with high probability we never fail.

For the ‘large’ case (see Claim
4.3
) we use a similar argument, but using
(PRGA 0)
and
(PRGA 0)
instead of
(GPE 0)
and
(PRGA 0)
.

4.1.2.
Proof of Lemma
1.25

Now we give the full details of the proof outlined above.

Proof of Lemma
1.25
.

First we choose constants as follows. Given
\Delta
,
\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{BL}}
,
\Delta_{J}
integers,
\alpha^{\mathrm{BL}}
,
\zeta^{\mathrm{BL}}
and
d>0
, and
\kappa^{\mathrm{BL}}>1
, we set
\vartheta=\Delta
,
\Delta_{R^{\prime}}=8(\Delta+\Delta_{J})^{10}\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{BL}}
,
\alpha=\tfrac{1}{2}\alpha^{\mathrm{BL}}
,
\zeta=\tfrac{1}{2}\zeta^{\mathrm{BL}}
and
\kappa=2\kappa^{\mathrm{BL}}
. We now choose

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha d^{\Delta}}{20000\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{4}\Delta^{10}(\Delta+2)},

\displaystyle\varrho

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\mu^{2}\zeta^{2}}{2000\Delta^{2}}\big(\tfrac{\mu\zeta d^{4\Delta+2}}{10^{6\Delta}}\big)^{\Delta}\,,

and

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta^{2}}\varrho}{10^{6\Delta}\Delta^{3}\kappa 4^{\Delta}}2^{-4/\varrho}\,.

Now for input
\Delta
,
d
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, Lemma
2.18
returns constants
\varepsilon_{a,b}
and
\varepsilon_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:det_jumbled}}}}}>0
.
We set

\varepsilon=\min\big(\tfrac{2^{10\Delta}d(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}}{\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}},\varepsilon_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:det_jumbled}}}}}\big)\,.

We let
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{BL}}=\tfrac{1}{16}(\Delta+\Delta_{J})^{-10}\varepsilon
and
\varrho^{\mathrm{BL}}=\tfrac{1}{16}(\Delta+\Delta_{J})^{-10}\varrho
.

Now Lemma
1.25
returns
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{BL}}
and
\varrho^{\mathrm{BL}}
. Given
r_{1}^{\mathrm{BL}}
we let
r_{1}=8(\Delta+\Delta_{J})^{10}r_{1}^{\mathrm{BL}}
. We choose
c
sufficiently small for Lemma
2.18
with input
\Delta
,
d
,
\varepsilon^{\prime}
and
T=r_{1}
.

We require

p\geq\tfrac{10^{6}(\Delta+\Delta_{J})^{10}\kappa r_{1}}{\mu\zeta\varrho d^{\Delta}\varepsilon^{-2}}\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/(2\Delta)}\,,

where the condition on the constant in front of the
\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/(2\Delta)}
-term comes from Lemma
2.22
, while the term
\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/(2\Delta)}
is required for Lemma
4.1
.
We remark, however, that this lower bound is not a restriction since a somewhat stronger lower bound on
p
of the form
\Omega(n^{-1/2(t-1)})
for any
(p,cp^{t}n)
-bijumbled graph follows by considering the inequality (
1.1
) which defines bijumbledness for, say, sets
X=\{v\}
and
Y=V(\Gamma)\setminus(\{v\}\cup N_{\Gamma}(v))
.

Let
\Gamma
be an
n
-vertex,
(p,cp^{\max(4,3\Delta/2+1/2)}n)
-bijumbled graph. Then Lemma
2.18
states that
\Gamma
has the three properties
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta+1)
,
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},\Delta+1)
and
\mathrm{LNS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
. From now on we will simply assume
\Gamma
is an
n
-vertex graph which satisfies these three properties.

Given a graph
R_{\mathrm{BL}}
on
r_{\mathrm{BL}}\leq r_{1}^{\mathrm{BL}}
vertices, a spanning subgraph
R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{BL}}
with
\Delta(R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{BL}})\leq\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{BL}}
, and graphs
H
and
G\subseteq\Gamma
with vertex partitions
\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{BL}}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{BL}}
, a family of potential buffer vertices
\mathcal{\tilde{X}}^{\mathrm{BL}}
, and a
(\varrho^{\mathrm{BL}}p^{\Delta},\zeta^{\mathrm{BL}},\Delta,\Delta_{J})
-restriction pair
\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{BL}}
,
\mathcal{J}
, suppose that the conditions of Lemma
1.25
are satisfied. Then the good partitions lemma, Lemma
2.22
, (with
b_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:matchreduce}}}}}=\Delta
) gives a graph
R
on
r\leq r_{1}
vertices, a spanning subgraph
R^{\prime}
with
\Delta(R^{\prime})\leq\Delta_{R^{\prime}}
, and
\kappa
-balanced size-compatible partitions
{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{V}}
of
H
and
G
respectively, each part having size at least
n/(\kappa r_{1})
, together with a family
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}
of potential buffer vertices and
\mathcal{I}
of image restrictions, subsets
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
of
\tilde{X}_{i}
for each
i\in[r]
, and partitions
V_{i}=V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
for each
i\in[r]
which satisfy the General Setup.

We let
X^{\mathrm{main}}=V(H)\setminus X^{\mathrm{buf}}
. We now begin the embedding of
H
into
G
. By Lemma
4.1
, there is a good partial embedding
\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
of
X^{\mathrm{main}}
into

V^{\mathrm{main}}\cup V^{\mathrm{q}}
with properties
(PRGA 0)
–
(PRGA 0)
as stated in that lemma.

Our aim now is to complete the embedding of
H
into
G
by finding for each

i
a matching in the available candidate graph between
V^{\prime}_{i}:=V_{i}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}})
and
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. Let us assume that
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a degree-
b
buffer. To show there is a matching, we will verify Hall’s condition, so let
Y
be a non-empty subset of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. Let
U
be the set of vertices in
V^{\prime}_{i}
which are candidate for at least one vertex of
Y
, then our aim is to show
|U|\geq|Y|
. We split this into three cases, the harder two of which are done in the following two claims.

Claim 4.2
.

If
0<|Y|\leq\varrho|X_{i}|
, then we have
|U|\geq|Y|
.

Proof.

By
(H 0)
no
y\in Y
is image restricted, so because
\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
is a good partial embedding, by
(GPE 0)
for each
y\in Y
we have

|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y)|\geq(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{b}p^{b}|V_{i}|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(dp)^{b}|V_{i}|\,,

where the second inequality is by choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
. Let
y_{1},\ldots,y_{|Y|}
be an enumeration of
Y
.
Now for each
j=1,\ldots,|Y|
we choose
v_{j}
uniformly at random from
C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})\setminus\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{j-1}\}
if this is possible; if not, we say
v_{j}
does not exist. Our aim is to show that this does not occur, in other words that we obtain
|Y|
distinct vertices of
|U|
, verifying the claim.

Let
\mathscr{H}_{0}
be the empty history, and for each
1\leq j\leq|Y|
, let
\mathscr{H}_{j}
be the history of this process up to and including the choice of
v_{j}
. We claim that a.a.s.

\big|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})\setminus\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{j-1}\}\big|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\big|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})\big|

(4.2)

holds for each
j
. For a given
j
we define random variables
Z^{(j)}_{\ell}
,
\ell\in[j-1]
, as follows.
We set
Z^{(j)}_{\ell}=1
if
v_{\ell}\in C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})
and
|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{\ell})\setminus\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell-1}\}|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{\ell})|
hold, and

Z^{(j)}_{\ell}=0
otherwise. For any history
\mathscr{H}_{\ell-1}
of this process we can bound the
conditional expectation of
Z^{(j)}_{\ell}
by

\mathbb{E}(Z^{(j)}_{\ell}|\mathscr{H}_{\ell-1})=\mathbb{P}(Z^{(j)}_{\ell}=1|\mathscr{H}_{\ell-1})\\
\leq\frac{\big|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{\ell})\cap C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})\setminus\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell-1}\}\big|}{\big|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{\ell})\setminus\{v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell-1}\}\big|}\leq\frac{\big|U^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{\ell})\cap C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})\big|}{\tfrac{1}{2}\big|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{\ell})\big|}\,.

(4.3)

Observe that
\sum_{\ell=1}^{j-1}Z^{(j)}_{\ell}
is exactly the number of those vertices
v_{k}
among
v_{1}
, …,
v_{\ell-1}
which lie in
C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})
and were chosen from a set of size at least
\tfrac{1}{2}|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{\ell})|
.
To obtain an upper bound on the expectation of

\sum_{\ell=1}^{j-1}Z^{(j)}_{\ell}
, we sum up (
4.3
) for all
\ell\in[j-1]
. We split this sum according to whether
\big|U^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{\ell})\cap C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})\big|\leq(p+\varepsilon p)^{b}|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})|
or not.
In the former case, each summand is by
(GPE 0)
and
(GPE 0)
at most

\frac{2(p+\varepsilon p)^{b}\mu(p+\varepsilon p)^{b}|V_{i}|}{(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})\mu(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{b}|V_{i}|}\leq 4d^{-\Delta}p^{b}\,,

where the inequality is by choice of
\varepsilon
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, while
(PRGA 0)
bounds the sum over the remaining terms. We get

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=1}^{j-1}\mathbb{E}\big[Z^{(j)}_{\ell}\big|\mathscr{H}_{\ell-1}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=1}^{j-1}\frac{\big|U^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{\ell})\cap C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})\big|}{\tfrac{1}{2}\big|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{\ell})\big|}

\displaystyle\leq 4(j-1)d^{-\Delta}p^{b}+\frac{|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})|}{10}\leq\frac{|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})|}{5}

where the last inequality uses
j\leq|Y|\leq\varrho|X_{i}|
,
(GPE 0)
and the choice of
\varrho
and
\varepsilon
. Using the sequential dependence lemma, Lemma
2.2
, with
\delta=1
, we conclude that the probability of the event
\sum_{\ell=1}^{j-1}Z^{(j)}_{\ell}>\tfrac{2}{5}|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})|
is at most
e^{-|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})|/15}\leq e^{-\mu d^{b}p^{b}|V_{i}|/30}
.

Observe that the event
\sum_{\ell=1}^{j-1}Z^{(j)}_{\ell}>\tfrac{2}{5}|C^{\mathrm{buf}}(y_{j})|
contains the event that (
4.2
) fails for
j
, given that it did not fail for smaller
j
. Thus, taking a union bound over the
|Y|\leq n
values of
j
, we see that the probability that (
4.2
) fails at any stage is at most

n\cdot e^{-\mu d^{b}p^{b}|V_{i}|/30}\,,

which is smaller than one for all sufficiently large
n
by choice of
p
.

We conclude that there is a positive probability of choosing
|Y|
distinct vertices in
U
, so
|U|\geq|Y|
as desired.

∎

As previously mentioned, although the proof of Claim
4.2
constructs an auxiliary embedding, this embedding is not part of the final embedding of
H
into
G
, and exists only to verify the claim. The same goes for the following claim.

Claim 4.3
.

If
|Y|>|X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}|-\varrho|X_{i}|
, then
|U|\geq|Y|
.

Proof.

The vertices
V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus U
are candidates only for vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus Y
, and since
|V^{\prime}_{i}|=|X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}|
, what we need to show is that
|V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus U|\leq|X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus Y|
(as explained in detail in the proof of Lemma
3.4
).

Let
v\in V_{i}^{\prime}
be a vertex and let
C(v)
denote those vertices
x
from
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}

for which
v
is a candidate, i.e.
C(v)
denotes the neighbours of
v
in the candidate graph between

V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus U
and
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus Y
.
By
(PRGA 0)
, for each vertex
v
of
V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus U
we have

|C(v)|\geq\mu(d^{\Delta}p/100)^{b}|X_{i}|
. By
(PRGA 0)
, for all but at most
\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\Delta}|V_{i}|
vertices
v
of
V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus U
, we have

\Big|\Big\{v^{\prime}\in V_{i}\colon\big|\{x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}:v,v^{\prime}\in C(x)\}\big|>24\mu\Delta^{2}\big(\tfrac{20}{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}}\big)^{b}p^{2b}|X_{i}|\Big\}\Big|\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\Delta}|V_{i}|\,.

(4.4)

We choose an ordering
v_{1},\ldots,v_{|V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus U|}
of
V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus U
which puts the vertices
v
failing (
4.4
) first. We choose, for each
j=1,\ldots,|V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus U|
, a vertex
x_{j}
uniformly at random from the vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{j-1}\}
for which
v_{j}
is a candidate if this is possible; if not, we say
x_{j}
does not exist. As in the previous claim, our aim is to show that a.a.s. all
x_{j}
exist, which implies the claim.

Let
\mathscr{H}^{\prime}_{0}
be the empty history, and for each
1\leq j\leq|V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus U|
, let
\mathscr{H}^{\prime}_{j}
be the history of this process up to and including choosing
x_{j}
. We claim that, with positive probability, at each step
j
we choose from a set of size at least
\tfrac{1}{2}|C(v_{j})|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d^{\Delta}p/100)^{b}|X_{i}|
. This in particular implies that each
x_{j}
exists.

We introduce random variables
\tilde{Z}^{(j)}_{i}
for
j\in[|V_{i}^{\prime}\setminus U|]
and
\ell\in[j-1]
as follows.
We set
\tilde{Z}^{(j)}_{\ell}=1
if
x_{\ell}\in C(v_{j})
and

|C(v_{\ell})\setminus\{x_{1},\ldots,x_{\ell-1}\}|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}|C(v_{\ell})|
hold, and we set

\tilde{Z}^{(j)}_{\ell}=0
otherwise. Observe that
\sum_{\ell=1}^{j-1}\tilde{Z}^{(j)}_{\ell}
is exactly the number of vertices
x_{k}
among
x_{1}
, …,
x_{j-1}
which lie in

C(v_{j})
and which were chosen from a set of size at least
\tfrac{1}{2}|C(v_{k})|
.
Thus, to prove the claim it suffices to show that with positive probability, for every
j
we have

\sum_{\ell=1}^{j-1}\tilde{Z}^{(j)}_{\ell}<\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d^{\Delta}p/100)^{b}|X_{i}|\,.

(4.5)

If
v_{j}
is a vertex failing (
4.4
), then

j\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\Delta}|V_{i}|<\tfrac{1}{4}\mu(d^{\Delta}p/100)^{b}|X_{i}|
,
where the second inequality is by choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
.
Thus, even if all vertices
x_{1},\ldots,x_{j-1}
happen to be
candidates for
v_{j}
, we still have (
4.5
). Next we assume that
v_{j}

satisfies (
4.4
). For each of the at most

\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\Delta}|X_{i}|
vertices
v_{\ell}
such that

\big|\{x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}:v_{j},v_{\ell}\in C(x)\}\big|>24\mu\Delta^{2}\big(\tfrac{20}{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}}\big)^{b}p^{2b}|X_{i}|\,,

the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}(\tilde{Z}^{(j)}_{\ell}|\mathscr{H}^{\prime}_{\ell-1})
can be bounded from above by
1
,
while for the remaining vertices, it is at most

\frac{\big|\{x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}:v_{j},v_{\ell}\in C(x)\}\big|}{\tfrac{1}{2}|C(v_{\ell})|}\leq\frac{24\mu\Delta^{2}\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}\big)^{b}p^{2b}}{\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d^{\Delta}p/100)^{b}}\leq 48\Delta^{2}\big(\tfrac{2000}{\mu\zeta d^{2\Delta}}\big)^{\Delta}p^{b}\,.

This yields

\sum_{\ell=1}^{j-1}\mathbb{E}\big[\tilde{Z}^{(j)}_{\ell}\big|\mathscr{H}^{\prime}_{\ell-1}\big]\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\Delta}|X_{i}|+|V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus U|\cdot 48\Delta^{2}\big(\tfrac{2000}{\mu\zeta d^{2\Delta}}\big)^{\Delta}p^{b}\leq\tfrac{1}{4}\mu(d^{\Delta}p/100)^{b}|X_{i}|\,,

where the last inequality is because
|V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus U|\leq\varrho|X_{i}|
and by choice of
\varrho
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}
. As before, we apply Lemma
2.2
for each
j\in[|V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus U|]
with
\delta=1
and
(\tilde{Z}^{(j)})_{\ell\in[j-1]}
. We thus may bound the probability that
v_{j}
is candidate for more than
\tfrac{1}{2}|C(v_{i})|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d^{\Delta}p/100)^{b}|X_{i}|
of the vertices
x_{1},\ldots,x_{j-1}
by at most
e^{-\mu(d^{\Delta}p/100)^{b}|X_{i}|/12}
. Taking a union bound over the at most
\varrho|X_{i}|
choices of
j
we see that with probability at least
1-\varrho|X_{i}|\cdot e^{-\mu(d^{\Delta}p/100)^{b}|X_{i}|/12}>0
(where the inequality is by choice of
p
), at each step we choose from a set of size at least
\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d^{\Delta}p/100)^{b}|X_{i}|
as claimed. In particular, we succeed in choosing
|V^{\prime}_{i}\setminus U|
distinct vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\setminus Y
, so as desired we have
|U|\geq|Y|
.

∎

The final case is to show that
\varrho|X_{i}|<|Y|\leq|X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}|-\varrho|X_{i}|=|V^{\prime}_{i}|-\varrho|V_{i}|
and
|U|<|Y|
is a contradiction. In this case, we have
|V_{i}^{\prime}\setminus U|>\varrho|V_{i}|
. By
(PRGA 0)
there are at most

\varrho|X_{i}|
vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
with fewer than
\tfrac{1}{2}(dp)^{b}|V_{i}^{\prime}\setminus U|

candidates in
V_{i}^{\prime}\setminus U
, so in particular there is a vertex of
Y
with
candidates in
V_{i}^{\prime}\setminus U
, in contradiction to the definition of
U
.

This completes the verification of Hall’s condition, so we can extend

\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
to an embedding
\psi
of
H
into
G
as desired, completing the
proof of Lemma
1.25
.
∎

4.2.
Proof of the bijumbled graphs RGA lemma

The proof of Lemma
4.1
is very similar to the proof of Lemma
3.1
(RGA lemma for random graphs), and indeed we can reuse the auxiliary lemmas of Section
2.4
. We again use a random greedy algorithm, Algorithm
3
, which we show produces the desired embedding with high probability. The difference from Algorithm
1
is that when at time
t
we reach a vertex
x
in the order
\tau
(given again by Lemma
2.28
) which is in the queue
Q_{t}
, we do not skip it, but instead we embed it into
V^{\mathrm{q}}
. Thus, we always have
t=\tau(x)-1
.
The embedding is done uniformly at random into the set
A^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)\setminus B_{t}(x)
. We remind the reader that
B_{t}(x)

is the set of bad vertices with respect to
\psi_{t}
and
Q_{t}
, that is, the vertices
v
such that the extension
\psi_{t}\cup\{x\to v\}
is not a good partial embedding or there is an unembedded neighbour
y
of
x
not in
Q_{t}
such that
\deg_{G}\big(v;A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(y)\big)<(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(y)|
holds (see Definition
2.26
).

Input :

G\subseteq\Gamma
and
H
with partitions satisfying the General Setup; an ordering
\tau
on
X^{\mathrm{main}}

t:=0
;

\psi_{0}:=\emptyset
;

Q_{0}:=\{x\in V(H):|I_{x}|<\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon)^{|J_{x}|}p^{|J_{x}|}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(x)|\}
;

repeat

let
x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{t})
be the next vertex in
the order
\tau
;

if

x\in Q_{t}

and

|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)\setminus B_{t}(x)|<\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|V(x)|

then

halt with failure ;

end if

choose
v
uniformly at random in
\begin{cases}A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)\setminus B_{t}(x)\quad&\text{if $x\not\in Q_{t}$}\\
A^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)\setminus B_{t}(x)&\text{if $x\in Q_{t}$}\end{cases}
;

\psi_{t+1}:=\psi_{t}\cup\{x\to v\}
;

Q_{t+1}:=Q_{t}
;

forall

y\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{t+1})

do

if

(\big|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t+1}(y)\big|<\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{*}_{t+1}(y)}p^{\pi^{*}_{t+1}(y)}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(y)|)

then

Q_{t+1}:=Q_{t+1}\cup\{y\}
;

end if

end forall

t:=t+1
;

until

\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{t})=X^{\mathrm{main}}

;

t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}:=t
;

Algorithm 3

Random greedy algorithm for bijumbled graphs

Observe that there is another important difference from Algorithm
1
. Algorithm
3
can fail, and we will have to prove that with high probability it does not. This means proving that sets
A^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)
do not get small, or equivalently that sets
C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)
are never substantially covered by
\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})
. However, there is also an important similarity: provided the algorithm has not yet halted with failure, it maintains the same invariants as Algorithm
1
(see Claim
4.4
).

Observe that (in contrast to Algorithm
1
) if Algorithm
3
has not yet failed at time
t
, then all of the first
t
vertices in the order
\tau
have been embedded; the vertices are not reordered. Thus
\pi^{*}_{\tau(x)-1}(x)
is always equal to
\pi^{\tau}(x)
, where

\pi^{\tau}(x):=\big|\{y\in N_{H}(x):\tau(y)<\tau(x)\}\big|+|J_{x}|\,.

Much of the work of proving Lemma
4.1
is contained in the auxiliary lemmas in Section
2.4
which we use again here. What remains is to justify that Algorithm
3
with high probability does not halt with failure and does give a good partial embedding with properties
(PRGA 0)
and
(PRGA 0)
.

Proof of Lemma
4.1
.

We require

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{d^{\Delta}}{1320\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}}\,,

\displaystyle\varrho

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{\mu^{2}\zeta^{2}d^{2\Delta}}{2000}\,,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}\varrho}{1000\kappa\Delta^{2}\Delta_{R^{\prime}}4^{\Delta}}2^{-4/\varrho}\,,

\displaystyle\varepsilon

\displaystyle\leq\min\big((\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2},2^{-\Delta}\varepsilon^{\prime},\tfrac{\varrho\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}}{20\kappa\Delta},\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}}\big)\,,

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1000\Delta^{4}\kappa r_{1}^{2}}{\mu\zeta\varrho d^{\Delta}\varepsilon}\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/(2\Delta)}\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle 2

{}^{-\varepsilon pn/(\kappa r_{1})}r_{1}n<1\,.

Recall that we assumed that
\Gamma
possesses the three properties
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta+1)
,
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},\Delta+1)
and
\mathrm{LNS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
. Let
\tau
be an order on
V(H)
given by Lemma
2.28
. We run Algorithm
3
, which maintains the same invariants as Algorithm
1
, as shown in the following claim.

Claim 4.4
.

The following hold at each time
t
in the running of Algorithm
3
.

(
INV
0)

\psi_{t}
is a good partial embedding,

(
INV
0)

Either
|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)|\geq\frac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}p^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(x)|

or
x\in Q_{t}
.

(
INV
0)

When we embed
x
to create
\psi_{t+1}
, we do so
uniformly at random into a set of size at least
\frac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|V(x)|
.

Proof.

Algorithm
3
maintains
(INV 0)
and
(INV 0)
by definition. When we embed
x
to create
\psi_{t+1}
, either
x\in Q_{t}
or not. In the former case, because Algorithm
3
has not failed we have
(INV 0)
. In the latter case, in particular
x
was not added to
Q_{t}
at time
t-1
. Using Lemma
2.27
to bound the size of
B_{t}(x)
, and since
\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{b}p^{b}-20\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{b}>\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta d^{b}p^{b}
by choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
for each
0\leq b\leq\Delta
, also in this case Algorithm
3
maintains
(INV 0)
.

∎

As in the proof of Lemma
3.1
, the conditions for Lemma
2.29
are met, so we conclude that with probability at least
1-r_{1}2^{-n/(\kappa r_{1})}
, at each time
t
when Algorithm
3
is running and for each
i\in[r]
we have
|Q_{t}\cap X_{i}|\leq\varrho|X_{i}|+|X^{*}_{i}|\leq 2\varrho|X_{i}|
, where the final inequality is by
(G 0)
. In order to show that Algorithm
3
runs successfully, we need to show that the ‘halt with failure’ line is a.a.s. never reached, i.e. that if
x\in Q_{\tau(x)-1}
then
\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\setminus B_{\tau(x)-1}(x)|
is at least
\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{*}_{\tau(x)-1}(x)}|V_{i}|
. Since we know by Lemma
2.27
that
B_{\tau(x)-1}(x)
is small and by
(GPE 0)
that
C^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(x)-1}(x)
is large, what we want is to show that
\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t-1})
covers only a small fraction of
C^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(x)-1}(x)
. We will show this in Claim
4.6
. Before this, we argue in Claim
4.5
that in the embedding no ‘dense spots’ are created, which we need for Claim
4.6
.

We now explain how the following Claim
4.5
helps us to establish Claim
4.6
. Observe that we embed the vertices
Q_{t}\cap X_{i}
into
V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
in a random procedure which is very similar to the embedding strategy we used in the proof of Claim
4.2
(verification of Hall’s condition for small sets of buffer vertices). However, here a complication is that we do not know ‘in advance’ what
C^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(x)-1}(x)
will be when embedding earlier vertices to
V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}
. But we do know that it will be contained in the
\Gamma
-neighbourhood in
V_{i}
of some collection of
\pi^{\tau}(x)\leq\Delta
vertices, and that it will be large. Thus, it suffices to prove the following. For each
i
, each
1\leq\ell\leq\Delta
and
v_{1},\dots,v_{\ell}
such that
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V_{i})
is large,
\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})
never covers much of
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V_{i})
. We can prove this statement by using the same analysis as in the proof of Lemma
1.21
, given a ‘sum condition’ (in
( )
‣
4.5
below) similar to
(PRGA 0)
. We also show that if Algorithm
3
does not halt with failure then it is likely to have
(PRGA 0)
(which is
( )
‣
4.5
below), since the arguments are similar.

Claim 4.5
.

Suppose that for each
i\in[r]
and each time
t
we have
|Q_{t}\cap X_{i}|\leq 2\varrho|X_{i}|
. Then a.a.s. the following statements hold.

(

)

For any
t
, any
1\leq\ell\leq\Delta
, any
i\in[r]
and any vertices
v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell}
such that

\deg_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V_{i})\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\ell}|V_{i}|

we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}\colon\\
\tau(y)\leq t,J_{y}=\emptyset\end{subarray}}\frac{\big|U_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\cap N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V_{i})\big|}{\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\setminus B_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\big|}\leq\frac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}\deg_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V_{i})}{20}\,.

(4.6)

(

)

For any
t
, for any
1\leq\ell\leq\Delta
, any
i\in[r]
and any vertices
v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell}
such that

\deg_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i})\geq(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\ell}|V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}|\,,

we define
X^{\prime}_{i}
to be the set

\big\{y\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}:\big|U^{\mathrm{buf}}_{t}(y)\cap N^{*}_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i})\big|>(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}\deg_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i})\big\}

and we have

\sum_{y\in X^{\prime}_{i}}\frac{\big|U^{\mathrm{buf}}_{t}(y)\cap N^{*}_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i})\big|}{\big|C^{\mathrm{buf}}_{t}(y)\big|}\leq\frac{\deg_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i})}{20}\,.

(4.7)

The idea of the proof of (
4.6
) is as follows. The denominators on the left hand side of (
4.6
) are by
(INV 0)
never much smaller than they ‘should’ be, so the main task is to show that the numerators do not tend to be too large. To show this, we consider the evolution of
U_{t}(y)\cap N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\dots,v_{\ell};V_{i})
for some fixed
y
as
t
increases. Since
J_{y}=\emptyset
, at first this set has the size we expect, namely it is all of
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\dots,v_{\ell};V_{i})
. Each time a neighbour of
y
is embedded, we expect that the set size shrinks by a factor roughly
p
. If this is the case for each neighbour, the size at
t=\tau(y)-1
is roughly a
p^{\pi^{\tau}(y)}
-factor times its original size, which turns out to be a good enough bound for (
4.6
). If not, there is some first time when we embed a neighbour of
y
, say the
s
th neighbour, ‘badly’, that is, the set size does not shrink by a factor roughly
p
. We say we fail at step
s
. At worst, it could be that the set size does not thereafter change, so that it stays roughly a
p^{s-1}
-factor times its original size. In this case we ‘lose’ a
p^{\pi^{\tau}(y)-s+1}
factor. But the
\mathrm{LNS}
property tells us that the probability of failing at step
s
is less than
p^{\pi^{\tau}(y)-s+1}
. Heuristically, this gets us back the lost factor; to make this rigorous, we apply Lemma
2.2
.

Proof of Claim
4.5
.

We require

\varrho\leq\frac{\mu^{2}\zeta^{2}d^{2\Delta}}{2000}\,,\quad\varepsilon\leq\min\Big(2^{-\Delta}\varepsilon^{\prime},\frac{\varrho\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}}{20\kappa\Delta}\Big)\quad\text{and}\quad p\geq\frac{20\Delta^{4}\kappa r^{2}_{1}}{\varrho}\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/\Delta}\,.

We start with (
4.6
). Since the sum in (
4.6
) is monotonically increasing in
t
, and since the upper bound claimed in (
4.6
) does not depend on
t
, it suffices to show that the bound holds when Algorithm
3
terminates (with or without failure).

Given
1\leq\ell\leq\Delta
, let
v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell}
be vertices of
\Gamma
such that the set
U:=N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V_{i})
has size at least
\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\ell}|V_{i}|
. By
(INV 0)
we have the lower bound

\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\setminus B_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\big|\geq\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)}|V_{i}|

for the denominator in each summand of (
4.6
), and the difficulty is to upper bound the numerator. Consider the running of Algorithm
3
. At any time
t\leq\tau(y)-1
, the set
U^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(y)\cap U
is the
\Gamma
-neighbourhood in
U
of the
\pi^{*}_{t}(y)
embedded vertices from
N_{H}(y)
, and in a truly random set we would thus expect to find that

|U_{t}(y)\cap U|\leq(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}|U|\,.

(4.8)

If this inequality remains true up to
t=\tau(y)-1
, then since
|J_{y}|=0
by assumption, we have
\pi^{*}_{\tau(y)-1}(y)=\pi^{\tau}(y)
, and (
4.8
) gives an upper bound good enough for the
summand
\frac{\big|U_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\cap N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V_{i})\big|}{\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\setminus B_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\big|}
.
However, it is likely that some vertices will not satisfy (
4.8
), and we have to estimate their contribution to (
4.6
).

We say that a vertex
y\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}

fails at step
s
if the vertex
z
is the
s
th vertex of
N_{H}(y)
in
\tau
and
y
satisfies (
4.8
) for each
t\leq\tau(z)-1
but fails (
4.8
) at
t=\tau(z)
. Observe that each vertex
y
in the sum (
4.6
) satisfies (
4.8
) before any neighbour of
y
is embedded because
J_{y}=\emptyset
. Thus, if
y
does not fail at any step
s
with
1\leq s\leq\Delta
then it satisfies (
4.8
) at time
t=\tau(y)-1
, but if for some
1\leq s\leq\Delta
it fails at step
s
, then
|U_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\cap U|\leq(p+\varepsilon p)^{s-1}|U|
.

Suppose now that
y
fails at step
s
. Then the reason is that the vertex
z
, which is the
s
th neighbour of
y
in
\tau
, is embedded to a vertex of
\Gamma
with more neighbours in
U_{\tau(z)-1}(y)\cap U
than allowed by (
4.8
). Let
W
be a superset of
U_{\tau(z)-1}(y)\cap U
of size
(p+\varepsilon p)^{s-1}|U|
. By choice of
\varepsilon
we have
|W|\geq\varepsilon p^{\ell+s-1}|V_{i}|
. Because
y
fails at step
s
we see that
z
is embedded to a vertex
v
of
\Gamma
with
\deg_{\Gamma}(v;W)>(p+\varepsilon p)^{s}|U|=(p+\varepsilon p)|W|
. By
\mathrm{LNS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
we know that the number of such vertices
v
in
\Gamma
is at most

\varepsilon p^{2\Delta-s}|V(\Gamma)|/r_{1}^{2}
(since
\ell\leq\Delta
). Therefore, by
(INV 0)
, the probability of embedding
z
(which has at least one unembedded neighbour and hence at most
\Delta-1
embedded neighbours) to such a vertex
v
, conditioning on the history up to but not including embedding
z
, is at most

\frac{\varepsilon p^{2\Delta-s}|V(\Gamma)|/r_{1}^{2}}{\mu\zeta(dp)^{\Delta-1}|V_{i}|/10}\leq\frac{\varrho}{2\Delta}p^{\Delta-s+1}\leq\frac{\varrho}{2\Delta}p^{\pi^{\tau}(y)-s+1}\,,

where the first inequality is by choice of
\varepsilon
and the second since
\pi^{\tau}(y)\leq\Delta
.

We can restate this as: the probability that
y
fails at step
s=\pi^{\tau}(y)-j+1
, conditioning on the history up to but not including the embedding of the vertex at step
s
, is at most
\tfrac{\varrho}{2\Delta}p^{j}
for each
1\leq j\leq\pi^{\tau}(y)
. It follows that the expected number of vertices
y\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
which fail at step
\pi^{\tau}(y)-j+1
is at most
\tfrac{\varrho}{2\Delta}p^{j}|X_{i}|
, and by Lemma
2.2
, applied with
\delta=1
, the probability that more than
\tfrac{\varrho}{\Delta}p^{j}|X_{i}|
vertices
y\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
fail at step
\pi^{\tau}(y)-j+1
is at most
\exp\big(-\tfrac{\varrho}{6\Delta}p^{j}|X_{i}|\big)
. Let
\mathcal{E}
be the event that, for each
1\leq j\leq\Delta
, at most
\tfrac{\varrho}{\Delta}p^{j}|X_{i}|
vertices
y
of
X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
fail at step
\pi^{\tau}(y)-j+1
. The probability that
\mathcal{E}
holds is thus at least

1-\Delta\exp\big(-\tfrac{\varrho}{6\Delta}p^{\Delta}|X_{i}|\big)\,.

(4.9)

Suppose
\mathcal{E}
occurs. Since a vertex
y
failing at step
j
satisfies

|U_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\cap U|\leq(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)-j}|U|

we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}:J_{y}=\emptyset,\\
|U_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\cap U|>(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)}|U|\end{subarray}}\frac{\big|U_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\cap U\big|}{\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\setminus B_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\big|}\\
\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}:\\
y\text{ fails at step }s=\pi^{\tau}(y)-j+1\end{subarray}}\frac{(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)-j}|U|}{\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)}|V_{i}|}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\Delta}\frac{(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)-j}|U|\cdot\tfrac{\varrho}{\Delta}p^{j}|X_{i}|}{\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)}|V_{i}|}\\
\leq\frac{20\varrho|U|}{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}}\leq\frac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}|U|}{40}\,,

(4.10)

where the last two inequalities are by choice of
\varepsilon
and
\varrho
respectively.
On the other hand, because
|X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}|\leq 2\varrho|X_{i}|
, we have

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}:\\
|U_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\cap U|\leq(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)}|U|\end{subarray}}\frac{\big|U_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\cap U\big|}{\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\setminus B_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\big|}\leq\\
\sum_{y\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}}\frac{(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)}|U|}{\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)}|V_{i}|}&\leq\frac{(1+\varepsilon)^{\Delta}|U|\cdot 2\varrho|X_{i}|}{\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}|V_{i}|}\\
&\leq\frac{40\varrho|U|}{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}}\leq\frac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}|U|}{40}\,,\end{split}

and putting these together we conclude (
4.6
).

It remains to bound the probability that for some vertices
v_{1}
, …,
v_{\ell}
and for some
i
with

\deg_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V_{i})\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\ell}|V_{i}|
the inequality (
4.6
)
is violated, in other words that
\mathcal{E}
fails for
v_{1}
, …,
v_{\ell}
and
i
.
There are
r\leq r_{1}
choices of
i
and at most
n+n^{2}+\dots+n^{\Delta}\leq\Delta n^{\Delta}
choices of
v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell}
, so by the union bound and (
4.9
) the probability of such a bad event occurring is at most

r_{1}\Delta n^{\Delta}\cdot 2\Delta\exp\big(-\tfrac{\varrho}{6\Delta\kappa r_{1}}p^{\Delta}n\big)

which tends to zero as
n
tends to infinity by choice of
p
.

We now come to (
4.7
). This inequality looks very much like (
4.10
) above, and its proof is almost identical to the proof of (
4.10
). The differences are that we consider
G
-neighbourhoods in
V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
not
\Gamma
-neighbourhoods in
V_{i}
and that we have a lower bound on
C^{\mathrm{buf}}_{t}(x)
from
(GPE 0)
rather than on
|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)\setminus B_{t}(x)|
from
(INV 0)
. Since this lower bound is larger, the same constant choices work. We omit the details.

∎

We can now use Lemma
2.2
to complete the proof that the sets
C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)
do not get covered by
\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})
, much as in Claim
4.2
(verification of Hall’s condition for small sets of buffer vertices).

Claim 4.6
.

A.a.s. for each
x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}
, at each time
t\leq\tau(x)-1
and before the termination of Algorithm
3
, we have
\big|C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)\cap\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})\big|<\tfrac{1}{2}\big|C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)\big|
.

Proof.

We require

\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq 2^{-2\Delta},\qquad\varrho\leq\tfrac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}}{30}\qquad\text{and}\qquad p\geq\tfrac{1000r_{1}}{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}}\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/\Delta}\,.

Suppose that the conclusions of Claim
4.5
hold (in particular (
4.6
) holds). Given
i\in[r]
and
x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
, if
t\leq\tau(x)-1
and Algorithm
3
has not terminated before time
t
, then by
(GPE 0)
and
(G 0)
we have
\big|C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)\big|\geq(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})\mu\zeta(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|V_{i}|
. Since
C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)\subseteq U_{t}(x)=N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V_{i})
for some vertices
v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell}
with
\ell=\pi^{*}_{t}(x)\leq\Delta
, we conclude by (
4.6
) that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}\colon\\
\tau(y)\leq t,J_{y}=\emptyset\end{subarray}}\frac{\big|U^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\cap C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)\big|}{\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\setminus B_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\big|}\leq\frac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}\big|U_{t}(x)\big|}{20}\,.

Observe that the summand in the above inequality is an upper bound for the probability that
y
is embedded to
C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)
for
y\in Q_{t}
, conditioning on the history up to but not including the embedding of
y
. Since the probability that
y
is embedded to
C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)
is zero if
y
is not in
Q_{t}
, we are in a position to apply Lemma
2.2
. This lemma, with
\delta=1
, tells us that the probability that more than
\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}\big|U_{t}(x)\big|
vertices
y
of
X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
with
J_{y}=\emptyset
are embedded to
C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)
is at most
\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{60}\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}|U_{t}(x)|\big)
. If this bad event does not occur, then by
(GPE 0)
,
(GPE 0)
and
(G 0)
, and because the number of vertices in
X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
with
J_{y}\neq\emptyset
is by
(G 0)
at most
\varrho p^{\Delta}|X_{i}|
, we have the desired statement:

\big|C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)\cap\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})\big|<\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}\big|U_{t}(x)\big|+\varrho p^{\Delta}|X_{i}|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})\mu\zeta(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|V_{i}|.

The probability that the conclusions of Claim
4.5
fail to hold, or that any of the above bad events occur, is at most
o(1)+r_{1}n^{2}\cdot\exp\big(-\tfrac{1}{60r_{1}}\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}(p-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\Delta}n\big)
, which tends to zero as
n
tends to infinity by choice of
p
, completing the proof.

∎

If the conclusions of Lemma
2.29
, Claim
4.5
and
Claim
4.6
hold (which we think of as being good events),
then by
(GPE 0)
and Lemma
2.27
we have

\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\setminus B_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\big|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\mu\zeta(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}|V_{i}|-20\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}|V_{i}|

and the right hand side is by choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
at least
\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}|V_{i}|
. In other words, the ‘halt with failure’ line of Algorithm
3
is never reached. Since each of the three good events a.a.s. occurs, the algorithm a.a.s. completes successfully. Now
(PRGA 0)
is guaranteed by successful completion of Algorithm
3
, and
(PRGA 0)
is as
(RGA 0)
in the proof of Lemma
3.1
implied by the good event of Lemma
2.29
, while
(PRGA 0)
is given by the good event of Claim
4.5
, specifically taking (
4.7
) with
t=t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}
and vertices
\psi_{t}\big(N_{H}(x)\big)
for each
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
.

Next we establish that
(PRGA 0)
a.a.s. holds. We apply Lemma
2.30
with
B=\Delta+1
and
T=\tau(z)
where
z
is the last vertex in
N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
. Since
N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
forms the initial segment of
\tau
by Lemma
2.28
( )
‣
2.28
, we see that all vertices
y
embedded up to time
T
have
\pi^{\tau}(y)\leq\Delta-1\leq B-2
, and by identical logic as in Claim
3.6
none of these vertices enter the queue. Therefore, by
(GPE 0)
, Lemma
2.27
and choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, each
y
from
N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
is embedded uniformly at random to a set of size at least
\tfrac{1}{10}(dp)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)}|V(y)|
. Finally, by
(H 0)
we have
|N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})\cap X_{i}|\leq 4\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}\mu|X_{i}|
for each
i\in[r]
. This justifies that the conditions of Lemma
2.30
are met, so we conclude that a.a.s. the following event
\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:large_nbs}}}}}
occurs. For each
v\in V_{i}
and
j
such that
ij\in E(R^{\prime})
we have
\big|N_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}_{j})\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{T})\big|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\deg_{G}(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}_{j})
.

We are now in a position to apply Lemma
2.31
, again with
B=\Delta+1
. Observe that this time, if
\mathcal{E}_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{lem:large_nbs}}}}}
occurs, it applies to any
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
and
v\in V_{i}
, whether or not
x
is in a clique buffer. The deduction that a.a.s. the embedding
\psi_{t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}}
has
(PRGA 0)
follows exactly as in the proof of Lemma
3.1
(RGA lemma), and we do not repeat it.

It remains to establish
(PRGA 0)
. For this we require the following claim, which shows that if vertices
v
and
v^{\prime}
are common candidates for too many
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
for
(PRGA 0)
, then the reason is that they have an exceptionally large common
\Gamma
-neighbourhood in some cluster.

Claim 4.7
.

Asymptotically almost surely at the termination of Algorithm
3
the following holds. For each
i
and pair of vertices
v,v^{\prime}\in V_{i}
such that
\deg_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime};V_{j})\leq(p+\varepsilon p)^{2}|V_{j}|
whenever
ij\in R^{\prime}
, if
b
is such that
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a degree-
b
buffer, then we have

\big|\{x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}:v,v^{\prime}\in C(x)\}\big|\leq 24\mu\Delta^{2}\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}\big)^{b}p^{2b}|X_{i}|\,.

(4.11)

To prove this we show that for each
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, however, previous vertices are embedded, it is not too likely that
N_{H}(x)
is embedded to
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})
, and apply Lemma
2.2
and the union bound to deduce (
4.11
) for all
i
,
v
, and
v^{\prime}
. In turn, to prove the desired upper bound on the probability of embedding
N_{H}(x)
to
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})
, we analyse the embedding of the vertices
N_{H}(x)=\{y_{1},\dots,y_{b}\}
one by one. We would expect that in each case roughly a
p^{2}
-fraction of
U(y_{i})
is contained in
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})
, and if this is the case at each step we obtain the desired upper bound. If not, the reason is that a previously embedded vertex—which must be one of the
y_{j}
since we have not yet embedded any other neighbouring vertices—was ‘badly embedded’. Using the
\mathrm{LNS}
property, we show this is an unlikely event and again obtain the desired upper bound. In fact, this is a slight oversimplification: we have to separate the cases that exactly one vertex is embedded badly (which implies we still have only at worst a probability
p
