of embedding future vertices of
N_{H}(x)
to
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})
) or that more than one vertex is embedded badly, in which case we might have probability
1
of embedding future vertices of
N_{H}(x)
to
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})
, but this is counterbalanced by the unlikelihood of embedding two vertices badly.

Proof of Claim
4.7
.

We require

\varepsilon\leq\min\big(2^{-\Delta},\tfrac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}}{10\Delta\kappa}\big)\quad\text{and}\quad p\geq 20\mu^{-1}\kappa r_{1}\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/(2\Delta)}\,.

Let
v,v^{\prime}\in V_{i}
be such that
\deg_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime};V_{j})\leq(p+\varepsilon p)^{2}|V_{j}|
for each
j
such that
ij\in R^{\prime}
. Suppose that
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a degree-
b
buffer and
b\in[\Delta]
.

Let
T=\tau(x)-|N_{H}(x)|-1
be the time immediately before the first vertex of
N_{H}(x)
is embedded. Since any two vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
are at distance at least ten in
H
by
(H 0)
, it is enough to show

(
\ast
)

for any one vertex
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, by considering the embedding of
N_{H}(x)
, that the probability of
v,v^{\prime}\in C(x)
, conditioning on the history up to time
T
, is at most
3\Delta^{2}\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}\big)^{b}p^{2b}
.

If we show this, then, since the vertices of
N_{H}(x)
are embedded consecutively, by Lemma
2.2
, applied with
\delta=1
and using
|X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}|=4\mu|X_{i}|
, we see that (
4.11
) holds with probability at least
1-\exp\big(-4\mu\Delta^{2}\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}\big)^{b}p^{2b}|X_{i}|\big)
, and taking a union bound over the at most
n^{2}
choices of
v,v^{\prime}
we conclude that by choice of
p
the conclusion of the claim a.a.s. holds as desired.

We show that the probability of
v,v^{\prime}\in C(x)
is at most
3\Delta^{2}\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}\big)^{b}p^{2b}
, conditioning on any
\psi_{T}
. Observe that
v,v^{\prime}\in C(x)
can occur only if
N_{H}(x)
is embedded into
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})
. If
y\in N_{H}(x)\cap X_{j}
, then the probability that
y
is embedded into
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})
is, using
(INV 0)
, at most

\frac{\big|U_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\cap N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})\big|}{\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)}|V_{j}|}\,.

We would expect that the numerator is at most
(p+\varepsilon p)^{2+\pi^{\tau}(y)}|V(y)|
for all
y\in N_{H}(x)
. Of course, this does not always happen, since it may be the case that a neighbour
z
of
y
in
H
with
\tau(z)<\tau(y)
was embedded
badly
, that is, to a vertex with more than

(p+\varepsilon p)\left|U_{\tau(z)-1}(y)\cap N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})\right|

(4.12)

neighbours in
U_{\tau(z)-1}(y)\cap N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})
.
Since
\tau
satisfies the condition of Lemma
2.28
( )
‣
2.28
, any such
z
is in
N(X^{\mathrm{buf}})
. Since
x
is at distance at most
2
from
z
in
H
, and at distance at least
5
from any other vertex of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
by
(H 0)
, we see that
z\in N_{H}(x)
. Thus, if exactly
s
many neighbours
z
of
y
with
\tau(z)<\tau(y)
are embedded badly then
we see that with (
4.12
) the following inequality

\big|U_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\cap N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})\big|\leq(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)-s}|N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})|\leq(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)+2-s}|V(y)|

(4.13)

holds.
We now show how this implies
(

∗

)
‣
4.2
. We separate three cases.

First, no vertices in
N_{H}(x)
are embedded badly. Then the probability that all
b
vertices in
N_{H}(x)
are embedded into
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})
, conditioning on
\psi_{T}
, is at most
\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}p^{2}\big)^{b}
by choice of
\varepsilon
.

Second, exactly one vertex in
N_{H}(x)
is embedded badly. We let the vertices of
N_{H}(x)
be
y_{1},\ldots,y_{b}
in order of
\tau
. Let us suppose that
y_{\ell}
is the vertex embedded badly. Then the probability that the first
\ell-1
vertices of
N_{H}(x)
are embedded into
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})
, conditioning on
\psi_{T}
, is at most
\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}p^{2}\big)^{\ell-1}
by choice of
\varepsilon
(since these are not badly embedded and conditional probabilities multiply).

We now estimate the probability of
y_{\ell}
being embedded badly, conditioning on
\psi_{T}
and on the embeddings of the previous
\ell-1
vertices not being bad. Observe that, since there has been no previous bad embedding, we have for each
\ell^{\prime}>\ell

\big|U_{\tau(y_{\ell})-1}(y_{\ell^{\prime}})\cap N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})\big|\leq(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi_{\tau(y_{\ell})-1}(y_{\ell^{\prime}})+2}|V(y_{\ell^{\prime}})|\,,

and since
\pi_{\tau(y_{\ell})-1}(y_{\ell^{\prime}})\leq\ell-1\leq\Delta-2
, the right hand side is, by choice of
\varepsilon
, at least
\varepsilon p^{\Delta}n/r_{1}
. By
\mathrm{LNS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
the number of vertices in
\Gamma
with more than

(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi_{\tau(y_{\ell})-1}(y_{\ell^{\prime}})+3}|V(y_{\ell^{\prime}})|
neighbours into
U_{\tau(y_{\ell})-1}(y_{\ell^{\prime}})\cap N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})

is at most
\varepsilon p^{2\Delta-1}n/r_{1}
. Since
\pi^{\tau}(y_{\ell})\leq\ell-1
, by
(INV 0)
the probability that
y_{\ell}
is embedded to such a bad vertex is at most

\frac{\Delta\varepsilon p^{2\Delta-1}n/r_{1}}{\mu\zeta(dp)^{\ell-1}|V(y_{\ell})|/10}\leq p^{2\Delta-\ell}

where the inequality is by choice of
\varepsilon
.

Now, the probability that the last
b-\ell
vertices are embedded into
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})
, conditioning on the previous embeddings, is at most
\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}p\big)^{b-\ell}
by choice of
\varepsilon
. Indeed,
for each vertex
\ell^{\prime}>\ell
we have by (
4.13
) that

\big|U_{\tau(y_{\ell^{\prime}})-1}(y_{\ell^{\prime}})\cap N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})\big|\leq(p+\varepsilon p)^{\pi^{\tau}(y_{\ell^{\prime}})+1}|V(y_{\ell^{\prime}})|

since we condition on exactly one neighbour of
y_{\ell^{\prime}}
being embedded badly. This gives a conditional probability of at most
20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}p
that
v_{\ell^{\prime}}
is embedded to
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})
. The conditional probabilities multiply, and taking the union bound over the choices of
\ell
, we see that the probability of this case occurring and all vertices of
N_{H}(x)
being embedded to
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})
, conditioning on
\psi_{T}
, is at most

\Delta\cdot\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}p^{2}\big)^{\ell-1}\cdot p^{2\Delta-\ell}\cdot\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}p\big)^{b-\ell}\leq\\
\Delta\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}\big)^{b-1}p^{2\Delta+b-2}\leq\Delta^{2}\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}\big)^{b}p^{2b},

where the last inequality follows since
\Delta\geq 2
and
b\leq\Delta
.

In the third case, at least two vertices in
N_{H}(x)
are embedded badly. Suppose that the first two badly embedded vertices are the
j
th and
k
th vertices. Then the same logic as above, in particular the inequality (
4.13
), tells us that the probability that this case occurs and all vertices of
N_{H}(x)
are embedded to
N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v,v^{\prime})
is at most

\Delta^{2}\cdot\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}p^{2}\big)^{j-1}\cdot p^{2\Delta-j}\cdot\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}p\big)^{k-j-1}\cdot p^{2\Delta-k}\leq\\
\Delta^{2}\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}\big)^{b-2}p^{4\Delta-3}.

Putting these three cases together, and using the fact that
4\Delta-3\geq 2b
since
\Delta\geq 2
and
b\leq\Delta
, we conclude that the probability that
v,v^{\prime}\in C(x)
is at most
3\Delta^{2}\big(20\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-\Delta}\big)^{b}p^{2b}
as desired.

∎

Suppose now that the good event of Claim
4.7
holds. For each
i\in[r]
, there may be exceptional vertices
v\in V_{i}
which have more than
(p+\varepsilon p)|V_{j}|
neighbours in
V_{j}
for some
j
such that
ij\in R^{\prime}
, but by
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta+1)
there are at most
\Delta_{R^{\prime}}\varepsilon p^{\Delta}n/r_{1}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\Delta}|V_{i}|
such vertices. If
v
is not exceptional—that is, it has at most
(p+\varepsilon)|V_{j}|
neighbours in each
V_{j}
with
ij\in R^{\prime}
—then again there may be vertices
v^{\prime}\in V_{i}
which are exceptional for
v
, i.e. vertices
v^{\prime}
with more than
(p+\varepsilon p)^{2}|V_{j}|
common neighbours with
v
in
V_{j}
for some
j
such that
ij\in R^{\prime}
. But again by
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta+1)
there are at most
\Delta_{R^{\prime}}\varepsilon p^{\Delta}n/r_{1}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\Delta}|V_{i}|
such. Because the good event of Claim
4.7
holds, for non-exceptional pairs of vertices
v,v^{\prime}
we have the bound given in
(PRGA 0)
. Because the good event of Claim
4.7
holds a.a.s., property
(PRGA 0)
is a.a.s. obtained. This completes the proof of Lemma
4.1
.
∎

We note that it is Claim
4.5
in this proof which is most responsible for our bijumbledness requirement on
\Gamma
in the blow-up lemma for bijumbled graphs, Lemma
1.25
. Although the proof of this claim may seem quite wasteful—we assume that if one neighbour is embedded badly then potentially all future neighbours can be embedded badly without any further penalty, which seems unreasonable—we were not able to make it work with any weaker condition than
\mathrm{LNS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},\Delta)
for general graphs
H
. Our analysis is in some sense tight for vertices
y
which fail at step
\pi^{\tau}(y)
, when the final neighbour
z
of
y
coming before
y
in
\tau
is embedded. What still seems unreasonable is that all the vertices in
\Gamma
which have exceptionally high degree to
U_{\tau(z)-1}(y)\cap U
turn out to be in
A_{\tau(z)-1}(z)
, which is the worst case we are effectively using in our proof. We expect that a more careful analysis, possibly involving some modification to Algorithm
3
, would allow one to improve on this and thus improve on the bijumbledness requirement of Lemma
1.25
.

Chapter 5
Improved bounds for degenerate graphs

5.1.
The RGA lemma and the proof of the blow-up lemma

In this section we prove Lemma
1.23
. We have already seen most of the ideas in the proofs of Lemmas
1.21
and
1.25
. We use the same General Setup, and we continue to obtain it using Lemma
2.22
. However, rather than defining an order
\tau
putting the buffer vertices
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
at the end and their neighbours at the front, as we did in the proofs of Lemmas
3.1
and
4.1
, we are supplied with an order
\tau
which we will modify only in that we move
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
to the end of the order. In particular, the neighbours of buffer vertices need not appear early in
\tau
. The reason for this is that moving vertices in the order could result in substantially increasing
\pi^{\tau}(x)
for some vertices
x
. Moving the vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
to the end of the order can destroy boundedness of the order, but it turns out that the only vertices which cause boundedness to fail are those in
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
, and in the proof the potentially failing conditions are not needed for these vertices. The following definition, which extends
(D,p,m)
-boundedness, accommodates this.

Definition 5.1

(
(D,p,m)
-bounded order)
.

Let
H
be a graph given with buffer sets
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}=\{\tilde{X}_{i}\}_{i\in[r]}
and
a restriction pair
\mathcal{I}=\{I_{x}\}_{x\in V(H)}
and
\mathcal{J}=\{J_{x}\}_{x\in V(H)}
.
Let
\tilde{X}=\bigcup_{i\in[r]}\tilde{X}_{i}
.
Let
\tau
be an ordering of
V(H)
and
X^{e},X^{\mathrm{buf}}\subseteq V(H)
.
Then
\tau
is a
(D,p,m)
-bounded order
for
H
,
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}
,

\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{J}

with
exceptional set

X^{e}
and
ignored set

X^{\mathrm{buf}}
if the following conditions are
satisfied for each
x\in V(H)
.

(
ORD
0’)

Define

D_{x}:=\begin{cases}D-2&\text{if there is $yz\in E(H)$ with $y,z\in N_{H}(x)$ and $\tau(y),\tau(z)>\tau(x)$}\\
D-1&\text{else if there is $y\in N_{H}(x)$ with $\tau(y)>\tau(x)$}\\
D&\text{otherwise}\,.\end{cases}

We have
\pi^{\tau}(x)\leq D_{x}
, and if
x\in N(\tilde{X})\setminus X^{\mathrm{buf}}
even
\pi^{\tau}(x)\leq D_{x}-1
. Finally, if
x\in\tilde{X}
we have
\deg(x)\leq D
.

(
ORD
0’)

One of the following holds:

•

x\in X^{e}\cup X^{\mathrm{buf}}
,

•

\pi^{\tau}(x)\leq\frac{1}{2}D
,

•

x
is not image restricted and every neighbour
y
of
x

with
\tau(y)<\tau(x)
satisfies
\tau(x)-\tau(y)\leq p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}m
.

(
ORD
0’)

If
x\in N(\tilde{X})\setminus X^{\mathrm{buf}}
then all but at most
D-1-\max_{z\not\in X^{e}}\pi^{\tau}(z)

neighbours
y
of
x
with
\tau(y)<\tau(x)
satisfy

\tau(x)-\tau(y)\leq p^{D}m
.

Observe that the only difference between Definitions
1.22
and
5.1
is the introduction of the ignored set
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
, and conditions
(ORD 0’)
and
(ORD 0’)
, and part of
(ORD 0’)
, are changed so that they are trivially satisfied by vertices of the ignored set
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
. If the ignored set is empty, then Definition
1.22
is recovered.

We will use a modification of Algorithm
3
to perform this embedding. The modification consists of handling the exceptional vertices
X^{e}
differently, which allows us to deal with a few vertices
x
with
\pi^{\tau}(x)
significantly larger than normal; such vertices appear in applications. We can show that this algorithm succeeds in embedding all the vertices of
X^{\mathrm{main}}
, in the order
\tau
, and that it returns a good partial embedding with the properties detailed in the lemma below with positive probability. Since we will have
X^{\mathrm{main}}=V(H)\setminus X^{\mathrm{buf}}
, we can complete the proof using Lemma
3.4
to embed the buffer vertices in much the same way as in the proof of Lemma
1.21
.

Note that property
\mathrm{LCON}
, which appears in the following lemma, plays the same rôle as
\mathrm{LNS}
in the proof of Lemma
4.1
. It is easier to work with, and allows for linearly many image restrictions (but it is not in general true in bijumbled graphs).

Lemma 5.2

(degenerate RGA lemma)
.

We assume the General Setup. Suppose that an exceptional set
X^{e}
with
|X^{e}|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon p^{\max_{x\in X^{e}}\pi^{\tau}(x)}n/r_{1}
is given. Suppose that
D\geq 1
, and
\tau
is a
\big(D,p,\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon n/r_{1}\big)
-bounded order for

,
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}
,
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{J}
with the exceptional set

and ignored set

. Suppose furthermore that the vertices
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
form the final segment of
\tau
. Suppose that
\Gamma
has properties
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)
,
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},D)
and
\mathrm{LCON}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)
. Then there is a good partial embedding
\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
of

into
G
with the following

. Let

be such that

(
DRGA
0)

Every vertex in

by

, and no vertex in
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is embedded.

(
DRGA
0)

For every set
W\subseteq V_{i}
of size at least

\varrho|V_{i}|

vertices in

with fewer than

(

Every vertex in

4^{-10\Delta^{3}}2^{-1000D^{2}\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-D}}d^{2D^{2}}\Delta^{-3}\mu p^{b}|X_{i}|

vertices of

.

We will prove this lemma in Section
5.2
. Assuming Lemma

, we are in a position to prove Lemma

. This amounts to a verification that the conditions of Lemma

suffice to apply Lemmas

,

and

Proof of Lemma
1.23
.

First we choose constants as follows. Given
\Delta
,
\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{BL}}
,
\Delta_{J}
and
D
integers,
\alpha^{\mathrm{BL}}
,
\zeta^{\mathrm{BL}}
and

, and
\kappa^{\mathrm{BL}}>1
, we set
\vartheta=0
,
\Delta_{R^{\prime}}=8(\Delta+\Delta_{J})^{10}\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{BL}}
,
\alpha=\tfrac{1}{2}\alpha^{\mathrm{BL}}
,

and
\kappa=2\kappa^{\mathrm{BL}}
. We now choose

\mu=\tfrac{\alpha}{20000\kappa\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{4}\Delta^{10}(\Delta+2)}\,,\quad\varrho=\tfrac{\mu^{2}\zeta^{2}d^{3D^{2}}}{10000\kappa(\Delta+1)}4^{-2000\Delta^{3}\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-D}}\\
\text{and}\quad\varepsilon^{\prime}=\tfrac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta+1}}{1000\Delta^{4}\kappa 4^{\Delta}}2^{-280(D+1)\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-D}}\,.\\

Now for input
D
and
d,\varepsilon^{\prime}
, Lemma
2.17
returns constants
(\varepsilon_{a,b})
and
\varepsilon_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{det_gnp:randri}}}}}>0
. We set

\varepsilon=\min\big(\varepsilon_{\textsc{{l\tiny{\ref{det_gnp:randri}}}}},\tfrac{d\varepsilon^{\prime}}{\kappa}\big)\,.

We let
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{BL}}=\tfrac{1}{16}(\Delta+\Delta_{J})^{-10}\varepsilon
and
\varrho^{\mathrm{BL}}=\tfrac{1}{16}(\Delta+\Delta_{J})^{-10}\varrho
.
Now Lemma
1.23
returns
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{BL}}
and
\varrho^{\mathrm{BL}}
. Given

we let

. We choose
C
sufficiently large for Lemma

with input

,

,
\varepsilon^{\prime}
,

and

, and such that

Given

, Lemma
2.17
states that a.a.s.

has the properties
\mathrm{NS}\big(\varepsilon,r_{1},D\big)
,
\mathrm{RI}\big(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},D\big)
,

and
\mathrm{LCON}\big(\varepsilon,r_{1},D\big)
respectively. From now on we will assume
\Gamma
is an
n
-vertex graph which satisfies these four properties.

Given a graph
R_{\mathrm{BL}}
on
r_{\mathrm{BL}}\leq r_{1}^{\mathrm{BL}}

with
\Delta(R^{\prime}_{\mathrm{BL}})\leq\Delta_{R^{\prime}}^{\mathrm{BL}}
, and graphs

and

and

, a family of potential buffer vertices

, and an exceptional set

, suppose that the conditions of Lemma
1.23
are satisfied. Then Lemma
2.22
gives a graph

on

vertices, a spanning subgraph

with
\Delta(R^{\prime})\leq\Delta_{R^{\prime}}
, and
\kappa
-balanced size-compatible partitions
{\mathcal{X}}
and

of

and
G
respectively, each part having size at least

, together with a family

of potential buffer vertices and
\mathcal{I}
of image restrictions, subsets

of

for each
i\in[r]
, and partitions
V_{i}=V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}\mathbin{\dot{\cup}}V^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
for each
i\in[r]
which satisfy the General Setup.

We now modify the provided
(D,p,\varepsilon^{\mathrm{BL}}n/r_{1}^{\mathrm{BL}})
-bounded order
\tau^{\mathrm{BL}}
on
V(H)
by moving the set
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
to the end of the order, obtaining a new order
\tau
. We claim
\tau
is
(D,p,\varepsilon^{\mathrm{BL}}n/r_{1}^{\mathrm{BL}})
-bounded for
H
,
\mathcal{\tilde{X}}^{\mathrm{BL}}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{BL}}
and
\mathcal{J}
with exceptional set
X^{e}
and ignored set
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
. To see this, observe that if
x\not\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}
then
\pi^{\tau}(x)\leq\pi^{\tau^{BL}}(x)
. It is easy to check from this that vertices not in
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
satisfy all three conditions. If
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}
then
\pi^{\tau}(x)=\deg(x)\leq D
by
(ORD 0)
, since
X^{\mathrm{buf}}\subseteq\tilde{X}
, and since
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
is an independent set by
(H 0)
it follows that vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
satisfy
(ORD 0’)
. Finally vertices in
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
trivially satisfy
(ORD 0’)
and
(ORD 0’)
.

Since
\varepsilon^{\mathrm{BL}}n/r_{1}^{\mathrm{BL}}=\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon n/r_{1}
, the order
\tau
is also
\big(D,p,\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon n/r_{1}\big)
-bounded for
H
,
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}
,
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{J}
with exceptional set
X^{e}
and ignored set
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
. To see this observe that the set
\mathcal{I}
and the specific partition
\tilde{\mathcal{X}}
of
\tilde{X}
do not play a rôle in Definition
5.1
(the former comes together with the set
\mathcal{J}
, which does play a rôle, and the latter is important only in that it defines
\tilde{X}
).

We let
X^{\mathrm{main}}=V(H)\setminus X^{\mathrm{buf}}
. We now begin the embedding of
H
into
G
.
By Lemma
5.2
, there is a good partial embedding
\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
with the properties stated in that lemma.
By
(GPE 0)
, condition
(BUF 0)
of Lemma
3.4
is satisfied, while
condition
(BUF 0)
holds by
(DRGA 0)
and
(BUF 0)
follows from
(DRGA 0)
with

\delta=4^{-10\Delta^{3}}2^{-1000D^{2}\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-D}}d^{2D^{2}}\Delta^{-3}\mu\,.

Therefore, we can find an embedding
\psi^{\prime}
extending
\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}}
which embeds
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
to
V_{i}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{\mathrm{RGA}})
. Repeating this for each
i\in[r]
, which we may do since
X^{\mathrm{buf}}
is an independent set in
H
, we obtain the desired embedding of
H
into
G
.
∎

5.2.
Proof of the degenerate graph RGA lemma

We prove Lemma
5.2
by analysing Algorithm
4
. The analysis is quite similar to what we saw before in the proofs of Lemmas
3.1
and
4.1
(indeed, the main difference is that we are more careful to bound powers of
p
using
D
rather than just
\Delta
), so we will be brief and highlight the differences. The only difference between Algorithm
3
and Algorithm
4
is that vertices of
X^{e}
are embedded into
V^{\mathrm{c}}
rather than
V^{\mathrm{main}}
or
V^{\mathrm{q}}
.

Input :

G\subseteq\Gamma
and
H
with partitions satisfying the General Setup; an ordering
\tau
on
X^{\mathrm{main}}

t:=0
;

\psi_{0}:=\emptyset
;

Q_{0}:=\{x\in V(H):|I_{x}|<\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon)^{|J_{x}|}p^{|J_{x}|}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(x)|\}
;

repeat

let
x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{t})
be the next vertex in
the order
\tau
;

if

x\in Q_{t}\setminus X^{e}

and

|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)\setminus B_{t}(x)|<\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|V(x)|

then

halt with failure ;

end if

if

x\in Q_{t}\cap X^{e}

and

|A^{\mathrm{c}}_{t}(x)\setminus B_{t}(x)|<\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|V(x)|

then

halt with failure ;

end if

choose
v
uniformly at random in
\begin{cases}A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)\setminus B_{t}(x)\quad&\text{if $x\not\in(Q_{t}\cup X^{e})\,,$}\\
A^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)\setminus B_{t}(x)&\text{if $x\in Q_{t}\setminus X^{e}\,,$}\\
A^{\mathrm{c}}_{t}(x)\setminus B_{t}(x)&\text{if $x\in X^{e}\,;$}\end{cases}

\psi_{t+1}:=\psi_{t}\cup\{x\to v\}
;

Q_{t+1}:=Q_{t}
;

forall

y\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{t+1})

do

if

(\big|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t+1}(y)\big|<\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{*}_{t+1}(y)}p^{\pi^{*}_{t+1}(y)}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(y)|)

then

Q_{t+1}:=Q_{t+1}\cup\{y\}
;

end if

end forall

t:=t+1
;

until

\mathrm{Dom}(\psi_{t})=X^{\mathrm{main}}

;

t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}:=t
;

Algorithm 4

Random greedy algorithm for degenerate graphs

We will see that
(ORD 0’)
(see Definition
5.1

of
(D,p,m)
-bounded order) is what we need to make
Lemma
2.27
work with properties
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)
and

\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},D)
, so allowing us to prove that

B_{t}(x)
is always much smaller than
A_{t}(x)
. As in the proof of
Lemma
4.1
, our first task is to show that the algorithm
a.a.s. completes successfully. Again by
Lemma
2.29
we can show that the queue remains
small. We can also show, using
(ORD 0’)
, that all vertices

x\in X_{i}\setminus X^{e}
which enter the queue have

\pi^{\tau}(x)\leq D/2
. We embed the vertices of
X^{e}\cap X_{i}
which
enter the queue greedily into
V^{\mathrm{c}}_{i}
, and there are so few such
vertices that this is guaranteed to succeed. Property

\mathrm{LCON}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)
turns out to be what we need to verify that the
queue embedding of the remaining vertices a.a.s. is successful,
using the same strategy as in the proof of Lemma
4.1
.

At this stage we have
(DRGA 0)
simply because the
algorithm completes, while
(DRGA 0)
follows from
Lemma
2.29
. It remains to
prove
(DRGA 0)
, which is where we need to
use
(ORD 0’)
. Here we deviate from the strategy we saw
previously. We can no longer assume that neighbours of buffer
vertices appear early in
\tau
, and thus we have to prove that for
any
v\in V(G)
, even towards the end of the embedding, it is still
reasonably likely that neighbours of buffer vertices are embedded to

N_{G}(v)
. We will see (Claim
5.7
below) that
properties
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)
,
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},D)

and
\mathrm{LCON}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)
allow us to show that a.a.s. given any

\ell\leq D-\max_{x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus X^{e}}\pi^{\tau}(x)
vertices

v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell}
of
G
such that

N^{*}_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i})
is not small, the set

N^{*}_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i})
is never completely filled by

\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})
. The idea of the proof remains similar to that of
Lemma
2.30
. We show, as there, that when the first
small fraction of the vertices in the order
\tau
are embedded, only
at most half of
N^{*}_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i})
is covered. But
then we repeat this, showing that the next small fraction of
\tau

covers only at most half of what remains, and so on, so that when all
vertices are embedded what remains is an exponentially small, but
bounded away from zero, fraction of the original

N^{*}_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i})
. Once we have shown this,
completing the proof that
N_{H}(x)
is not too unlikely to be embedded
to
N_{G}(v)
for any
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}
and
v\in V(x)
can be done along
similar lines to the proof of Lemma
2.31
.

Proof of Lemma
5.2
.

We require

\displaystyle\mu\leq\tfrac{1}{8}\,,\,\,\varrho\leq\frac{\mu^{2}\zeta^{2}d^{2D}}{10000(\Delta+1)}\,,\,\,\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\frac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta+1}}{1000\Delta^{2}\kappa 4^{\Delta}}2^{-280(D+1)\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-D}}\,,\,\,\varepsilon\leq\tfrac{d\varepsilon^{\prime}}{\kappa}

\displaystyle\text{and}\quad p\geq\tfrac{10000\cdot 4^{10\Delta^{3}}\Delta^{3}\kappa r_{1}^{2}}{\varepsilon\mu\zeta d^{\Delta+1}}2^{280(D+1)\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-D}}\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/D}\,.

We run Algorithm
4
, using the order
\tau
supplied to Lemma
5.2
. We claim that we can apply Lemma
2.27
to bound
B_{\tau(x)-1}(x)
. To see this, we verify properties
( )
‣
2.27
–
( )
‣
2.27
of Lemma
2.27
. We consider three cases.

If
x
has no neighbours after
\tau(x)
in
\tau
, then Lemma
2.27
states that
B_{\tau(x)-1}(x)=\emptyset
without requiring any property of
\Gamma
.

If
x
has a neighbour
y
with
\tau(x)<\tau(y)
, then
(ORD 0’)
states that
\pi^{\tau}(x)\leq D-1
, so in particular
\pi^{*}_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\leq D-1
, satisfying
( )
‣
2.27
. Furthermore,
(ORD 0’)
states that
\pi^{\tau}(y)\leq D
, so
\pi^{*}_{\tau(x)-1}(y)\leq D-1
since
x
is not embedded, satisfying
( )
‣
2.27
. Now suppose that
xy,yz\in E(H)
for some unembedded
y
and
z
. There are two cases to consider. First, if
\tau(y)<\tau(z)
then
(ORD 0’)
states that
\pi^{\tau}(y)\leq D-1
and
\pi^{\tau}(z)\leq D
. Since
x
and
y
are unembedded at time
{\tau(x)-1}
, we thus have
\pi^{*}_{\tau(x)-1}(y)\leq D-2
and
\pi^{*}_{\tau(x)-1}(z)\leq D-1
, as required by
( )
‣
2.27
. Second, if
\tau(y)>\tau(z)
, then
\pi^{*}_{\tau(x)-1}(z)\leq\pi^{\tau}(z)\leq D-1
by
(ORD 0’)
, while since
\pi^{\tau}(y)\leq D
and both the vertices
x
and
z
are unembedded, we have
\pi^{*}_{\tau(x)-1}(y)\leq D-2
, again as required by
( )
‣
2.27
.

Lastly, if there are unembedded vertices
y
and
z
such that
xy,yz,xz
are all edges of
H
, then we claim
\pi_{\tau(x)-1}^{*}(x),\pi_{\tau(x)-1}^{*}(y),\pi_{\tau(x)-1}^{*}(z)\leq D-2
. To see this, suppose
\tau(y)<\tau(z)
. By
(ORD 0’)
we have
\pi^{\tau}(x)\leq D-2
,
\pi^{\tau}(y)\leq D-1
and
\pi^{\tau}(z)\leq D
. Since
x
and
y
are unembedded, we have
\pi^{*}_{\tau(x)-1}(y)\leq D-1-1=D-2
, and
\pi^{*}_{\tau(x)-1}(z)\leq D-2
. Thus condition
( )
‣
2.27
is satisfied.

We conclude that we can apply Lemma
2.27
, and hence obtain
\big|B_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\big|\leq 20\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}|V(x)|
for each
x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}
.
Next, we show that certain invariants are maintained during the running of Algorithm
4
. These are identical to the invariants seen in the previous two RGA lemmas, but are repeated here for the reader’s convenience.

Claim 5.3
.

The following hold at each time
t
in the running of Algorithm
4
.

(
INV
0)

\psi_{t}
is a good partial embedding,

(
INV
0)

Either
|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)|\geq\frac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}p^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(x)|

or
x\in Q_{t}
.

(
INV
0)

When we embed
x
to create
\psi_{t+1}
, we do so
uniformly at random into a set of size at least
\frac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}|V(x)|
.

Proof.

Algorithm
4
maintains
(INV 0)
and
(INV 0)
by definition. Since
\tfrac{1}{2}\mu(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}-20\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}>\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta d^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}
by choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
for each
x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}
, by Lemma
2.27
it also maintains
(INV 0)
.

∎

As in the previous proofs, the conditions of Lemma
2.29
are met, so we conclude that with probability at least
1-2^{-n/(\kappa r_{1})}r_{1}
, for each
i\in[r]
and time
t
when Algorithm
4
is running, we have
\big|Q_{t}\cap X_{i}\big|<\varrho|X_{i}|+|X^{*}_{i}|\leq 2\varrho|X_{i}|
by
(G 0)
.

We now want to show that Algorithm
4
a.a.s. does not halt with failure. Since we know that
\big|B_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\big|<20\Delta^{2}\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}|V(x)|
, by
(GPE 0)
and
(G 0)
it is enough to show that a.a.s. we have

\displaystyle\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\big|

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\big|C^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\big|\quad\text{ for }\quad x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\cap Q_{\tau(x)-1}\setminus X^{e}\,,\quad\text{ and}

(5.1)

\displaystyle\big|A^{\mathrm{c}}_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\big|

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\big|C^{\mathrm{c}}_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\big|\quad\text{ for }\quad x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\cap Q_{\tau(x)-1}\cap X^{e}\,.

The latter of these is easy, since we embed only vertices of
X^{e}
into
V^{\mathrm{c}}
, and
|X^{e}|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon p^{\max_{x\in X^{e}}\pi^{\tau}(x)}n/r_{1}
, which by
(GPE 0)
and choice of
\varepsilon
is smaller than
\tfrac{1}{2}\big|C^{\mathrm{c}}_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\big|
. In the next three claims we show (
5.1
) holds.

First, we show that we need only concern ourselves with
x
such that
\pi^{\tau}(x)\leq D/2
.

Claim 5.4
.

For each
x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus X^{e}
, if
\pi^{\tau}(x)>D/2
then there is no time
t
such that
x\in Q_{t}
.

Proof.

We require
\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\tfrac{1}{4}d^{D}\mu
and
\varepsilon\leq\kappa^{-1}\varepsilon^{\prime}
.

Suppose
\pi^{\tau}(x)>D/2
and
x\not\in X^{e}
. Let
i
be such that
x\in X_{i}
. By
(ORD 0’)
,
x
is not image restricted, and all
\pi^{\tau}(x)
neighbours of
x
which precede
x
in
\tau
occur in the

\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}n/r_{1}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}|X_{i}|
places of
\tau
before
x
. Because
x
is not image restricted, at time
t\leq\tau(x)-\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}|X_{i}|
we have
C^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)=V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
for some
i
, and hence
A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)=V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})
. Because
|X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|=|V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|-\mu|V_{i}|
, it follows that
|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)|\geq\mu|V_{i}|
, and in particular
x
does not enter
Q_{t}
at time
t
.

Now suppose
t=\tau(x)-\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}|X_{i}|+s
for some
0\leq s\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}|X_{i}|-1
. We claim that

|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)|\geq(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}\mu|V_{i}|-s\,.

Indeed, for
s=0
we have
\pi^{*}_{t}(x)=0
and the statement
|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)\geq\mu|V_{i}|
was established above. Now suppose the statement holds for some
s\geq 0
. At time
t=\tau(x)-\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}|X_{i}|+s
we embed a vertex
y
to obtain
\psi_{t+1}
. If
y
is neither a neighbour of
x
nor in
X_{i}
, then
A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)=A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t+1}(x)
and the statement holds for
s+1
. If
y
is a neighbour of
x
, then we embed
y
to a vertex of
A_{t}(y)\setminus B_{t}(y)
, and in particular to a vertex
v
such that
\deg_{G}\big(v;A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)\big)\geq(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)|
. We conclude that

|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t+1}(x)|\geq(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p\big((dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}\mu|V_{i}|-s\big)>(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t+1}(x)}\mu|V_{i}|-s-1\,.

as desired. Finally, if
y\in X_{i}
then we have
A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t+1}(x)=A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)\setminus\{y\}
, and again

|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t+1}(x)|\geq(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t+1}(x)}\mu|V_{i}|-s-1\,.

Thus for each
\tau(x)-\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}|X_{i}|\leq t\leq\tau(x)-1
we have

|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(x)|\geq(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}\mu|V_{i}|-\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}|X_{i}|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(x)}\mu|V_{i}|\,,

where the final inequality is by choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
. It follows that
x
does not satisfy the condition to enter
Q_{t}
. Since we embed
x
at time
\tau(x)-1
, we conclude that as desired there is no time
t
such that
x\in Q_{t}
.

∎

As in the proof of Lemma
4.1
the next step is to establish a ‘sum condition’. In fact, we establish almost the same inequality as (
4.6
) in Claim
4.5
(the right hand side is identical, while the left hand side sum runs over all vertices outside
X^{e}
, including those
y
with
J_{y}\neq\emptyset
), though we use a different analysis to do so.

Claim 5.5
.

Suppose that
\Gamma
has
\mathrm{LCON}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)
. Then the following statement holds.

For any
t
, any
1\leq\ell\leq D/2
, any
i\in[r]
and any vertices
v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell}
such that

\deg_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V_{i})\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\ell}|V_{i}|

we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}\setminus X^{e}\colon\\
\tau(y)\leq t\end{subarray}}\hskip-10.00002pt\frac{\big|U_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\cap N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V_{i})\big|}{\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\setminus B_{\tau(y)-1}(y)\big|}\leq\frac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}\deg_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V_{i})}{20}\,.

(5.2)

The proof of this amounts to checking that the images under our embedding of the
N_{H}(y)
for
y\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}\setminus X^{e}
form a family of sets to which we can apply the
\mathrm{LCON}
property. This property gives us the desired inequality.

Proof.

We require
\varrho\leq\tfrac{\mu^{2}\zeta^{2}d^{D+\Delta}}{10000(\Delta+1)}
,
\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\mu\zeta(d/4)^{D}
and
\varepsilon\leq\tfrac{\varepsilon^{\prime}}{\kappa}
. Since the left hand side of (
5.2
) is increasing in
t
, we may assume
t
is the time at which Algorithm
4
ends.

Given
1\leq\ell\leq D/2
and
i\in[r]
, let
v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell}
be vertices such that
U:=N^{*}_{\Gamma}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V_{i})
has size at least
\varepsilon^{\prime}p^{\ell}|V_{i}|
. By choice of
\varepsilon
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, we have
|U|\geq\varepsilon p^{\ell}n/r_{1}
. It follows that we can apply
\mathrm{LCON}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)
to bound the number of edges in
\mathrm{CG}(\Gamma,U,\mathcal{F})
for any
1\leq j\leq D/2
and family
\mathcal{F}
of pairwise disjoint
j
-sets in
V(\Gamma)\setminus U
. We set

\mathcal{F}_{j}:=\big\{\Pi_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\cup J_{x}:x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}\setminus X^{e},\pi^{\tau}(x)=j,\tau(x)\leq t\big\}\,.

Observe that
\mathcal{F}_{j}
is indeed a family of
j
-sets by definition of
\pi^{\tau}(x)
, that these
j
-sets are pairwise disjoint because no vertex of
H
has more than one neighbour in
X_{i}
by
(H 0)
, and element
F
of
\mathcal{F}_{j}
intersects
U\subseteq V_{i}
because these sets
F
are images under
\psi
of neighbours of vertices in
X_{i}
. Because
\varepsilon|U|,|X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}|\leq 2\varrho|X_{i}|
, by choice of
\varepsilon
and by
\mathrm{LCON}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)
we have

e\big(\mathrm{CG}(\Gamma,U,\mathcal{F}_{j})\big)\leq 7p^{j}|U|(2\varrho|X_{i}|)=14\varrho p^{j}|U||X_{i}|\,.

By definition of
U_{t}(x)
(see Section
2.3.5
) we have the following equality

e\big(\mathrm{CG}(\Gamma,U,\mathcal{F}_{j})\big)=\sum_{x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}\setminus X^{e}:\pi^{\tau}(x)=j,\tau(x)\leq t}|U_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\cap U|

and hence

\sum_{x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}\setminus X^{e}:\pi^{\tau}(x)=j,\tau(x)\leq t}|U_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\cap U|\leq 14\varrho p^{j}|U||X_{i}|

for each
1\leq j\leq D/2
. Observe that the
j=0
case of the same inequality is trivially true (indeed with
14
replaced by
2
, though we will not use this): each vertex of
X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}
contributes at most
|U|
to the sum. Now by
(INV 0)
we have

\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(x)}(x)\setminus B_{\tau(x)}(x)\big|\geq\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{\tau}(x)}|V_{i}|

for each
x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}\setminus X^{e}
with
\tau(x)\leq t
. Finally, since any vertex
x
in
X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}\setminus X^{e}
satisfies

\pi^{\tau}(x)\leq D/2
by Claim
5.4
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}\setminus X^{e}:\\
\tau(x)\leq t\end{subarray}}\frac{|U^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(x)}(x)\cap U|}{|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(x)}(x)\setminus B_{\tau(x)}(x)\big|}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{D/2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\cap Q_{t}\setminus X^{e}:\\
\pi^{\tau}(x)=j,\tau(x)\leq t\end{subarray}}\frac{|U^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(x)}(x)\cap U|}{\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{j}|V_{i}|}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{D/2}\frac{14\varrho p^{j}|U||X_{i}|}{\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{j}|V_{i}|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{14(1+D/2)\varrho|U||X_{i}|}{\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta d^{D/2}|V_{i}|}\leq\tfrac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}}{20}|U|,

where the final inequality is by choice of
\varrho
, as desired.

∎

We can now complete the proof that candidate sets in
V^{\mathrm{q}}
are not substantially covered by
\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})
for any
t
.

Claim 5.6
.

A.a.s. for each
x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus X^{e}
, at each time
t\leq\tau(x)-1
and before the termination of Algorithm
4
, we have
\big|C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)\cap\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})\big|<\tfrac{1}{2}\big|C^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(x)\big|
.

Proof.

The proof of Claim
4.6
, if we replace
X^{\mathrm{main}}
with
X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus X^{e}
, and replace (
4.6
) with (
5.2
), without handling image restricted vertices specially (since condition (
5.2
) deals with them), gives this claim verbatim.

∎

By the same logic as in the proof of Lemma
4.1
, if the good event of Claim
5.6
holds, it follows that candidate sets in
V^{\mathrm{q}}
are not significantly covered by
\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})
, i.e. the inequality (
5.1
) holds. This implies that Algorithm
4
a.a.s. completes successfully. Again, the successful running implies
(DRGA 0)
, and the good event of Lemma
2.29
holding implies
(DRGA 0)
.

We would like to emphasise at this point that we have established all
the desired conclusions of Lemma
5.2

except
(DRGA 0)
, and we have so far not used
condition
(ORD 0’)
, and not used the second part of
(ORD 0’)

(which states
\pi^{\tau}(x)\leq D_{x}-1
for
x\in N(\tilde{X})\setminus X^{\mathrm{buf}}
). It follows
that one can establish a version of Lemma
5.2

omitting
(DRGA 0)
given an order
\tau
which need only
satisfy the first part of
(ORD 0’)
(which states

\pi^{\tau}(x)\leq D_{x}
for all
x\in V(H)
) and
(ORD 0’)
. We will
return to this point in Section
7.2.3
.

We now turn to proving
(DRGA 0)
in the next two claims. First, in Claim
5.7
, we show that
G
-common neighbourhoods of at most
D-\max_{x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus X^{e}}\pi^{\tau}(x)

vertices which are large do not get completely filled at any time in the running of Algorithm
4
. Note that the upper bound on
\ell
in Claim
5.7
comes from
(ORD 0’)
. What the good event of Claim
5.7
, together with
(ORD 0’)
, says is the following. If
v=v_{1}\in V_{i}
is any vertex (which we eventually want to show is candidate for many vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
) and
v_{2},\dots,v_{\ell}
are the embedded neighbours of some
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, then the
G
-common neighbourhood of
v_{1},\dots,v_{\ell}
is not covered by
\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})
provided
t\leq\tau(x)-\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon p^{D}n/r_{1}
. We will see that neighbours embedded after this time can be dealt with easily. Claim
5.7
has two parts,
( )
‣
5.7
dealing with candidate sets in
V^{\mathrm{main}}
and
( )
‣
5.7
dealing with candidate sets in
V^{\mathrm{q}}
. The reason for this is that neighbours of a buffer vertex
x
may end up in the queue, and we need to show that even in this case
v
is reasonably likely to be a candidate for
x
.
This claim replaces Lemma
2.30
, which we used in the proofs of the previous two RGA lemmas.

Claim 5.7
.

Suppose that
\Gamma
has
\mathrm{LCON}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)
. Then a.a.s. the following holds at each time
t
. Given any
i\in[r]
, any
1\leq\ell\leq D-\max_{x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus X^{e}}\pi^{\tau}(x)
, and any vertices
v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell}
of
G
, the following hold.

(

)

If
\deg_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i})\geq(dp/2)^{\ell}|V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|
, then

\begin{split}\big|N^{*}_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i})&\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})\big|\geq\\
(1-4\mu)&2^{-280(D+1)\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-D}}\deg_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i})\,.\end{split}

(5.3)

(

)

If
\deg_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i})\geq(dp/2)^{\ell}|V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}|
, then

\big|N^{*}_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i})\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t})\big|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\deg_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i})\,.

(5.4)

The proof of (
5.3
), as discussed at the beginning of this section, roughly amounts to repeating the argument used to prove Lemma
2.30
several times. We show that, for some small constant
\eta
, each successive interval consisting of an
\eta
-fraction of the vertices
X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}
is likely to cover less than half of whatever of
N^{*}_{G}(v_{1},\dots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i})
was uncovered before embedding that interval. The argument for each given interval is similar to that seen in Lemma
2.30
, though here we take a short-cut by using property
\mathrm{LCON}
rather than
\mathrm{NS}
, which simplifies the calculations.

Proof.

Let
h=\max_{x\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus X^{e}}\pi^{\tau}(x)
.
We require

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{1}{8}\,,\quad

\displaystyle\varrho

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu^{2}\zeta d^{D}}{1000(D+1)}\,,

\displaystyle\varepsilon

\displaystyle<\tfrac{1}{2}2^{-280(D+1)\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-D}}\big(\tfrac{d}{2}\big)^{D+1}\quad\text{and}\quad

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1000\Delta^{2}}{\varepsilon\mu\zeta d^{D}}\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/D}\,.

We first prove inequality (
5.3
). Set
\eta=\frac{\mu\zeta d^{D}}{280(D+1)}
.
Given
i\in[r]
and
1\leq\ell\leq D-h
, suppose that
v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell}\in V(\Gamma)
are such that
N^{*}_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i})\geq(dp/2)^{\ell}|V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|
. We split the vertices of
X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\setminus X^{e}
into intervals
\mathrm{Int}_{1},\ldots,\mathrm{Int}_{1/\eta}
of equal size, with the first being the first
\eta|X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}\setminus X^{e}|
vertices in the order
\tau
, and so on.

We now aim to show the following.

(‡)

For any fixed set
U\subseteq V_{i}
of size at least
\varepsilon p^{\ell}|V_{i}|
and any
1\leq j\leq 1/\eta
, conditioning on the embedding up to the last vertex of
\mathrm{Int}_{j-1}
, with probability at least
1-n^{-D-1}
there are at most
\tfrac{1}{2}|U|
vertices of
\mathrm{Int}_{j}
are embedded to
U
.

To that end, for each
x\in\mathrm{Int}_{j}
let
\mathscr{H}_{x,j}
denote the history up to but not including the embedding of
x
. By definition of
h
, all vertices
x\in\mathrm{Int}_{j}
have
\pi^{\tau}(x)\leq h
, so we split the vertices
x
of
\mathrm{Int}_{j}
up into
h+1
classes according to
\pi^{\tau}(x)
. We apply
\mathrm{LCON}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)
with the set
U
and the family
\mathcal{F}_{s}
of
s
-sets given by the embedded images of
N_{H}(x)
for
x\in\mathrm{Int}_{j}
with
\pi^{\tau}(x)=s
. Since
\varepsilon|U|,|\mathcal{F}_{s}|\leq\eta|X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|
, we obtain the inequality

\sum_{x\in\mathrm{Int}_{j}:\pi^{\tau}(x)=s}\big|U_{\tau(x)-1}(x)\cap U\big|\leq 7p^{s}|U|\eta|X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|\,.

Now each
x\in\mathrm{Int}_{j}
with
\pi^{\tau}(x)=s
is by
(INV 0)
embedded uniformly at random into a set of size at least
\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{s}|V_{i}|
. We thus have

\sum_{x\in\mathrm{Int}_{j}}\mathbb{P}\big(x\text{ is embedded to }U\big|\mathscr{H}_{x,j}\big)\leq(h+1)\frac{70\eta|U|}{\mu\zeta d^{h}}\leq(D+1)\frac{70\eta|U|}{\mu\zeta d^{D}}

and by Lemma
2.2
with
\delta=1
we see that the probability that more than

140(D+1)\eta\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-D}|U|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}|U|

of the vertices in
\mathrm{Int}_{j}
are embedded to
U
is at most

\exp\left(-(D+1)\tfrac{70\eta|U|}{3\mu\zeta d^{D}}\right)\leq n^{-D-1}\,,

where the inequality is because
|U|\geq\varepsilon p^{D-h}|V_{i}|
and by choice of
p
. This completes the proof of
(‡)
‣
5.2
.

Let
U_{0}=N^{*}_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i})
, and
U_{j}=U_{j-1}\setminus\psi_{t_{j+1}}(\mathrm{Int}_{j})
for each
j\geq 1
. In the event that
|U_{j}|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}|U_{j-1}|
for each
j
, we have

|U_{j}|\geq 2^{-1/\eta}(d/2)^{\ell}p^{\ell}|V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|\geq\varepsilon p^{\ell}|V_{i}|

for each
j
, where the final inequality is by choice of
\varepsilon
. In other words, each
U_{j-1}
is large enough to play the rôle of
U
in
(‡)
‣
5.2
, so taking a union bound over the
1/\eta
choices of
j
, we see that with probability at least
1-\eta^{-1}n^{-D-1}
we have

\big|N^{*}_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i})\setminus\mathrm{Im}(\psi_{t_{j}})\big|\geq(1-4\mu)2^{1-j}\deg_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{main}}_{i})

for each
1\leq j\leq 1/\eta+1
. Taking a union bound over the at most
r_{1}
choices of
i\in[r]
and
n+n^{2}+\dots+n^{D}
choices of
v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell}
we see that the desired event of the lemma holds with probability at least
1-r_{1}D\eta^{-1}n^{-1}
, which tends to one as
n
tends to infinity. This completes the proof of (
5.3
).

The proof of (
5.4
) is simpler. Observe that
|X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}|\leq 2\varrho|X_{i}|<\eta|X^{\mathrm{main}}_{i}|
, and
\deg_{G}(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\ell};V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i})\geq(dp/2)^{\ell}|V^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}|\geq\varepsilon p^{\ell}|V_{i}|
so that the analysis above, with only one ‘interval’
X^{\mathrm{q}}_{i}\setminus X^{e}
, gives the desired result.

∎

Finally, we are in a position to prove
(DRGA 0)
, which we do in the following claim. The proof is quite similar to that of Lemma
2.31
, which amounts to showing that any vertex
v\in V_{i}
is likely to be a candidate vertex for ‘reasonably’ many of the buffer vertices
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. More precisely, our strategy is to fix a vertex
v\in V_{i}
and show that the inequality (
5.5
) below, which encapsulates these reasonable bounds, holds with sufficiently high probability to apply the union bound over all
v\in V(G)
.
To do this, we introduce the concept of an
(x\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding for
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. As in the proof of Lemma
2.31
, this is a good partial embedding which has a few extra properties that let us show that it is not too unlikely that
N_{H}(x)
is embedded to
N_{G}(v)
. As there, the proof that it is not too unlikely that at each step Algorithm
4
maintains an
(x\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding is mainly ‘bookkeeping’ and long but not very hard. However, there is an important difference. It is no longer useful to simply multiply the conditional probabilities of maintaining an
(x\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding in order to estimate the conditional probability that
N_{H}(x)
is embedded to
N_{G}(v)
. This is because the vertices
N_{H}(x)
are no longer embedded as a segment of
\tau
, so that Lemma
2.2
is not applicable directly to these products of conditional probabilities. Nevertheless we show below that we can still apply Lemma
2.2
, several times and with some extra care, to obtain the desired bounds.

Claim 5.8
.

The following holds a.a.s. for each
i\in[r]
. Let
b
be such that
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
is a degree-
b
buffer. Then for each
v\in V_{i}
we have

\big|\{x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}:v\in C_{t_{\mathrm{RGAend}}}(x)\}\big|\geq 4^{-10\Delta^{3}}2^{-1000D^{2}\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-D}}d^{2D^{2}}\Delta^{-3}\mu p^{b}|X_{i}|\,.

(5.5)

Proof.

We require

\displaystyle\mu\leq\tfrac{1}{8}\,,\quad

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\prime}

\displaystyle<\tfrac{\mu\zeta d^{\Delta}}{1000\Delta^{2}\kappa 4^{\Delta}}\cdot 2^{-280(D+1)\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-D}}\,,\qquad\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\,,

and

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle>10000\cdot 4^{10\Delta^{3}}\Delta^{3}\kappa r_{1}\mu^{-1}d^{-\Delta-1}\cdot 2^{280(D+1)\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-D}}\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/D}\,.

Let
\beta=(1-4\mu)2^{-280(D+1)\mu^{-1}\zeta^{-1}d^{-D}}
. It is convenient to consider only vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
which are far from
X^{e}
; since
X^{e}
is very small, doing so does not exclude many vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
.

Given
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
which is at distance greater than three from any vertex of
X^{e}
, and
v\in V_{i}
, we say that a good partial embedding
\psi
is an
(x\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding
if the following hold.

(
BPE
0)

For each
y\in\mathrm{Dom}(\psi)\cap N_{H}(x)
we have
\psi(y)\in N_{G}(v)
.

(
BPE
0)

For each unembedded
y\in N_{H}(x)
we have

\displaystyle|U(y)\cap N_{\Gamma}(v)|

\displaystyle=(p\pm\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}(y)}\deg_{\Gamma}\big(v;V(y)\big)\,,

\displaystyle|U^{\mathrm{main}}(y)\cap N_{\Gamma}(v)|

\displaystyle=(p\pm\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}(y)}\deg_{\Gamma}\big(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}(y)\big)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle|U^{\mathrm{q}}(y)\cap N_{\Gamma}(v)|

\displaystyle=(p\pm\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}(y)}\deg_{\Gamma}\big(v;V^{\mathrm{q}}(y)\big)

(
BPE
0)

For each unembedded
y\in N_{H}(x)
we have

\displaystyle|C^{\mathrm{q}}(y)\cap N_{G}(v)|

\displaystyle\geq(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}(y)}\deg_{G}\big(v;V^{\mathrm{q}}(y)\big)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle|C^{\mathrm{main}}(y)\cap N_{G}(v)|

\displaystyle\geq(dp-\varepsilon^{\prime}p)^{\pi^{*}(y)}\deg_{G}\big(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}(y)\big)

(
BPE
0)

For each unembedded
y,z\in N_{H}(x)
with
yz\in E(H)
, the pair
\big(U(y)\cap N_{\Gamma}(v),U(z)\cap N_{\Gamma}(v)\big)
is
(\varepsilon_{\pi^{*}(y),\pi^{*}(z)},d,p)
-regular in
G
.

(
BPE
0)

For each unembedded
y\in N_{H}(x)
and
z\in N_{H}(y)
, the pair
\big(U(y)\cap N_{\Gamma}(v),U(z)\big)
is
(\varepsilon_{\pi^{*}(y),\pi^{*}(z)},d,p)
-regular in
G
.

Much as in the proof of Lemma
2.31
, the empty partial embedding
\psi_{0}
is an
(x\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding. Indeed,
(BPE 0)
–
(BPE 0)
are trivially true since by
(H 0)
neighbours of buffer vertices are not image restricted. For
(BPE 0)
and
(BPE 0)
, since buffer vertices are by
(H 0)
at distance at least three from image restricted vertices the sets
U(y)
and
U(z)
are equal to
V(y)
and
V(z)
respectively, and the required regularity is thus given by
(G 0)
, since
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\subseteq\tilde{X}_{i}
.

Our aim now is to give lower bounds on the probability that, given that at some time
t
we currently have an
(x\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding, Algorithm
4
embeds the next vertex in a way that maintains an
(x\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding. To that end, given an
(x\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding
\psi
and an unembedded vertex
y
, we let
P(y)
be the set of
poor vertices for
y
, namely those vertices
u\in C(y)
such that either
\psi\cup\{y\to u\}
is not an
(x\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding, or such that there is an unembedded
z\in N_{H}(y)\cap N_{H}(x)
such that either of the following conditions hold:

\begin{split}\deg_{G}\big(u;A^{\mathrm{main}}(z)\cap N_{G}(v)\big)\leq&(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p\big|A^{\mathrm{main}}(z)\cap N_{G}(v)\big|\quad\text{or}\\
\deg_{G}\big(u;A^{\mathrm{q}}(z)\cap N_{G}(v)\big)\leq&(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}(z)\cap N_{G}(v)\big|\,.\end{split}

(5.6)

Observe that if
y
is at distance four or greater from
x
in
H
, then
P(y)
is always empty. This has two important consequences. First, we are about to talk about the probability of no vertices being embedded to poor vertices: this is really a condition on at most
3\Delta^{3}
vertex embeddings. Second, it means that if
x,x^{\prime}\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, by
(H 0)
the distance between
x
and
x^{\prime}
is at least ten, so that any given vertex embedding affects at most one of having an
(x\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding and having an
(x^{\prime}\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding.

Before we try to estimate the probability of maintaining an
(x\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding, we strengthen the conclusion of Claim
5.7
to cover the ‘last few vertices’ before embedding
y\in N_{H}(x)
.

Fact 5.9
.

For a given
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
at distance greater than three from
X^{e}
and a fixed
v\in V(x)
, provided that up to time
t
no vertex has been embedded to a poor vertex (with respect to an
(x\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding), for each
y\in N_{H}(x)
with
\tau(y)>t
, the following a.a.s. hold.

\begin{split}\big|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(y)\cap N_{G}(v)\big|&\geq\tfrac{\beta}{2}(dp/2)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}\deg_{G}\big(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}(y)\big)\quad\text{and}\\
\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(y)\cap N_{G}(v)\big|&\geq\tfrac{1}{4}(dp/2)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}\deg_{G}\big(v;V^{\mathrm{q}}(y)\big)\,.\end{split}

(5.7)

We will see that Claim
5.7
, together with
(ORD 0’)
, show that this fact holds provided
t\leq\tau(y)-\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon p^{D}n/r_{1}
. The number of vertices embedded in the remaining
\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon p^{D}n/r_{1}
steps is too small to significantly fill up either of these sets, so we need only show that embedding neighbours of
y
does not adversely affect (
5.7
). This is guaranteed by condition (
5.6
) in the definition of poor vertices.

Proof.

Let
h=\max_{z\in X^{\mathrm{main}}\setminus X^{e}}\pi^{\tau}(z)
.
Since
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}\subseteq\tilde{X}
and
y\in N_{H}(x)
we infer by
(ORD 0’)
that all
but at most
s
neighbours
z
of
y

satisfy
\tau(y)-\tau(z)\leq p^{D}\cdot\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon n/r_{1}
for some
s\leq D-1-h

(recall that
\tau
is a
(D,p,\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon n/r_{1})
-bounded order).
Therefore, if
t\leq\tau(y)-\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon p^{D}n/r_{1}
then
\pi^{*}_{t}(y)\leq s\leq D-1-h
,
so that
\Pi^{*}_{t}(y)\cup\{v\}
is by
(BPE 0)
,
(G 0)
and
(G 0)
a set of
s+1\leq D-h
vertices in
G
with at least
(dp/2)^{s+1}|V^{\mathrm{q}}(y)|
common neighbours in
V^{\mathrm{q}}(y)
and at least
(dp/2)^{s+1}|V^{\mathrm{main}}(y)|
common neighbours in
V^{\mathrm{main}}(y)
.

Therefore, by Claim
5.7
, we have

\displaystyle\big|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(y)\cap N_{G}(v)\big|

\displaystyle\geq\beta(dp/2)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}\deg_{G}\big(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}(y)\big)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(y)\cap N_{G}(v)\big|

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}(dp/2)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}\deg_{G}\big(v;V^{\mathrm{q}}(y)\big)\,,

which gives (
5.7
) for this range of
t
. Now suppose that

t=\tau(y)-\varepsilon p^{D}\cdot\tfrac{1}{2}n/r_{1}+\ell

for some
0\leq\ell<\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon p^{D}n/r_{1}
. We claim that

\begin{split}\big|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{t}(y)\cap N_{G}(v)\big|&\geq\beta(dp/2)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}\deg_{G}\big(v;V^{\mathrm{main}}(y)\big)-\ell\quad\text{and}\\
\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{t}(y)\cap N_{G}(v)\big|&\geq\tfrac{1}{2}(dp/2)^{\pi^{*}_{t}(y)}\deg_{G}\big(v;V^{\mathrm{q}}(y)\big)-\ell\,.\end{split}

(5.8)

Indeed, we have just established that (
5.8
) holds for
\ell=0
. Given
\ell\geq 1
, suppose that (
5.8
) holds for
\ell-1
, and consider the vertex
z
embedded at time
\tau(z)=\tau(y)-\varepsilon p^{D}\cdot\tfrac{1}{2}n/r_{1}+\ell
.

If
z
is not a neighbour of
y
, then its embedding decreases the sizes of the sets on the left hand side of (
5.8
) by at most one, so the inequality continues to hold. If
z
is a neighbour of
y
, then by (
5.6
) in the definition of
P(z)
and since, by the assumption of Fact
5.9
no vertex has been embedded to a poor vertex, we have

\displaystyle\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(z)}(y)\cap N_{G}(v)\big|

\displaystyle\geq(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}_{\tau(z)-1}(y)\cap N_{G}(v)\big|

\displaystyle\geq\tfrac{1}{2}(dp/2)^{\pi^{*}_{\tau(z)}(y)}\deg_{G}\big(v;V^{\mathrm{q}}(y)\big)-(d-\varepsilon^{\prime})p\ell

where the second inequality is by choice of
\varepsilon^{\prime}
and (
5.8
). A similar inequality holds for
\big|A^{\mathrm{main}}_{\tau(z)}(y)\cap N_{G}(v)\big|
. Thus again (
5.8
) continues to hold. We conclude that (
5.8
) holds for all
0\leq\ell<\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon p^{D}n/r_{1}
.

Now since
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}
, we have
\tau(x)>\tau(y)
, so that by
(ORD 0’)
we have
\pi^{*}_{t}(y)\leq\pi^{\tau}(y)\leq D-1
. By
(G 0)
and choice of
\varepsilon
, we conclude that (
5.7
) holds for all
t<\tau(y)
as desired.

∎

We now continue with the proof of Claim
5.8
by estimating the probability of not choosing a poor vertex at a given step in Algorithm
4
. Recall that
B(y)
is the set of bad vertices for a given good partial embedding and unembedded
y
, as defined in Definition
2.26
.

Fact 5.10
.

Suppose that
\psi
is a good partial embedding generated together with a queue
Q
by Algorithm
4
which is also an
(x\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding, where
x
is at distance greater than three from any vertex of
X^{e}
, and (
5.7
) holds for
\psi
and all unembedded
y
. Given an unembedded
y
, suppose that
u
is a vertex chosen uniformly at random in
A^{\mathrm{main}}(y)\setminus B(y)
, or in
A^{\mathrm{q}}(y)\setminus B(y)
, or in
A^{\mathrm{c}}(y)\setminus B(y)
, according to whether
y\not\in Q\cup X^{e}
, or
y\in Q\setminus X^{e}
, or
y\in X^{e}
respectively.
Then the following hold.

(

)

If
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(x,y)>3
then
\mathbb{P}\big(u\not\in P(y)\big)=1
.

(

)

If
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(x,y)\in\{2,3\}
then
\mathbb{P}\big(u\not\in P(y)\big)\geq\tfrac{1}{2}
.

(

)

If
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(x,y)=1
then
\mathbb{P}\big(u\not\in P(y)\big)\geq\beta d^{D-1}4^{-D}p
.

The proof of this fact is quite similar to the analysis in Lemma
2.31
. We remark that the use we make of
\psi
and
Q
being generated by Algorithm
4
is that the invariants of Claim
5.3
hold; we do not in the following proof perform any analysis of the probabilistic process generating
\psi
and
Q
.

Proof.

If
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(x,y)>3
then
P(y)=\emptyset
, so
( )
‣
5.10
is trivial. From now on we assume
\mathrm{dist}_{H}(x,y)\leq 3
.

By
(INV 0)
we embed
y
uniformly at random into a set whose size is at least
\tfrac{1}{10}\mu\zeta(dp)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)}|V(y)|
. Thus in order to show
( )
‣
5.10
we simply need to establish that
P(y)\cup B(y)
is small compared to this set; this can be established using
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)
,
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},D)
and the buffer and good partial embedding properties, as in the proof of Lemma
2.31
. We need to use the fact, given by
(ORD 0’)
, that
\pi^{\tau}(z)\leq D_{z}-1
for each
z\in N(\tilde{X})\setminus X^{\mathrm{buf}}
, in other words that
\pi^{\tau}(z)\leq D-2
for all
z\in N_{H}(x)
and
\pi^{\tau}(z)\leq D-3
if there is a triangle
zww^{\prime}
in
H
with
\tau(w),\tau(w^{\prime})>\tau(z)
. In order to establish that few vertices fail (
5.6
), we need to know that
A^{\mathrm{main}}(z)\cap N_{G}(v)
and
A^{\mathrm{q}}(z)\cap N_{G}(v)
are not small in comparison to
U(z)\cap N_{\Gamma}(v)
. This follows from (
5.7
) since
z\in N_{H}(x)
. Since the proof is otherwise as in the proof of Lemma
2.31
, we omit further details.

Finally we come to
( )
‣
5.10
. We embed
y
uniformly at random into a subset of
U(y)
, which by
(GPE 0)
has size at most
2p^{\pi^{\tau}(y)}|V(y)|
. If
xy\in H
, we need to show that it is not too unlikely that
y
is embedded to
N_{G}(v)
. By (
5.7
),
(G 0)
and choice of
\beta
, we see that

\big|A^{\mathrm{main}}(y)\cap N_{G}(v)\big|,\big|A^{\mathrm{q}}(y)\cap N_{G}(v)\big|\geq\tfrac{\beta}{4}(dp/2)^{\pi^{\tau}(y)}\deg_{G}(v;V(y))\,.

It is thus enough to show that at most half of these vertices are in
P(y)\cup B(y)
, which again we can do using
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon,r_{1},D)
,
\mathrm{RI}(\varepsilon,(\varepsilon_{a,b}),\varepsilon^{\prime},d,r_{1},D)
and the buffer and good partial embedding properties, as in the proof of Lemma
2.31
. Again, we use the fact
\pi^{\tau}(z)\leq D_{z}-1
for
z\in N_{H}(x)
in this verification. We omit the details.

∎

We now continue with the proof of Claim
5.8
. We fix
i
and
v\in V_{i}
. Our goal is to show that with very high probability a reasonable fraction of the
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
have
N_{H}(x)
embedded to
N_{G}(v)
, since this immediately implies the claim. We have established in Fact
5.10
that for any given vertex
x
of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
far from
X^{e}
, the probability that
N_{H}(x)
is embedded to
N_{G}(v)
is not too small. Fact
5.10
states that this probability is more or less given by the embeddings of vertices at distance at most three from
x
, and distinct vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
are at distance at least ten by
(H 0)
, so that no vertex is within distance three of two distinct
x,x^{\prime}\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
. In the proof of Lemma
2.31
at this point we simply apply Lemma
2.2
to complete the proof. However, here we cannot do this, because the sets
N_{H}(x)
interleave each other in the order
\tau
. We have to be a bit more careful, as follows.

For each
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, let
M_{x}
be the set of vertices of
H
at distance one, two or three from
x
. We say
x

survives at step
j
if after the embedding of the
j
th vertex of
M_{x}
in the order
\tau
, we still have an
(x\to v)
-buffer-friendly partial embedding. We will use Lemma
2.2
to show that a reasonable fraction of
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
survive at step
1
. We would like then to use Lemma
2.2
again to show that (because a reasonable fraction of vertices survive at step
1
) a reasonable fraction of vertices survive at step
2
, and so on.

In order to carry this out, it is convenient not in fact to look at all
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, but only at a subset
Y
in which the probability of surviving at each step
j
(as given by Fact
5.10
) does not depend on the particular vertex in
x\in Y
but only on
j
. We now construct such a set
Y
. For any
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
at distance greater than three from
X^{e}
, there are at most
\Delta^{3}+\Delta^{2}+\Delta<3\Delta^{3}
vertices of
H
at distance at most
3
from
x
in
H
. We can therefore associate to each
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
at distance greater than three from
X^{e}
a
0
–
1
vector with at most
3\Delta^{3}
non-zero entries, taking the value
0
at place
j
if the
j
th vertex
y
of
M_{x}
(in the order
\tau
) is not a neighbour of
x
, and
1
if
y
is a neighbour of
x
. There are at most
3\Delta^{3}2^{3\Delta^{3}}
choices for this vector; we fix a most common choice
\mathbf{c}=(c_{i})
, and let
Y
be the subset of vertices of
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
at distance at least three from
X^{e}
associated to this most common choice. By construction, by
(H 0)
, by choice of
\varepsilon
and since
|X^{e}|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\varepsilon p^{D}n/r_{1}
and
|X_{i}|\geq n/\kappa r_{1}
, we have

|Y|\geq\tfrac{1}{6}\Delta^{-3}2^{-3\Delta^{3}}|X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}|\,.

(5.9)

For each
x\in Y
and
1\leq j\leq|\mathbf{c}|
(where
|\mathbf{c}|
is the number of 1-entries of
\mathbf{c}
), let
\mathscr{H}_{x,j}
be the history of Algorithm
4
up to the point immediately before embedding the
j
th vertex of
M_{x}
. For each
x\in Y
, we create a collection of Bernoulli random variables
A_{x,1}
for
x\in Y
, which are set equal to one if either
x
survives at step
1
or (
5.7
) has failed before embedding the first element of
M_{x}
, and zero otherwise. We identify in each
M_{x}
the first (in
\tau
) element
w_{x,1}
, and sort the
A_{x,1}
according to the order induced by
\tau
on the
w_{x,1}
. In this order, the
A_{x,1}
are sequentially dependent. We define

s_{1}=\begin{cases}\tfrac{1}{2}\beta d^{D+1}4^{-D}p|Y|&\text{if}\quad c_{1}=1\\
\tfrac{1}{4}|Y|&\text{if}\quad c_{1}=0\end{cases}\,.

Now for each
A_{x,1}
, either Fact
5.10
gives us a lower bound on
\mathbb{E}[A_{x,1}|\mathscr{H}_{x,1}]
, or (
5.7
) has failed before we embed the first vertex of
M_{x}
, in which case
\mathbb{E}[A_{x,1}|\mathscr{H}_{x,1}]=1
. We conclude, by definition of
s_{1}
, that

\sum_{x\in Y}\mathbb{E}[A_{x,1}|\mathscr{H}_{x,1}]\geq 2s_{1}\,.

By Lemma
2.2
, with
\delta=1/4
, with probability at least
1-e^{-s_{1}/24}
we have
\sum_{x\in Y}A_{x,1}\geq s_{1}+1
. Observe that if (
5.7
) does not fail for any
y
or
t
(which is a.a.s. the case by Fact
5.9
) then
\mathcal{A}_{1}
holding says that at least
s_{1}+1
vertices survive at step
1
.

There remains a small technical difficulty in continuing. We would like to count vertices surviving at step
2
, and use Lemma
2.2
and a lower bound on the sum of conditional expectations given by the product of
s_{1}
and the probability bound from Fact
5.10
to show that the number of vertices surviving at step
2
is likely to be large. But it is possible that less than
s_{1}
vertices survive at step
1
, so that this bound on the sum of conditional expectations does not hold almost surely (it only holds a.a.s.).

To get around this problem, we use the following trick. Given
x
, immediately after embedding the first vertex of
M_{x}
in Algorithm
4
, let
g
be the number of vertices
x^{\prime}
in
Y
such that
A_{x,1}
is certainly equal to one, i.e. such that the first vertex of
M_{x^{\prime}}
was embedded to a vertex which is neither poor nor bad, so
x^{\prime}
survives at step
1
. Let
h
be the number of vertices
x^{\prime}
in
Y
such that the first vertex of
M_{x^{\prime}}
has not yet been embedded. If
g+h\leq s_{1}
, we say
x
is
dangerous at step
1
if the following holds. In other words, we say
x
is dangerous at step
1
if after embedding the first vertex of
M_{x}
we already know that
\mathcal{A}_{1}
does not occur.

We now define Bernoulli random variables
A_{x,2}
for
x\in Y
, set equal to one if either
x
survives at step
2
, or
x
is dangerous at step
1
, or (
5.7
) has failed for some
y
prior to embedding the second vertex of
M_{x}
, and zero otherwise. The point of this definition is that it gives us an
a priori
lower bound on the sum of conditional expectations of the
A_{x,2}
, as we require to apply Lemma
2.2
, but nevertheless with very high probability the sum of the
A_{x,2}
does simply count the number of vertices in
Y
which survive at step
2
, because with very high probability (
5.7
) does not fail and
\mathcal{A}_{1}
does occur, so no vertex is dangerous at step
1
. Again, this collection of random variables has a natural order given by the order
\tau
on the second vertices of the
M_{x}
. Observe that we know whether
x
is dangerous at step
1
before we embed the second vertex of
M_{x}
. Thus in this order the random variables are sequentially dependent. This justifies that we can apply Lemma
2.2
to estimate the sum of the
A_{x,2}
.

We now say what exactly this application of Lemma
2.2
gives us, and explain how to analyse steps
3,\dots,|\mathbf{c}|
in the same way. We need to give integers
s_{j}
for
2\leq j\leq|\mathbf{c}|
, which are our desired lower bounds on the number of vertices in
Y
surviving at step
j
, to define the events
\mathcal{A}_{j}
and the concept of
dangerous at step
j
for
j\geq 2
, and define the random variables
A_{x,j}
for
3\leq j\leq\mathbf{|c|}
.

For each
2\leq j\leq|\mathbf{c}|
, we set

s_{j}=\begin{cases}\tfrac{1}{2}\beta d^{D+1}4^{-D}ps_{j-1}&\text{if}\quad c_{j}=1\\
\tfrac{1}{4}s_{j-1}&\text{if}\quad c_{j}=0\end{cases}\,.

We let the event
\sum_{x\in Y}A_{x,j}\geq s_{j}+1
be
\mathcal{A}_{j}
. We say
x\in Y
is dangerous at step
j
if immediately after embedding the
j
th vertex of
M_{x}
, the number of
x^{\prime}\in Y
such that
A_{x^{\prime},j}
is certainly equal to one, plus the number of
x^{\prime}\in Y
such that the
j
th vertex of
M_{x^{\prime}}
has not yet been embedded, is at most
s_{j}
. Again, this means that after embedding the
j
th vertex of
M_{x}
, we already know
\mathcal{A}_{j}
does not occur. Finally, for each
j\geq 3
we define the Bernoulli random variables
A_{x,j}
for
x\in Y
, set equal to one if
x
survives at step
j
, or is dangerous at step
j-1
, or (
5.7
) has failed before embedding the
j
th vertex of
M_{x}
, and zero otherwise.

Before we continue, we observe that

s_{1}\geq s_{2}\geq\dots\geq s_{|\mathbf{c}|}\geq\big(\tfrac{1}{4}\big)^{\Delta^{2}+\Delta^{3}}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\beta d^{D+1}4^{-D}p\big)^{b}|Y|\,,

(5.10)

where
b
is the degree of the buffer
X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
, i.e.
\deg_{H}(x)=b
for each
x\in X^{\mathrm{buf}}_{i}
; this number exists by
(H 0)
. The reason is simply that there are at most
\Delta^{2}+\Delta^{3}
vertices of
H
at distance two or three in
H
from any given
x\in Y
, and
b
neighbours of
x
. In particular, by choice of
p
, by our lower bound on
|Y|
and since
n
is sufficiently large, we have
3s_{j}/2>s_{j}+1
for each
1\leq j\leq|\mathbf{c}|
.

We now show that with very high probability
\mathcal{A}_{j}
occurs for each
1\leq j\leq|\mathbf{c}|
. Recall that by Fact
5.10
, we have the lower bound
\sum_{x\in Y}\mathbb{E}[A_{x,1}|\mathscr{H}_{x,1}]\geq 2s_{1}
. By Lemma
2.2
with
\delta=1/4
, and the observation
3s_{1}/2>s_{1}+1
, the probability that
\mathcal{A}_{1}
fails is thus at most
e^{-s_{1}/24}
.

Now for
j\geq 2
, for each
x\in Y
, either
x
survived at step
j-1
and (
5.7
) has not failed before embedding the
j
th vertex of
M_{x}
(in which case the expectation of
A_{x,j}
conditioned on
\mathscr{H}_{x,j}
is at least the quantity given in Fact
5.10
), or
x
was dangerous at step
j-1
, or (
5.7
) failed before embedding the
j
th vertex of
M_{x}
(in which case we have
\mathbb{E}[A_{x,h}|\mathscr{H}_{x,j}]=1
), or none of these occur (in which case we have
\mathbb{E}[A_{x,h}|\mathscr{H}_{x,j}]\geq 0
). Furthermore, by definition of the
A_{x,j}
and of the event
\mathcal{A}_{j-1}
, we know (a priori, before beginning the embedding) that one of the first three cases occurs for at least
s_{j-1}
of the vertices
Y
, so that we have
\sum_{x\in Y}\mathbb{E}[A_{x,j}|\mathscr{H}_{x,j}]\geq 2s_{j}
by definition of
s_{j}
. Applying Lemma
2.2
, with
\delta=1/4
, and since
3s_{j}/2>s_{j}+1
, we conclude that the probability that
\mathcal{A}_{j}
fails is at most
e^{-s_{j}/24}
.

By definition, if (
5.7
) never fails and each event
\mathcal{A}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{A}_{|\mathbf{c}|}
occurs, then no vertex is dangerous at any step. Thus the number of vertices of
Y
surviving at step
|\mathbf{c}|
is at least
s_{|\mathbf{c}|}
. By definition, if
x
survives at step
|\mathbf{c}|
then
N_{H}(x)
is embedded to
N_{G}(v)
, so that
v
is a candidate for
x
. Since
s_{|\mathbf{c}|}
is by (
5.10
), (
5.9
) and
(H 0)
at least

\big(\tfrac{1}{4}\big)^{\Delta^{2}+\Delta^{3}}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\beta d^{D+1}4^{-D}p\big)^{b}\cdot\tfrac{1}{6}\Delta^{-3}2^{-3\Delta^{3}}\cdot 4\mu|X_{i}|

we conclude that (
5.5
) holds for
v
, as desired.
The probability that any given one of the events
\mathcal{A}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{A}_{|\mathbf{c}|}
fails is at most
e^{-s_{|\mathbf{c}|}/24}<n^{-D-1}
by (
5.10
), (
5.9
),
(H 0)
, since
|X_{i}|\geq n/\kappa r_{1}
and by choice of
p
. Taking the union bound over the at most
3\Delta^{3}
events
\mathcal{A}_{j}
, and the at most
n
choices of
v
, and since a.a.s. (
5.7
) never fails by Fact
5.9
, we conclude that a.a.s. (
5.5
) holds for all
v\in V_{i}
for each
i\in[r]
, completing the proof of Claim
5.8
.

∎

The good event of Claim
5.8
holding gives
(DRGA 0)
. Since a.a.s. the good events of each of the above claims and lemmas hold, this completes the proof of Lemma
5.2
.
∎

Chapter 6
Proofs of applications

In this chapter we prove the various theorems listed in Section
1.2
. We indicate when stronger results are proved (subsequently) elsewhere. To keep this section brief we often only sketch proofs.

6.1.
Universal graphs

We begin by showing universality of
G(n,p)
for (almost) spanning graphs with bounded degree and bounded degeneracy.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

We first show
( )
‣
1.1
.
Observe that any
d
-degenerate
n
-vertex graph has at most
dn
edges
and so contains at least
\tfrac{n}{2d+1}
vertices of degree at most

2d
. We will apply Lemma
1.23
with input
\Delta
, with

\Delta_{R^{\prime}}=8\Delta
,
D=2d+1
,
\alpha=\tfrac{\gamma}{10d}
,
\zeta=1
,
density
\tfrac{1}{2}
, and
\kappa=2
. Lemma
1.23
returns

\varepsilon>0
, and for input
r_{1}=8\Delta
also
C
. Choose
\varepsilon^{*}\ll\varepsilon

and suppose
C
is also large enough for
Lemma
2.17
( )
‣
2.17
with input
\varepsilon^{*}
,

8\Delta
and
2
. Let
p\geq C\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/(2d+1)}
. Fix an equipartition
V_{1},\ldots,V_{8\Delta}

of
[n]
. Now
G(n,p)
a.a.s. satisfies the good event of
Lemma
1.23
. By Lemma
2.17
( )
‣
2.17

it a.a.s. has property
\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon^{*},8\Delta,D)
. Finally, using
Theorem
2.1
and the union bound it is easy to check that
a.a.s. for each
i
and
v\in V_{i}
, the vertex
v
has

(1\pm\varepsilon)p|V_{j}|
neighbours in
V_{j}
for each
j\neq i
. Fix a graph

\Gamma
with each of these properties. Property

\mathrm{NS}(\varepsilon^{*},8\Delta,D)
implies that any pair of disjoint subsets of

V(\Gamma)
, each of size at least
\tfrac{pn}{16\Delta}
, is

(\varepsilon,\tfrac{1}{2},p)
-regular, so letting
R=R^{\prime}=K_{8\Delta}
we have an

(\varepsilon,\tfrac{1}{2},p)
-regular
R
-partition which has super-regularity
and one- and two-sided inheritance on
R^{\prime}
.

Given
H
on
n
vertices with degeneracy
d
and maximum degree

\Delta
, let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{8\Delta}
be an equipartition of
V(H)

into independent sets, with each
X_{i}
containing at least

\tfrac{1}{8\Delta(2d+1)}|X_{i}|
vertices of degree at most
2d
. This
equipartition exists by Lemma
2.4
. We designate the
vertices of degree at most
2d
as potential buffer vertices. We do not
image restrict any vertices. Then (perhaps after reordering) the
X_{i}

form an
R
-partition of
V(H)
which is size-compatible with the

V_{i}
. We let
\tau
be a degeneracy order on
V(H)
. We move all
buffer vertices to the end of the ordering
\tau
. Observe that this
slightly changed ordering satisfies
conditions
(ORD 0’)
–
(ORD 0’)
and thus is

(D,p,\varepsilon^{*}n/r_{1})
-bounded. Then the conditions of
Lemma
1.23
are satisfied, so
H\subseteq\Gamma
as desired.

To obtain
( )
‣
1.1
, we work identically except that we add to each part of
V(H)
isolated vertices to obtain a size-compatible
R
-partition, and we designate these isolated vertices as potential buffer vertices instead of the low-degree vertices in
H
. Then Lemma
1.23
, applied with
D=2d
,
p=C(\frac{\log n}{n})^{1/D}
and all other parameters staying as before, gives the desired conclusion.
∎

It is quite easy to use Lemma
1.25
to show that sufficiently pseudorandom graphs are universal for bounded-degree graphs.

Sketch proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Given
G
, we take a random equipartition of
V(G)
into
\Delta+1
clusters
V_{1},\ldots,V_{\Delta+1}
. For each
v\in V(G)
and
i
, the quantity
\deg(v;V_{i})
is hypergeometrically distributed with mean at least
\tfrac{1}{4}p|V_{i}|
, so by Theorem
2.1
and the union bound we see that with positive probability we have
\deg(v;V_{i})\geq\tfrac{1}{8}p|V_{i}|
for each
v
and
i
. We fix such a partition, and let
R=R^{\prime}=K_{\Delta+1}
. Using
the Hajnal-Szemerédi theorem (Theorem
2.3
) we can for any
n
-vertex graph
H
with
\Delta(H)\leq\Delta
find a size-compatible
R
-partition of
V(H)
, and applying Lemma
1.25
with all vertices designated as potential buffer vertices, with
d=\tfrac{1}{16}
and with no vertices image restricted, we see that
H\subseteq G
as desired.
∎

6.2.
Partition universality

In this section we prove Theorems
1.3
,
1.4
and
1.5
. We need Ramsey’s Theorem and Turán’s Theorem.
The Ramsey number
r_{k}(K_{t})
is the smallest integer
n
such that no matter how one colours the edges of
K_{n}
with
k
colours, there is a monochromatic copy of
K_{t}
. Ramsey
[
67
]
proved these numbers exist, while the following quantitative statement is due to Erdős and Szekeres
[
35
]
.

Theorem 6.1

(Erdős and Szekeres
[
35
]
)
.

For any
k
and
t
we have
r_{k}(K_{t})\leq k^{kt}
.

Turán, generalising a result of Mantel
[
65
]
, proved the following.

Theorem 6.2

(Turán
[
76
]
)
.

For any
r\geq 3
, any
n
-vertex
K_{r}
-free graph has at most
\frac{(r-2)n^{2}}{2(r-1)}+o(n^{2})
edges.

Finally we state the version of the sparse regularity lemma for many colours that we are going to apply.
We also say that a graph
G
with density
p
is
(\eta,D)
-upper-uniform
if for all disjoint sets
U
and
W
of cardinality at least

\eta v(G)
we have
e_{G}(U,W)\leq Dp|U||W|
.

Lemma 6.3

(Sparse regularity lemma, coloured version
[
48
]
)
.

For any real
D,\varepsilon>0
, integers
k
and
t_{0}
, there exist
\eta>0
and
T
such that any graph
G
of edge density
p
and on at least
t
vertices, which is
(\eta,D)
-upper-uniform and whose edges are coloured with
k
colours admits a partition of
V(G)
into

V_{1}
, …,
V_{t}
with the following properties.

(

)

t_{0}\leq t\leq T
.

(

)

\big||V_{i}|-|V_{j}|\big|\leq 1
for all
i
,
j
.

(

)

all but at most
\varepsilon t^{2}
pairs
(V_{i},V_{j})
are
(\varepsilon,d,p)
-fully-regular in each of the
k
colours for some possibly different
d
.

The assumption on
(\eta,D)
-upper uniformity is easily seen to be satisfied for any moderate
\eta
and
D>1
by our random graph

G(n,p)
and by the bijumbled graphs that we will be considering. The partition asserted by
Lemma
6.3
is called
\varepsilon
-regular. We define a coloured multigraph
R(\delta)
on
[t]
(associating each
i\in[t]
with the class
V_{i}
) as follows. We put an edge
ij
into
E({R})
in colour
c
if the pair
(V_{i},V_{j})
is
(\varepsilon,\delta,p)
-regular in colour
c
in
G
.

We now prove that
G(n,p)
is a.a.s.
r
-partition universal for
\mathcal{H}(n,d,\Delta)
provided
p\geq C\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/(2d)}
, which improves on the result of Kohayakawa, Rödl, Schacht and Szemerédi
[
52
]
for graphs with
d\leq\Delta/2
. A proof of the result of
[
52
]
can be obtained along very similar lines.

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

We apply Lemma
1.23
with input
\Delta
, with
\Delta_{R^{\prime}}=0
,
D=2d
,
\alpha=\tfrac{1}{2}
,
\zeta=1
, density
\tfrac{1}{2d}
, and
\kappa=1
. Lemma
1.23
returns
\varepsilon>0
, which we suppose is small enough for the application of Turán’s Theorem below. We let
r_{1}
be large enough for the
k
-coloured sparse regularity lemma with input
\varepsilon
and also for the applications of Turán’s and Ramsey’s Theorem below, and obtain
C\geq r_{1}
from Lemma
1.23
. Given
p
, we generate
\Gamma=G(Cn,p)
, suppose it satisfies the good event of Lemma
1.23
and take any
k
-colouring of its edges. We apply the sparse regularity lemma for coloured graphs, Lemma
6.3
, to this coloured graph. We obtain a coloured reduced graph with at most an
\varepsilon
-fraction of pairs not forming edges, in which we find a
4^{\Delta+1}
-vertex clique by Turán’s Theorem, and in that a monochromatic
(\Delta+1)
-vertex clique by Ramsey’s Theorem. Thus we have
\Delta+1
equal-sized clusters
V_{1},\ldots,V_{\Delta+1}
, each of size at least
n
, such that there is a colour
c
in which each pair of clusters is
(\varepsilon,\tfrac{1}{2r},p)
-regular. Let
G
be the subgraph of
\Gamma[V_{1},\dots,V_{\Delta+1}]
containing all edges of colour
c
, let
R=K_{\Delta+1}
and
R^{\prime}
be the empty graph.

Given
H\in\mathcal{H}(n,\Delta)
, by Theorem
2.3
we can find an equipartition of
V(H)
into
\Delta+1
independent sets, each of size at most
\lceil n/(\Delta+1)\rceil<n/2
. We ‘pad’ each set by adding at least
n/2
isolated vertices to obtain a size-compatible
R
-partition
X_{1},\ldots,X_{\Delta+1}
, and designate the isolated vertices as potential buffer vertices which come last in the degeneracy order
\tau
of
H
. Then the result follows by Lemma
1.23
, with
\tau
being a
(D,p,\varepsilon n/r)
-bounded order on
V(H)
.
∎

Sketch proof of Theorem
1.4
.

The same approach as for Theorem
1.3
works, replacing Lemma
1.23
with Lemma
1.25
. It is easy to check that the upper-uniformity condition of Lemma
6.3
is satisfied for
\Gamma
with the bijumbledness condition of the theorem.
∎

Sketch proof of Theorem
1.5
.

We follow the same approach as in the proof of Theorem
1.3
, with the exceptions that we use
p=C\big(\tfrac{\log n}{n}\big)^{1/\Delta}
, that we use Lemma
1.21
instead of Lemma
1.23
, and that after generating
\Gamma=G(n,p)
which satisfies the good event of Lemma
1.21
, we form
G
by deleting a minimum number of edges to remove all copies of
K_{2\Delta,2\Delta}
